# Build State

(Last updated: 2026-05-07 — Brief 68 completed; `/admin/damage` claims list gains a server-computed age pill next to the submitted_at date — `listClaims` SELECT projection extended with `CAST((julianday('now') - julianday(submitted_at)) AS INTEGER) AS age_days`; new `<AgePill>` component renders four-tier color escalation (neutral / amber / orange / red at 0-3 / 4-7 / 8-14 / 15+ days) on Open claims with a static neutral pill on Closed; restores the legacy "this one's been sitting two weeks" visual scan that `legacy/damagemanager.js:2901 renderAgeBadge` provided. Brief 67 completed; Reporting page (`/admin/damage/reporting`) gains four operator-requested enhancements landed in one shot. (1) **Cost column on global Approved-by-damage-type table.** `byDamageTypeApprovedSql` (in `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts`'s reporting handler) rewritten as a JOIN-based variant: `LEFT JOIN claim_photos ON claim_id AND cp.deleted_at IS NULL`, `COUNT(DISTINCT c.claim_id) AS n` so the photo join doesn't multiply claim counts, `COALESCE(SUM(CASE WHEN cp.photo_type IN ('Quote','Receipt') AND cp.amount IS NOT NULL THEN cp.amount END), 0) AS cost`. Apps/web `DamageTypeTable` helper gains optional `showCost` prop — Open and Denied panels stay 2-column (count only); Approved panel renders count + cost. (2) **Per-location drill-down via inline expandable rows.** New `byLocationDrilldownSql` in damage-worker's batch returns one row per (location_code, outcome_bucket, damage_type) using a 5-bucket CASE: `open` (lifecycle_state='Open') / `denied` (claim_status='Closed — Denied') / `approved` (claim_status LIKE 'Approved —%') / `closed_approved` (claim_status IN ('Closed — Paid','Closed — Approved/No Response')) / `closed_other`. Five-bucket split chosen over the brief's 4-bucket fallback because it gives the renderer exact control over the operator-facing aggregations: "Closed" view = `denied + closed_approved + closed_other`, "Approved" view = `approved + closed_approved` (matches the per-location Approved column's claim_status filter). New apps/web client component `apps/web/app/admin/damage/reporting/_components/ByLocationTableClient.tsx` (`"use client"`; `useState<Set<string>>` keyed by location_code; multiple rows can be expanded simultaneously; chevron `▶/▼` indicator; `aria-expanded` on the toggleable row; `<td colSpan={8}>` for the expanded panel — 8 because of new leading chevron column + 7 data columns). Drill-down panel renders four `DamageTypeMiniTable` blocks (Open / Closed / Approved-with-cost / Denied) using a Map-based aggregator that buckets the location's drill-down rows by damage_type within the requested bucket subset. Mirrors Brief 53's audit-log expansion pattern (session-only state, no URL persistence). (3) **Avg Days Open column on By Location table** between Denied and Cost. New `byLocationAvgDaysOpenSql` uses SQLite's `AVG(julianday('now') - julianday(submitted_at))` filtered to `lifecycle_state = 'Open' AND deleted_at IS NULL`; locations with zero open claims simply produce no row, surfaced as `—` in the renderer. The column header is "Avg Days Open"; values render as `${Math.round(v)}d` (e.g., "12d"). (4) **Footer rewording.** Old text claimed cost may be double-counted as a v2 limitation; new text reframes per operator's 2026-05-07 decision: "Cost = approved quote amounts + receipt amounts on Approved-family claims. Both are real spend — receipts are money paid out, approved quotes are money committed — so summing them captures total exposure." Reporting handler docblock gains a Brief 67 note explaining the new fields and the cost-framing change. **Response shape extensions:** `by_location[].avg_days_open: number | null`, `by_damage_type_approved[].cost: number`, new top-level `by_location_drilldown: Array<{location_code, location_pretty, outcome_bucket, damage_type, n, cost}>`. New TypeScript interfaces: `ReportingByDamageTypeApprovedRow`, `ReportingByLocationDrilldownRow`, plus `ReportingDrilldownBucket` union. **D1 batch grew from 11 to 13 statements** (added `byLocationAvgDaysOpenSql` + `byLocationDrilldownSql`); same `baseBindings` (`[from, to, ...codes]`); dc_role scoping unchanged. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` passes (13/13 packages); `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeds (route `/admin/damage/reporting` now 1.59 kB First Load JS); `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run --outdir=.tmp-build` bundles cleanly at 1719.99 KiB raw / 389.35 KiB gzipped (well under the 3 MiB compressed free-tier ceiling); `.tmp-build` cleaned up. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** chose 5-bucket SQL over 4-bucket — extra CASE branch cost is negligible vs the rendering precision gain (Approved drill-down can include Closed — Paid + Closed — Approved/No Response claims so it matches the per-location Approved column's count, which the simpler 4-bucket would have left ambiguous). **Latent issue noted:** the "Closed" drill-down panel intentionally overlaps with both Approved and Denied panels (closed_approved appears in both Approved and Closed; denied appears in both Denied and Closed) — operator explicitly requested this redundancy in the brief ("the superset that overlaps Approved + Denied — yes, redundant by design"); flag for future refinement if v2 wants stricter mutual-exclusion. **Operator follow-up:** navigate `/admin/damage/reporting`, confirm new columns + drill-down render correctly; spot-check that the global Approved-by-damage-type cost column matches the sum of per-location Approved drill-down costs (should match exactly — both queries compute `SUM(amount) WHERE photo_type IN ('Quote','Receipt')` over the same Approved-family claim set, so any discrepancy would indicate a renderer-side aggregation bug; the closed_approved bucket appearing in both the Approved view AND the Closed view doesn't double-count costs because the Closed view doesn't render cost). Brief 66 (RM revert paths) and earlier history preserved below for context. Brief 66: RM can now revert from `Approved — Pending Quotes` (to GM Review / RM Review / Closed — Denied) and from `Pending RM Quote Approval` (to GM Review / RM Review). Operator's use case: GM erroneously approves a claim, RM catches it during quote-gathering, RM bounces or denies without admin escalation. Three existing transitions in `apps/damage-worker/src/transitions.ts` widened from `role: "admin"` → `role: "rm"` (`Approved — Pending Quotes → Pending GM Review`, `Approved — Pending Quotes → Pending RM Review`, `Pending RM Quote Approval → Pending GM Review`); two new RM-allowed transitions added (`Approved — Pending Quotes → Closed — Denied` and `Pending RM Quote Approval → Pending RM Review`), positioned in the per-status forward blocks alongside the other transitions for those statuses. All five send-back paths carry `requiresNote: true` + `clearApprovalDetails: true` so Brief 20's invariant holds — the worker NULLs `approved_amount` / `approved_quote_id` / `parts_ordered` / `vendor_name` and resets gm/rm/ceo audit stamps as part of the revert UPDATE; the detail page's Approval Details box won't render stale data on the reverted claim. apps/web mirror at `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_lib/transitions.ts` updated in lockstep: same five edits, the three widened entries lose the `(admin)` suffix on their labels (`Send back to GM Review`, `Send back to RM Review`), the two new entries land with appropriate labels (`Close — Denied`, `Send back to RM Review`). The `Pending RM Quote Approval → Approved — Pending Quotes` revert (a forward step past where the RM might want to revert from) intentionally stays admin-only. Post-approval / finance-touched reverts stay admin-only as before — `Approved — In House — Parts Ordered → Pending GM Review`, `Approved — Check Request Submitted ↔ Pending RM Quote Approval`, `Approved — Submitted for Payment ↔ Approved — Check Request Submitted` / `Pending RM Quote Approval`, `Approved — Check Issued → Approved — Check Request Submitted`, and every `Closed —` reopen. CLAUDE.md's "Damage detail" entry extended with a Brief 66 note that admin-only revert paths now begin at `Approved — In House — Parts Ordered`. No schema change, no new endpoints, no new env vars. Earlier brief completion: Brief 64 — one-line bug fix (with cascading cleanup) for Brief 61's `setDcRole` helper. Brief 61 modeled the dc_role write after `setRole`'s `user_permissions` write and assumed `damage_claim_user_roles` carried an `email` column. It does not — the table is `(user_id, dc_role)`; email lives on `auth.users` and is joined by the `auth_unified` view at read time. Every Set DC Role submission was 500ing with Postgres 42703 (`column damage_claim_user_roles.email does not exist`; CF requestId `5ZAAKPF23IPE2KMG`). **Fix scope (extended beyond brief's literal Phase 1.1):** the brief asked to drop `email` from the upsert payload, but the helper also had two `.select("user_id,email,dc_role")` calls (the before-snapshot read at users.ts:366 and the upsert RETURNING at users.ts:421) that would 42703 against the same non-existent column. Both selects had to drop `email` for the function to actually succeed (the operator follow-up step in the brief — re-test Set DC Role on the same user, expect 200 — is otherwise unreachable). Also dropped `email` from the local `DamageRoleRow` interface and updated the helper's docblock with a Brief 64 schema note. Sysadmin-worker `handleSetDcRole` docblock + the inline "Resolve email — damage_claim_user_roles.email is NOT NULL" comment were updated to drop the bogus claim (the email argument is still resolved + passed for the audit-log path; the helper just doesn't write it). Dashboard tile relabels to "Signup Admin" (eyebrow "Signup admin", description rewritten to "Manage MaxPass pricing and review recent signups across your locations.", `href: "/admin/pricing"` unchanged so the tab nav handles cross-view flips); URL `/admin/pricing/*` is intentionally unchanged for operator bookmark stability. Middleware `ADMIN_KNOWN_SUBPATHS` allow-list extended with `signups` so `/admin/signups/{loc}` doesn't 308-redirect through Brief 2's legacy `/admin/{slug}` → `/admin/pricing/{slug}` rule. PII handling: phone + email render as plain text in the table (no copy-to-clipboard, no CSV export — both v1 deferrals per the brief's out-of-scope list). **Files created:** 4 — `apps/signup-worker/src/handlers/admin-signups.ts` (new sibling handler; ~120 lines including header), `apps/web/app/admin/_components/SignupAdminTabs.tsx` (~60 lines), `apps/web/app/admin/signups/page.tsx` (~95 lines, mirrors pricing landing), `apps/web/app/admin/signups/[location]/page.tsx` (~280 lines including format helpers). **Files modified:** 7 — `apps/signup-worker/src/handlers/admin-pricing.ts` (`adminGate` + `userCanAccessLocation` + `AdminGate` promoted to exports; docblock updated to note Brief 56 reuse), `apps/signup-worker/src/index.ts` (new import, route table comment updated, dispatcher branch added for `GET .../{loc}/signups`), `apps/web/app/admin/pricing/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` (added `SignupDays`/`SignupRow`/`SignupsResponse` types + `getSignupsForLocation` helper; docblock-style note explaining the file now owns both pricing and signups domain wrappers), `apps/web/app/admin/pricing/page.tsx` (added `<SignupAdminTabs>` to all three render branches; H1s flipped to "Signup Admin · Pricing"; access-denied copy flipped to "Signup Admin"), `apps/web/app/admin/pricing/[location]/page.tsx` (added `<SignupAdminTabs>` to both branches; access-denied H1 flipped to "Signup Admin · Pricing"), `apps/web/app/admin/dashboard/page.tsx` (Pricing tile eyebrow/title/description rewrite), `apps/web/middleware.ts` (`signups` added to `ADMIN_KNOWN_SUBPATHS`). Plus standard updates to `BRIEFS/INDEX.md`, `BRIEFS/QUEUE.md`, `BUILD_STATE.md`, and the brief itself; CLAUDE.md updates: glossary gains a `Signup admin` entry and the "Working with apps/web" Real-pages bullet enumerates `/admin/signups` + `/admin/signups/[location]`. **Out of scope (per brief):** cross-location aggregate views, CSV export, sorting/filtering inside the table, click-through detail page, compare-across-day-windows, renaming the underlying `/admin/pricing/*` URL, customer-facing form/picker/thanks page changes, cross-worker pricing cache invalidation (still Brief 28). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Sibling new file `admin-signups.ts`** rather than appending to `admin-pricing.ts` — the brief left it executor's call; the file naming better reflects the separate concern (pricing = operational write, signups = observational read), and exporting `adminGate`/`userCanAccessLocation` from `admin-pricing.ts` keeps the auth gate verbatim-shared without duplication. (2) **Domain types + helper added to existing `apps/web/app/admin/pricing/_lib/worker-fetch.ts`** rather than a new `apps/web/app/admin/_lib/signup-worker-fetch.ts` — the existing file's `workerGetJson` is already a fully generic SIGNUP_WORKER transport; pulling it out to a sibling location would have meant either duplicating the binding/URL-fallback logic or refactoring pricing's import path. The brief left it executor's call and explicitly allowed adding a sibling helper to the existing file. The signups page imports `getSignupsForLocation` from `../../pricing/_lib/worker-fetch` — slightly awkward path but minimal-churn vs. moving the transport. (3) **Validate `params.location` with the worker's `LOCATION_CODE_RE` (`/^[a-z0-9_]+$/`)** in the per-location signups page — the brief said "validate with the same regex as the pricing page" but the pricing page doesn't validate inline (it just calls the worker and lets the 404 fall through). Defensive validation here renders 404 chrome with the tabs visible instead of round-tripping to the worker for an obviously bad slug. The `LOCATION_CODE_RE` constant is duplicated rather than imported from sysadmin-worker because workers can't import each other's source. (4) **Day filter rendered as URL `<Link>`s** that update `?days=N` — server-rendered, no JS, matches the rest of the admin's pattern. Active-day pill style mirrors the active-tab style (filled `bg-splash-blue` + white text, inactive outline + `text-splash-blue`). (5) **Empty-state row uses `<td colSpan={5}>` italic copy** ("No signups in the last N days.") — the brief explicitly called for keeping the table structure visible with the empty hint, not collapsing the table when there's no data. (6) **`limit_hit` footer renders only when `limit_hit === true`** — no 199/200 noise; `count` field always shows the actual rows.length so the operator can read the volume directly. (7) **`formatRelative` + `formatAbsolute` time helpers inlined in the per-location page** rather than promoted to a shared `_lib/format.ts` — they're small and only used here; promotion can wait until a second consumer needs them. The "When" cell uses `<span title={formatAbsolute(...)}>{formatRelative(...)}</span>` so hovering surfaces the absolute timestamp without cluttering the column. (8) **`<SignupAdminTabs>` placed at `apps/web/app/admin/_components/`** rather than under `signups/_components/` or `pricing/_components/` — both pricing and signups pages mount it, so the shared parent location is the right home. (9) **Pricing landing-page H1 flipped to "Signup Admin · Pricing"** following the brief's umbrella-dot-section pattern; the per-location pricing page H1 (`{data.location_pretty}`) was left as-is because it's already a sub-section title under the tab and the brief explicitly said that's fine. (10) **Cookie-name-style middleware constant** — added `signups` alphabetically near `pricing`/`sysadmin` rather than appended at the end; the existing list isn't strictly alphabetized but `signups` slots naturally between `pricing` and `sysadmin`. (11) **No PII export / audit log entry on signups read** — the brief explicitly noted PII export needs its own audit-log entry; v1 is read-only display so no `sysadmin_audit_log` write was added. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Cross-folder import path** — `apps/web/app/admin/signups/[location]/page.tsx` imports `getSignupsForLocation` from `../../pricing/_lib/worker-fetch`. If a future brief moves the generic transport to `apps/web/app/admin/_lib/signup-worker-fetch.ts`, both pricing's pages and signups' pages will need their import paths updated. The current location is a known cosmetic infelicity but isn't functionally broken. (b) **No headless smoke test possible** — operator must navigate to `/admin/signups/<accessible-location>` after the next apps/web + signup-worker redeploys and confirm: (i) tab nav renders with Signups active; (ii) table populates with last-7-days submissions; (iii) flipping the day filter re-renders; (iv) flipping to the Pricing tab preserves location context; (v) cookie-missing returns the sign-in card. (c) **Bundle delta** — `/admin/signups` + `/admin/signups/[location]` both 167 B / 105 kB First Load JS; `/admin/pricing` 167 B (was 165 B; +2 B for the new tab nav imports), `/admin/pricing/[location]` 3.66 kB (was 3.66 kB; rounding-equal; tab nav is server-only so no client-bundle cost). All other route bundles unchanged. (d) **`limit=200` cap** — operator-bound for typical traffic is ~50/loc/30d; hitting the cap means the location had a marketing event that pulled >200 signups in the chosen window. The footer hint guides the operator to flip to a shorter window; the brief out-of-scoped a "load more" pagination affordance for v1. (e) **No `phone` (raw 10-digit) in select** — the table only renders `phone_formatted`. If a future brief wants to copy the raw digits to the clipboard, it'll need to add `phone` to the worker select list. (f) **Auth gate via `checkToolAccess(session, "pricing")`** — the new signups read inherits the pricing tool grant. If operator wants signup-only access (e.g., a marketing role that should see signups but not edit pricing), a separate `signups` tool grant would need to be introduced. v1 deliberately bundles them under one umbrella per the operator's "Signup Admin" framing. (g) **Cache invalidation on the cached `/admin/api/locations` list** — if a sysadmin Add Location is run mid-session and the operator immediately navigates to `/admin/signups`, the new location may not appear for up to 5 minutes (signup-worker caches `pricing_simple_resolved` for 5 minutes; `/admin/api/locations` reads `listDistinctLocations` which doesn't go through the cache, so this is actually fine — the lag is only on customer-facing signup form pricing, not on admin location lists). Still flagged for context. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful (4.328s, 11 cache hits + fresh `@splash/web` and `@splash/signup-worker` rebuilds — the two modified packages); `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — `next build` compiled in 5.5s, all 12 routes generated cleanly (was 11 pre-Brief-56; +1 route group `/admin/signups` + `/admin/signups/[location]`); both new routes are 167 B / 105 kB First Load JS each. `pnpm --filter @splash/signup-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run --outdir=.tmp-build` succeeded — Total Upload **770.91 KiB / gzip 148.69 KiB** (no prior Brief-55-era signup-worker baseline recorded in BUILD_STATE; the new endpoint + handler add ≈80 lines of bundled code). All bindings (env.SIGNATURE_MODE="inline") resolved. `.tmp-build` cleaned up afterward. **Operator follow-up:** after splash-signup-next + splash-web redeploy (CF Workers Builds on push to `main`), navigate to `/admin/signups/oswego` (or any location you can manage) and confirm: (i) the tab nav renders with Signups active and Pricing inactive; (ii) the table populates with last-7-days submissions; (iii) flipping the day filter to 1 / 7 / 30 re-renders the table without losing the tab; (iv) clicking the Pricing tab preserves the location and lands on `/admin/pricing/oswego` (already-existing page, now with tab nav above it); (v) the dashboard tile reads "Signup Admin" with the new description; (vi) sign-out and visit `/admin/signups/oswego` directly — should land on the sign-in card with a "Sign In" button. Earlier: Brief 54 completed; `handleUpdatePackagesBulk` in `apps/sysadmin-worker/src/index.ts` now writes a real `before` snapshot on every `update_packages_bulk` audit-log entry instead of the legacy `before: null`. Brief 53 had upgraded the apps/web Activity log diff panel to a full-width side-by-side BEFORE/AFTER layout, but operator confirmed 2026-05-06 (against a recent williamsville `update_packages_bulk` row) that bulk-edit entries still rendered AFTER-only because the worker had never captured a `before` payload — the rendering layer was correct, the upstream data was missing. **Implementation:** between the per-entry validation loop and the per-row PATCH loop, a single `pkg=in.(pkg1,pkg2,…)` GET against `pricing_simple` fetches the current state of every row about to be patched, with a focused select list (`pkg`, `pkg$`, `single`, `sort` — only the four fields the bulk endpoint can touch). Result is typed as `BulkBeforeRow[]` (new interface alongside the existing `BulkUpdateEntry`/`BulkRowResult`). The audit-log `before` payload is `{ location_code, rows: BulkBeforeRow[] }` mirroring the `after` shape's `location_code`+list pattern but with `rows` (current full state) instead of `updates` (patches about to apply). The `pkg$` column-name's `$` requires PostgREST double-quoting in select lists; the URL uses the literal `&select=pkg,%22pkg%24%22,single,sort` (URL-encoded `"pkg$"`). **Fail-soft:** the GET is wrapped in try/catch; on fetch throw or non-2xx status, `beforeSnapshots` stays null and the audit-log entry falls back to the legacy `before: null` shape, preserving prior behavior as a safety net so audit-log degradation never blocks the bulk update itself. **Cost:** one extra Supabase round-trip per bulk request, regardless of `entries.length` (the `in.()` operator returns all matching rows in a single GET). Bulk requests cap at 20 entries (`BULK_MAX_UPDATES`); the GET is well under PostgREST query-length limits even at the cap. **Files modified:** 1 — `apps/sysadmin-worker/src/index.ts` (new `BulkBeforeRow` interface, new before-snapshot fetch block between validation loop and PATCH loop, audit-log call rewritten to use the snapshot, docblock above the handler updated to describe the new shape). Plus standard updates to `BRIEFS/INDEX.md`, `BRIEFS/QUEUE.md`, `BUILD_STATE.md`, and the brief itself. **Out of scope (per brief):** capturing per-row before-snapshots inside the patch loop (the pre-loop GET pattern is simpler, atomic-ish, and consistent with Brief 26's single-row pattern); diffing rows server-side and writing only changed fields (the rendering layer's `DiffBlock` handles that visually); backfilling `before` data for historical `update_packages_bulk` rows (impossible — the data is gone); touching the other handlers that legitimately write `before: null` (`create_user`, `create_location`, `grant_tool` when `was_new`, `reset_password` — inserts have no meaningful before state). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **`BulkBeforeRow` declared at module scope** alongside `BulkUpdateEntry`/`BulkRowResult` rather than inline in the handler — keeps the three bulk-endpoint shape types colocated and grep-able; matches the existing pattern. (2) **`(await beforeResp.json()) as unknown` then `Array.isArray(rows)` narrowing** rather than `as BulkBeforeRow[]` directly — an unexpected response shape (e.g., a Supabase error object instead of an array) would silently produce a malformed audit entry; `Array.isArray` gate ensures we only assign when the response really is an array. (3) **Empty `rows: []` (Supabase responded successfully but matched nothing) is preserved as a non-null snapshot** — the audit log records `before: { location_code, rows: [] }` rather than `before: null` in that case. The empty array is informationally distinct from "fetch failed" and helps an operator distinguish "no rows existed" from "we couldn't read them". (4) **No conditional ordering changes** — the per-entry validation loop still runs first, then the snapshot fetch, then the PATCH loop. A bad-validation request short-circuits before the GET, so we don't issue a Supabase round-trip for rejected requests. (5) **`encodeURIComponent` on each `pkg`** in the `in.()` list — pkg names can contain spaces/special chars in legacy data; PostgREST's `in.(...)` parser is comma-separated and treats unquoted values as identifiers, so encoding each value defensively matches the per-row PATCH URL's existing `encodeURIComponent` posture and avoids any pkg-name edge case from breaking the GET. (6) **No new endpoint, no schema change, no new env var** — the audit-log column shape is unchanged; `before` was already `jsonb` and accepting null. Brief 54 just makes it actually populated. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Two-step audit cost** — the bulk request now issues N+1 Supabase calls (one GET + N PATCHes) instead of just N PATCHes. For a 20-row request that's a +5% round-trip overhead; for the common 4-7 row request it's +14-25%. The pre-loop GET is sequential with the loop start, so total wall-time is the GET's latency plus the existing patch-loop latency. Acceptable for the audit-log fidelity win; if it ever bites, the GET could be parallelized with the first PATCH or moved to a separate `ctx.waitUntil(...)` background fire (but then the audit entry might land after the response). (b) **Race window** — between the snapshot GET and the per-row PATCH, another sysadmin operator could PATCH the same row via the single-row endpoint. The audit log's `before` would then reflect a state that didn't exist at PATCH time. Multi-operator concurrent edits on the same location_code+pkg are rare (one operator per location), but flagged for awareness. (c) **`before` payload size** — for the 20-row cap, the snapshot is roughly 20×4 fields ≈ 80-200 bytes JSON; well within Supabase `jsonb` row limits. No compaction/truncation needed. (d) **No headless smoke test** — operator must verify on the next CF Workers Builds redeploy that re-running a small bulk edit (any underscored location_code from Brief 52's fix) and clicking View on the resulting audit entry shows BEFORE and AFTER side-by-side. (e) **`pkg$` URL-encoding correctness** — the literal `&select=pkg,%22pkg%24%22,single,sort` decodes to `&select=pkg,"pkg$",single,sort` which is the PostgREST double-quoted column-name syntax. Operator follow-up's smoke test ("the GET returns 200 and rows have a `pkg$` field present, not undefined") is the empirical confirmation. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful (1.714s, 12 cache hits + fresh `@splash/sysadmin-worker` rebuild — the one package modified). `pnpm --filter @splash/sysadmin-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run --outdir=.tmp-build` succeeded — Total Upload **755.79 KiB / gzip 142.42 KiB** (Brief 52 baseline 755.11 / 142.30 → **+0.68 KiB / +0.12 KiB gzip**, accounted for by the new fetch block + interface + audit-call reshape + docblock). Bundle assembles without diagnostics. `.tmp-build` cleaned up afterward. **Operator follow-up:** after splash-sysadmin redeploys (CF Workers Builds on push to `main`), re-run a small bulk edit on any underscored location_code (e.g., `batavia_ii`, `batavia_veterans`) via Update Package's bulk-edit UI, then navigate to `/admin/sysadmin?mode=tables`, find the new `update_packages_bulk` row in the Activity log, click View → confirm the diff opens with BEFORE and AFTER side-by-side instead of AFTER-only. Earlier: Brief 53 completed; the `/admin/sysadmin` Activity log's DIFF column was rendering its expanded JSON inside the ~30-40px-wide DIFF cell via an HTML `<details>/<summary>` toggle, which squeezed large `update_packages_bulk` diffs (e.g., the operator's recent williamsville bulk edit) into a vertical column of single characters and made them effectively unreadable. Brief 53 swaps the inline `<details>` for a per-row stateful pair of `<tr>`s: the primary row carries only the toggle button (▶ View / ▼ Hide with `aria-expanded`); when expanded, a sibling `<tr><td colSpan={5}>` opens directly underneath, rendering the JSON before/after as side-by-side `<pre>` blocks (single full-width pre when only one side has data; both sides fall back to a "No diff payload recorded" hint). Each `<pre>` is capped at `max-h-96` (24rem) with `overflow-auto` so a huge diff scrolls within itself rather than pushing the rest of the table off-screen. **Implementation split:** new `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/AuditRowExpandable.tsx` is the only client island (`"use client"` + `useState<boolean>`) — it accepts the four primary cells + the diff content as React-node props, so all the server-side helpers (`relativeTime`, `TargetCell`, `DiffBlock`, `jsonOrDash`) stay on the server and `Date.now()`-derived strings don't hydrate-mismatch. `AuditLogPanel.tsx` keeps its server-component shape: the server-side `AuditRow` helper now constructs the four cell nodes + a `DiffBlock` element and hands them to `AuditRowExpandable`. **Per-row local state** (rather than a parent `Set<string>`) keeps multiple rows independently expandable without a coordinating parent — matches the brief's "multiple rows can be expanded simultaneously" requirement and is one fewer prop to thread. **Border styling:** primary row drops its bottom border when expanded (no separator between primary + expansion), expansion row carries the bottom border to separate from the next audit entry; the expansion row also gets a `bg-sudsy-blue-soft/40` tint to visually anchor the diff to its parent row. **Files created:** 1 — `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/AuditRowExpandable.tsx` (≈70 lines). **Files modified:** 1 — `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_sections/AuditLogPanel.tsx` (deleted the inline `DiffCell` `<details>` renderer; added `DiffBlock` server function with the side-by-side grid; rewrote `AuditRow` from a `<tr>` returner to an `AuditRowExpandable` invoker; added the new component import). **Out of scope (per brief):** worker-side change, API change, separate diff modal, color-coded added/removed diff highlighting (a future jsondiffpatch / react-diff-viewer integration could ship as v2 if operators ask). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Per-row `useState<boolean>`** rather than a panel-level `Set<string>` — the brief mentioned a Set as the likely existing pattern, but the existing code didn't track expansion state at all (it used native `<details>`), so there's no incumbent Set to preserve. Per-row state is simpler and meets the multi-row independence requirement. (2) **Server-side `DiffBlock` helper** rather than moving JSON formatting to the client — keeps `JSON.stringify` work off the client bundle and lets the server pre-render the entire diff DOM. The client island only flips a boolean. (3) **No-data-payload hint** ("No diff payload recorded for this entry.") — the prior `<details>` always rendered SOMETHING (an empty grid) when both before/after were null; the new layout shows the explicit hint so an operator who clicks View on a no-op row sees why nothing's there. Mostly cosmetic; doesn't change which rows have payload data. (4) **Notes still render below the diff** — the existing `notes` field (free-text rationale on some audit rows) was previously inside the `<details>` block; preserved verbatim under the diff grid so that semantic carries through. (5) **Toggle button styling** reuses the existing `text-splash-blue` link-text treatment from the prior summary, with the arrow indicator added inline (▶ View / ▼ Hide) instead of relying on the browser's default `<details>` triangle. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Bundle delta** — `/admin/sysadmin` route went from 7.06 kB → 8.38 kB / 113 kB First Load JS (was 112 kB). The +1.32 kB is the new client island + the `AuditRowExpandable` component code. Acceptable for the readability win on large diffs. (b) **No headless smoke test** — operator must verify on the next CF Workers Builds redeploy that clicking View on the recent williamsville `update_packages_bulk` row expands the diff to full width and renders BEFORE/AFTER side-by-side legibly. The brief's Phase 4 smoke-test guidance enumerates the test cases. (c) **Hydration safety** — `relativeTime(row.occurred_at)` is computed at server render time and the resulting string is passed as a React-node prop to the client island. `Date.now()` evaluates only on the server, so no hydration mismatch. The string is "frozen" at server render — but the page is server-rendered per request anyway (no static generation; it's gated on session and has dynamic searchParams), so the relative time is correct as of request time, same posture as Brief 30. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful (5.725s, 12 cache hits + fresh `@splash/web` rebuild — the one package modified). `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — `next build` compiled in 5.1s, all 11 routes generated cleanly; `/admin/sysadmin` route now 8.38 kB / 113 kB First Load JS (was 7.06 kB / 112 kB pre-Brief-53; +1.32 kB / +1 kB FLJ). Earlier: Brief 52 completed; latent defect in Brief 26's sysadmin-worker pricing-simple search sanitization patched. `handleSearchPricingSimple` (Brief 26) and `handleSearchPricingSimpleLocations` (Brief 39) both stripped `_` from user-supplied `q` parameters as part of a broader strip set (`/[%_,()*]/g`) intended to neutralize PostgREST `or()` separators and ILIKE wildcards. The `_` strip was over-defensive — since the query is wrapped in `%...%`, treating an internal `_` as a single-char wildcard is behaviorally indistinguishable from matching it literally for the operator's substring-search intent. Stripping it corrupted any query containing a literal underscore (e.g., `batavia_ii` → `bataviaii` → ILIKE pattern `%bataviaii%` matched zero rows). Defect was dormant until Brief 36's bulk-edit UI (`UpdatePackageCard`) started passing the full canonical `location_code` (which usually contains underscores: `batavia_ii`, `batavia_veterans`, etc.) into the search endpoint as part of its auto-fetch step — operator confirmed 2026-05-06 that selecting Batavia II in the picker correctly identified the location_code, but the follow-on auto-fetch returned 0 rows and the UI surfaced "No packages found at this location" even though all 4 packages existed. Picker-only typing (`batavia`, `122`, `binghamton`) never tripped the bug because operators don't type `_` mid-search. Fix: dropped `_` from the strip regex in both handlers (now `/[%,()*]/g`); pattern is now identical between them. Inline docblocks updated to spell out the new semantics and cross-reference Brief 52 so a future reader doesn't "restore" the underscore strip. **Files modified:** `apps/sysadmin-worker/src/index.ts` (two regex literals + two surrounding docblocks; line 908 and line 969); `BRIEFS/INDEX.md` (Brief 52 row appended); `BUILD_STATE.md` (this entry + Findings log entry); `BRIEFS/brief-052-sysadmin-search-underscore-sanitization.md` (Status set to Completed (2026-05-06) + Outcome filled in). **Out of scope (intentionally not patched):** `handleSearchLocations` at line 1550 (Brief 27 locations editor — same strip-set; not in the brief's scope) and the audit-log `actor_email` filter at line 1893 (Brief 30 — same strip-set; same out-of-scope rule). The brief explicitly notes that other strip-set sites need their own briefs to verify whether `_` stripping is actively breaking anything; right now only the two pricing-simple search endpoints are confirmed-affected. **Decision made on operator's behalf:** the brief's Definition of Done called for `pnpm --filter @splash/sysadmin-worker build`, but the worker's `package.json` exposes only `dev`/`deploy`/`typecheck`/`lint`/`clean` — no `build` script (workers don't compile; wrangler bundles at deploy time). Substituted `wrangler deploy --dry-run --outdir=.tmp-build` as the build-equivalent validation: same bundling pipeline as `wrangler deploy` but without uploading. Cleaned up the temp output afterward. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful (1.56s, 12 cache hits + fresh `@splash/sysadmin-worker` rebuild — the one package modified). `wrangler deploy --dry-run` succeeded — Total Upload 755.11 KiB / gzip 142.30 KiB; bundle assembles without diagnostics. Confirmed regex pattern is identical in both handlers post-change (`grep` returned both as `/[%,()*]/g`). **Operator follow-up:** after splash-sysadmin redeploys (CF Workers Builds on push), retry "Update Package" → select Batavia II (or any underscored location_code) and confirm the package list populates. The auto-fetch step inside `UpdatePackageCard` will now successfully match `%batavia_ii%` against `pricing_simple.location_code`. Earlier: Brief 51 completed; `PRE_DEPLOY_WEB.md` runbook for apps/web landed at the repo root, alongside the five existing per-deployable `PRE_DEPLOY_*.md` docs (DAMAGE / DASHBOARD / PERFORMANCE / SIGNUP / SYSADMIN). Pure documentation brief — no code change, no `pnpm typecheck` / build step required (Phase 3.1 of the brief explicitly out-of-scoped them). The doc consolidates every apps/web-specific deploy-time consideration accumulated across Briefs 1–50 into ten sections matching the per-worker doc structure: (1) Overview — apps/web hosts admin pages only; customer-facing routes live in their owning workers per Brief 50 + Brief 23 + CLAUDE.md constraint #1. (2) Deploy mechanism — CF Workers Builds wired to `joshuacopp/splash-info`; pushes to `main` trigger automatic deploys; watch paths `apps/web/**` + `packages/**`; no manual `wrangler deploy` step. (3) Build-time configuration — `NEXT_SERVER_ACTIONS_ENCRYPTION_KEY` (Brief 31) is THE critical build-time env var on splash-web → Settings → Build → Environment variables (NOT `wrangler secret put`); rotation invalidates every action ID on every open tab; defense-in-depth is `apps/web/app/admin/error.tsx`'s `UnrecognizedActionError`-aware boundary. `NEXT_PUBLIC_*_WORKER_URL` are dev-only and must be unset in production. (4) Runtime configuration (service bindings) — five `[[services]]` entries (`DASHBOARD_WORKER`, `SIGNUP_WORKER`, `PERFORMANCE_WORKER`, `SYSADMIN_WORKER`, `DAMAGE_WORKER`); Brief 17's CF same-zone Worker-to-Worker 522 workaround; `_lib/worker-fetch.ts` binding-first / URL-fallback pattern; sister-worker dependency posture (apps/web fails open-but-degraded). (5) Pre-deploy checklist — 8 items including watch-path verification, the encryption key check, sister-worker health, `pnpm typecheck` + `pnpm --filter @splash/web build`, and the no-`.js`-extension-in-`packages/ui` invariant from Brief 1. (6) Smoke tests post-deploy — 11 items covering all 9 admin/auth routes plus the deploy-vintage-tab encryption-key sanity check. (7) Known gotchas — eight subsections: CF edge propagation lag (1-3 min), server-action redirect-vs-`router.refresh()` from Brief 19 (with the document-upload exception per Brief 37), modal-portal pattern from Brief 45, cookie-based middleware Edge-runtime constraint from Brief 1 (constants must NOT be imported from `@splash/auth`), cross-origin dev limitation, customer-facing routes are NOT apps/web's job (Brief 50 + 23), service binding == sister-worker dependency, service bindings require explicit `Origin` header. (8) Rollback procedure — git revert (preferred), CF dashboard rollback (faster but requires git follow-up), emergency cookie-purge protocol. (9) Cutover-day specific (BUILD_STATE item 13) — uncomment routes block to `splashcarwashes.info/*` catch-all (most-specific-match-wins handles per-worker route precedence automatically), remove `NEXT_PUBLIC_*_WORKER_URL` from CF Builds env, smoke-test all 8 admin routes on production hostname. (10) Post-cutover follow-ups — rename `splash-signup-next` → `splash-signup` in CF + apps/web `[[services]]`, retire legacy `info-signup-worker`, decommission staging routes, optional cross-references backfill into the five sister `PRE_DEPLOY_*.md` files. **Files created:** 1 — `PRE_DEPLOY_WEB.md` (≈3,554 words; brief expected 2,000-3,500 words — slightly over the upper bound but acceptable for the listed scope; the eight gotchas + ten-section structure don't compress further without losing substance). **Files modified:** 3 — `BRIEFS/INDEX.md` (item 15 row updated to "Completed (2026-05-06 — Brief 51)" pointing at the brief file; new Brief 51 row appended at the bottom of the table mirroring Brief 50's pattern of having both an item-numbered row and a brief-numbered row), `CLAUDE.md` (the "Read these every session, in order" section's entry #4 updated from "five files (DAMAGE, DASHBOARD, PERFORMANCE, SIGNUP, SYSADMIN). Per-worker deploy notes. There is no PRE_DEPLOY_WEB.md (gap noted in BUILD_STATE.md)" to "six files (DAMAGE, DASHBOARD, PERFORMANCE, SIGNUP, SYSADMIN, WEB). Per-deployable deploy notes. PRE_DEPLOY_WEB.md (Brief 51) consolidates every apps/web-specific deploy-time gotcha learned across Briefs 1-50"), `BUILD_STATE.md` (this entry; item 15 status flipped to Completed; deployed-components row for apps/web updated to drop "No PRE_DEPLOY_WEB.md"). **Cross-reference verification:** every file path / link inside `PRE_DEPLOY_WEB.md` was glob-checked against the working tree before commit — `PRE_DEPLOY_DASHBOARD.md`, `PRE_DEPLOY_SIGNUP.md`, `PRE_DEPLOY_PERFORMANCE.md`, `PRE_DEPLOY_SYSADMIN.md`, `PRE_DEPLOY_DAMAGE.md`, `apps/web/.env.example`, `apps/web/middleware.ts`, `apps/web/wrangler.toml`, `apps/web/next.config.mjs`, `apps/web/app/admin/_components/ActionForm.tsx`, `apps/web/app/admin/error.tsx`, `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_components/EquipmentOverrideModal.tsx`, `apps/web/app/_lib/me.ts`, and the four `_lib/worker-fetch.ts` files (pricing / damage / sysadmin / performance) all resolve. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Word count slightly over the brief's 3,500-word upper bound** — section 7 (Known gotchas) carries 8 subsections (one per accumulated brief lesson) and section 9 (Cutover-day) walks 8 ordered steps; trimming below 3,500 words would have meant dropping subsections that exist precisely because they bit operators in past staging deploys. The brief's "doc should be substantial" framing was honored over the upper-bound suggestion. (2) **Brief 51 row added to BRIEFS/INDEX.md alongside the existing item 15 row** — Brief 50 set the precedent (it landed both an "item 8/12 status updated" change and a separate "Brief 50" row at the bottom of the table). Following that pattern keeps the index navigable from either the item-priority lens or the brief-number lens. (3) **CLAUDE.md edit phrasing chosen to remove the gap callout entirely** rather than just adding "and WEB" — the gap-flag verbiage ("There is no PRE_DEPLOY_WEB.md (gap noted in BUILD_STATE.md)") is no longer accurate after this brief and would mis-lead a future reader. (4) **Cutover-day section enumerates a single catch-all route** (`splashcarwashes.info/*`) rather than per-path overrides — CF's most-specific-match-wins routing means the five sister workers' explicit per-path bindings outrank the apps/web catch-all automatically; per-path overrides on apps/web's side would only matter if there were apps/web paths LESS specific than worker paths, which is never the case in the current architecture. (5) **`NEXT_PUBLIC_*_WORKER_URL` removal explicitly called out as a step in cutover** — leaving them set in CF Builds env post-cutover would silently route traffic through `*.workers.dev` URLs (double-billing latency and bypassing service bindings). (b) **The "Watch paths" entry of `apps/web/**` + `packages/**`** is similarly inferred from the brief's own Phase 1 section 2 instructions; the operator should verify in CF dashboard. (c) **No `pnpm typecheck` or `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` was run for this brief** — Phase 3.1 of the brief explicitly out-of-scoped both ("No code change. No `pnpm typecheck` or build needed for this brief"). The pre-deploy checklist nonetheless instructs future operators to run them before pushing apps/web changes; the doc preserves that contract for future deploys. (d) **The optional cross-references-backfill into the five sister `PRE_DEPLOY_*.md` files** (mentioning `PRE_DEPLOY_WEB.md` from each) is intentionally NOT done in this brief per the Out-of-scope section ("Updating the per-worker `PRE_DEPLOY_*.md` files with cross-references to the new doc — that's a future hygiene pass if anyone wants it"). The new doc DOES cross-reference all five sister docs in its preamble, so operators who land on `PRE_DEPLOY_WEB.md` first can navigate outward; operators who land on a sister doc first won't see a backref to apps/web's runbook until that hygiene pass runs. **Validation:** No `pnpm typecheck` / build run (out-of-scope per Phase 3.1). "/admin/dashboard" : "/login"` redirect; `dynamic = "force-dynamic"` exported so the cookie read isn't statically analyzed at build time; cookie name duplicated as a literal string per the same Edge-runtime posture as `apps/web/middleware.ts` which already inlines the constant), `CLAUDE.md` (Constraint #1 extended with a one-line callout: "Customer-facing routes are served by their owning workers, NOT by apps/web. apps/web is admin-only post-Brief-50"; "Working with apps/web" section's page lists reorganized — `/signup/[location]` dropped from placeholder list since the file no longer exists, `/` moved to a new third category "Redirect-only pages" with Brief 50 cross-reference). **Files deleted:** 1 — `apps/web/app/signup/[location]/page.tsx` (the never-filled-in Step-7-placeholder; parent `apps/web/app/signup/[location]/` and `apps/web/app/signup/` directories also removed since they became empty). No `next.config.mjs` change needed: the dev rewrite map's comment block already carries the per-decision-8 explanation and there is no `/signup/:path*` rewrite entry to scrub (only `/api/submit-signup` and `/admin/api/:path*` go through signup-worker rewrites; customer URLs were never proxied through apps/web). No `apps/web/wrangler.toml` change needed: production routes are commented per CLAUDE.md constraint #6, the staging catch-all (`staging.splashcarwashes.info/*`) outranks-by-most-specific-match against the existing per-worker patterns and so doesn't need explicit `/signup/*` exclusion. No middleware change needed: the matcher already excludes `/` and `/signup/*` (matcher list is `/admin/:path*`, `/sysadmin/:path*`, `/change-password`, `/login`, `/logout`); the root-page redirect runs in the page component itself, before any /admin/* middleware fires, so there's no loop risk for the authed→`/admin/dashboard` path. **Closes audit items 8 + 12** (signup customer flow ownership decision + root `/` page fill-in) — both were unblocked once Brief 23's "customer forms live in their owning worker" precedent was set, and operator confirmed 2026-05-06 that the same logic applies to signup. Behavior matrix: unauth `/` → 307 `/login`; authed-with-valid-cookie `/` → 307 `/admin/dashboard`; authed-with-EXPIRED-cookie `/` → 307 `/admin/dashboard` → middleware re-validates and 308s to `/login?required=true` (one extra hop on stale-cookie cases, acceptable trade for keeping `/` cookie-presence-only). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Cookie name inlined as the literal string `"sb-access-token"`** rather than imported from `@splash/auth`'s `ACCESS_TOKEN_COOKIE` — `@splash/auth` is server-only Node and breaks Edge runtime; `apps/web/middleware.ts` already takes the same posture (inlined `const ACCESS_TOKEN_COOKIE = "sb-access-token"` at top of file, with a comment noting the duplication is intentional). The root page's runtime is also Edge-bounded post-OpenNext build, so the same trade-off applies. (2) **`export const dynamic = "force-dynamic"`** added — Next.js 15 will attempt to statically analyze server components by default; reading `cookies()` at request time requires the dynamic opt-out so the redirect runs per-request rather than getting a stale build-time bake. (3) **No loader UI / spinner / shell** rendered before the redirect — the brief explicitly out-of-scoped a UX shell, and the SSR redirect lands as a 307 before any HTML ships, so there's no flicker to mask. (4) **Empty parent directories deleted** — both `apps/web/app/signup/[location]/` and `apps/web/app/signup/` had no other children once the placeholder was deleted; leaving them as empty dirs would have been clutter and would have shown up in future grepping for signup-related code in apps/web. (5) **No `next.config.mjs` rewrite scrubbing** — the existing rewrite map already explicitly comments that `/signup/*` customer routes aren't proxied through apps/web (per decision 8), so there's no entry to remove. The dev rewrites that DO proxy through signup-worker (`/api/submit-signup`, `/admin/api/:path*`) stay — those are admin/API surfaces, not the customer signup form itself. (6) **CLAUDE.md callout placed inside Constraint #1's body rather than as a new constraint** — keeps the customer-URL-immutability rule and the worker-ownership rule colocated; a future reader looking at constraint #1 sees both pieces in the same paragraph. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **No headless smoke test possible** — Phase 5's smoke tests (incognito visit to staging `/`, sign-in then `/`, visit `/signup/oswego` to prove edge route binding is live) require operator-side staging deploy; CLAUDE.md headless rule prohibits this session from deploying. Operator runs the three smokes after the next CF Workers Builds redeploy. (b) **The `apps/web/wrangler.toml` staging catch-all relies on per-worker route patterns being more specific** — confirmed in Brief 16's outcome notes; no change needed for Brief 50, but flagged for awareness because cutover (Brief 13) will need to replace the staging catch-all with explicit production routes. (c) **`apps/web/app/admin/error.tsx`'s `UnrecognizedActionError` boundary (Brief 31) does NOT cover `/`** — the boundary lives at `app/admin/error.tsx`, scoped to the `/admin/*` segment. The root `/` page is a pure redirect with no server actions, so it has no failure mode that requires a boundary. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful (1.972s, fully cached — no source change in any package other than `apps/web`'s root page, which is not a typecheck-distinguishable surface since the Next.js typecheck via `tsc -p tsconfig.json` runs once per turbo task and turbo's cache hit short-circuits on file-level hashes that didn't change for `apps/web`'s app code in a typecheck-relevant way; explicit clean check via `pnpm --filter @splash/web typecheck` re-ran fresh and succeeded in 2.1s with zero errors); `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — `next build` compiled in 7.4s, all 11 routes generated (down from 12 pre-Brief-50 because `/signup/[location]` is gone); `/` route bundle 127 B / 102 kB First Load JS (the placeholder also called `redirect()`, so the bundle shape is essentially unchanged; the only delta is the cookie-presence branch added to the server function, which doesn't ship to the client at all). All other route bundles unchanged. **Operator follow-up (smoke tests on next staging redeploy):** (i) `https://staging.splashcarwashes.info/` in incognito → instant land on `/login` (no flicker, no shell); (ii) sign in → land on `/admin/dashboard`; manually navigate back to `/` → instant land on `/admin/dashboard` (no flicker); (iii) `https://staging.splashcarwashes.info/signup/oswego` → renders the customer signup form from `splash-signup-next` (not 404, not the apps/web placeholder), proving the edge route binding for `/signup/*` is live and apps/web is no longer in the path. Why: Brief 42's executor encoded assignees as `[{ id: <id> }]` matching the brief's documented sample, but MaintainX's API actually requires every assignee object to include `type: "USER"` — confirmed via 400 response on 2026-05-06 with `{"errors":[{"error":"must have required property type","fieldPath":"assignees.0.type"}]}` on both the Brief 42 customer-submit path and the Brief 43 GM-modal override path (both flow through the same `createMaintainXWorkOrder` helper, so a single helper-side fix lands both). The `type` field was a documentation gap inherited from the brief itself (operator's pasted MaintainX sample omitted it too). One-line schema fix; nothing else in `buildPayload` / `createMaintainXWorkOrder` / `extractWorkOrderId` / call sites changed. Brief-042's docs (JSON example in Context + Phase 3.2 bullets) and CLAUDE.md's MaintainX integration entry under "Working with workers" now both document the `type: "USER"` requirement with a Brief 46 cross-reference so a future executor / reader doesn't reproduce the bug. `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful (2.518s, fresh build on `@splash/damage-worker` only); `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker build` N/A (workers have no `build` script — they bundle at deploy time via wrangler), so equivalent dry-run validation `wrangler deploy --dry-run` succeeded — Total Upload 1685.75 KiB / gzip 381.94 KiB, all 6 bindings (DB / R2_BUCKET / IMAGES / MAINTAINX_MODE="test" / MAINTAINX_BASE_URL / APPS_WEB_BASE_URL) resolved cleanly. Operator must confirm post-redeploy that both an `equipment_related = yes` customer submission AND a GM modal override produce a `[maintainx]` success entry in the claim's activity log instead of the `assignees.0.type` 400. Earlier: Brief 45 completed; Brief 43's `EquipmentOverrideModal` now renders its dialog through `createPortal(…, document.body)` instead of inline, eliminating the nested-`<form>`-inside-the-parent-transition-`<form>` invalidity that caused operator-confirmed ~4/5 silent submit failures (the 1/5 success was the browser sometimes inerting the inner form correctly; the 4/5 failure was the inner form's submit handler interfering with React 19 `useActionState` dispatch on the outer form, resulting in only the post-`setOpen(false)` `router.refresh()` GET firing — no transition POST). Submit button stays in the parent form so `submitButton.form` continues to resolve, but the dialog's `<form onSubmit={handleConfirm}>` now lives outside it in the DOM, so `form.requestSubmit(submitButton)` walks the React form-action pipeline cleanly every time. SSR-guarded via `typeof document !== "undefined"`. Leading comment block on `EquipmentOverrideModal.tsx` extended to record the rationale so a future reader doesn't "simplify" it back. apps/web typecheck 13/13 green; `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded; `/admin/damage/[id]` bundle 4.11 → 4.15 kB (+0.04 kB) / 109 kB First Load JS unchanged. No worker-side changes — damage-worker not redeployed. Operator must confirm 5/5 submissions reach the worker after the next apps/web CF Workers Builds deploy. Earlier: Brief 43 completed; GM/RM/admin/super_admin can now flip a claim's `equipment_related` no→yes when approving into the two active-repair branches ("Approved — Pending Quotes", "Approved — In House — Parts Ordered"). New apps/web `EquipmentOverrideSubmit` client island (`apps/web/app/admin/damage/_components/EquipmentOverrideModal.tsx`) renders a modal-gated submit button on those two transition rows when `claim.equipment_related === 0`; modal asks "Was this damage equipment related?" with No/Yes radios + a 6-option `equipment_piece` dropdown (hard-forked from worker `EQUIPMENT_CHOICES`); confirm appends `override_equipment_related` + `override_equipment_piece` hidden inputs to the existing transition `<form>` and re-submits via `form.requestSubmit()` so the existing `<ActionForm>` + `useActionState` pipeline handles the dispatch unchanged. Worker `handleStatusTransition` now reads/validates the override fields (yes only valid on the two target statuses, only no→yes flips, equipment_piece required + ≤200), folds `equipment_related = 1` + `equipment_piece = ?` into the same UPDATE that applies the status change, then post-batch fires `tryCreateMaintainXIfMissing` — re-reads `claims.maintainx_workorder_id` and short-circuits when non-null (defense-in-depth dedupe), constructs a transient post-UPDATE `ClaimRow`, calls Brief 42's `createMaintainXWorkOrder` with the same fail-soft posture (8 s `AbortController` timeout, helper never throws, `[maintainx]` activity-log entries on success/failure). Worker response now optionally includes `maintainx_attempted` + `maintainx_ok` flags; `transitionAction` surfaces a "Status updated to X, but the MaintainX work order couldn't be created — see activity log" success-toned message on the failure path (the transition itself committed). Override side-effect annotated on the same `status_change` activity row via a `[Equipment override]` sentinel suffix in the notes — no new activity_type introduced (D1 CHECK constraint still blocks them). Two paths now create MaintainX WOs: Brief 42's customer-form submit and Brief 43's GM-side approval override; both share the helper + the `claims.maintainx_workorder_id IS NULL` dedupe gate so re-triggers land at most one WO per claim. Empirical MaintainX probe still deferred to operator from Brief 42; Brief 43 inherits the same unverified-response-shape posture.)

## Status snapshot

The 5-worker port + 7 shared packages are feature-complete on `pnpm typecheck` (13/13 green) and deployed only to `*.workers.dev`; apps/web has 8 real pages + 0 placeholders + 3 Step-4 stubs, with Brief 1 (login + middleware), Brief 2 (legacy URL redirects + global Header + logout flow), Brief 4 (admin landing dashboard), Brief 5a (damage claim list), Brief 5b (damage claim detail, read-only), Brief 5c (damage write actions: transitions, notes, check-request preview), Brief 5d (damage documents: upload, edit, delete + photo lightbox), Brief 6 (performance tracker list + new-submission form + LocationPicker typeahead), and Brief 7 (sysadmin UI: five-card super_admin gate consuming all 5 sysadmin-worker mutation endpoints) landed — the damage manager UI, performance tracker UI, and sysadmin UI are at functional parity with legacy. Production routes for the new workers remain commented in every `wrangler.toml`, and the legacy `info-signup-worker` still owns customer signup traffic on `splashcarwashes.info`. Cutover is gated on apps/web reaching parity for admin / signup / claim surfaces and on a dedicated cutover-strategy plan being run for the only production-critical worker (signup).

| 37 | Brief 37 | Mobile quote-upload fix — bypass Next 15 server actions for the multipart upload path. Legacy `info-signup-worker` upload (`legacy/damagemanager.js:2446` `handleDocumentUpload`) "just worked" on iPhone Safari because it's a vanilla CF-Worker multipart POST + 303 redirect; the new monorepo port routed the same multipart through `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts uploadDocumentAction` (a Next 15 server action invoked through OpenNext-on-Cloudflare-Workers), which threw the digest 924441341@e394 white-page on iPhone (Brief 36 Part B). Brief 37 retires the server-action path entirely: `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_components/UploadDocumentCard.tsx` was rewritten as a plain `<form action="/manage/api/claim/{id}/document" method="POST" enctype="multipart/form-data">` — relative URL resolves through CF same-zone routing in prod/staging and through `next.config.mjs`'s `/manage/api/:path*` rewrite in dev. `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts` `handleDocumentUpload` was changed to 303-redirect on every branch (success → `${appsWebOrigin}/admin/damage/{claimId}`; validation/storage failure → same path with `?upload_error=<msg>`; new helper `buildUploadRedirect(request, claimId, errorMessage?)`); the apps-web origin is read from the request's `Origin` header (set by the browser on every form POST), falling back to `new URL(request.url).origin` for the same-zone production case. `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts uploadDocumentAction` deleted (along with its `damagePostMultipart` import and Brief 36 Part B's defensive try/catch — both now dead code). `damagePostMultipart` deleted from `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` (sole consumer was the deleted action). `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/page.tsx` reads `?upload_error=...` searchParam and renders a new `UploadErrorBanner` above `UploadDocumentCard` when present (re-introduces the param-based error pattern Brief 19 retired, scoped strictly to this one form because it bypasses `<ActionForm>`). The "Add document" anchor button (`<a href="#upload-document">`) was added next to the existing "Add note" button in `RecentNotesBox` — same shape, same smooth-scroll target (the global `html { scroll-behavior: smooth; }` from Brief 22 still applies). HEIC handling unchanged: `packages/storage-r2/src/index.ts uploadClaimPhoto` already mirrors the legacy `uploadToR2` (HEIC → JPEG via `env.IMAGES.input(file.stream()).output({ format: "image/jpeg" })`, fallback to passthrough on conversion failure). **Files modified:** `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts` (handleDocumentUpload + new buildUploadRedirect helper), `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_components/UploadDocumentCard.tsx` (full rewrite — drop ActionForm, plain form posting to `/manage/api/...`), `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts` (delete uploadDocumentAction + drop damagePostMultipart import; re-title docblock), `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` (delete damagePostMultipart), `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/page.tsx` (read ?upload_error + render UploadErrorBanner; add "Add document" anchor button in RecentNotesBox), `BRIEFS/INDEX.md` (Brief 37 row marked Completed), `BRIEFS/QUEUE.md` (Brief 37 line moved to completed-tombstone block), `BUILD_STATE.md`, `BRIEFS/brief-037-mobile-upload-legacy-port-plus-add-doc-anchor.md` (Outcome filled in, Status set to Completed). **Files created:** none. **Files deleted:** none (logical deletions of `uploadDocumentAction` and `damagePostMultipart` are line-level edits inside surviving files). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Worker returns 303 unconditionally** — every error branch redirects with `?upload_error=<msg>` rather than returning JSON. The brief explicitly chose Option A ("Worker responds with a 302 to /admin/damage/{claim_id} — browser follows the redirect"); JSON return values would only matter for non-browser API consumers, and the only consumer was the deleted `uploadDocumentAction`. (2) **Form action is a relative URL** (`/manage/api/claim/{id}/document`) rather than an absolute env-resolved URL — works in prod via CF same-zone routing, works in dev via the existing `next.config.mjs` rewrite. Avoids passing a server-resolved URL prop into the client component, which would have re-introduced the apps/web↔worker indirection this brief is designed to eliminate. (3) **Apps-web origin for the redirect Location is read from the `Origin` header**, falling back to `new URL(request.url).origin`. Browser sets `Origin` on every POST submission; same-zone production has matching origin/url-origin so both paths land at the same place; cross-zone dev (without Next rewrites) gets the apps/web localhost origin via the header. (4) **`?upload_error=<msg>` re-introduces the param-based banner pattern Brief 19 retired**, scoped to this one form. Brief 19's pattern flip was specifically for Next 15 server actions whose `redirect()` calls were unreliable on OpenNext-on-CF-Workers; raw HTTP form POSTs from a plain `<form>` to a Cloudflare Worker have no such issue (browser handles the redirect natively). The banner reads `?upload_error` once at SSR; on a fresh load (no form submit) the param is absent and nothing renders. (5) **`damagePostMultipart` deleted entirely** rather than left in place for future use — sole consumer was `uploadDocumentAction`, both retired together. CLAUDE.md guidance: "If you are certain that something is unused, you can delete it completely." (6) **No new pattern abstraction** — brief explicitly out-of-scoped a generalized "raw worker form" helper. The single use case is the document upload; if a second one shows up later, lift the pattern then. (7) **HEIC handling left as-is** — `packages/storage-r2/src/index.ts uploadClaimPhoto` already mirrors the legacy `uploadToR2`. Brief Phase 2.4 said "If the legacy code transcodes HEIC to JPEG before the R2 write, replicate that. … otherwise the legacy worker probably just passes HEIC through" — verified the new helper already transcodes via `env.IMAGES` when bound, falls back to passthrough when not, which is identical to legacy. No code change needed. (8) **`UploadErrorBanner` is rendered above `UploadDocumentCard`** (between the photo gallery and the upload form) rather than at the top of the page next to the confirm-delete banner. The error is contextually about the upload action; rendering it where the user's eye goes after the failed submit is the smaller cognitive jump. (9) **`docType`/`payToType` client state preserved** in the rewritten card so the conditional `required` UX (Brief 20 Bugs 5/6) still gates Quote-row amount/pay_to/vendor/vendor_address — worker re-validates as defense in depth, but pre-submit native HTML `required` attrs are still the better UX. (10) **The "Add document" anchor button's HTML structure mirrors "Add note" exactly** — same container, same Tailwind classes, same `<a href>` shape; the only diff is the href target (`#upload-document` vs `#add-note`) and label text. Pattern continuity matters more than micro-optimizing the markup. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Cross-origin dev without Next rewrites is unsupported.** If the operator runs `next dev` without `NEXT_PUBLIC_DAMAGE_WORKER_URL` set, the upload form's relative-URL POST 404s on apps/web (no rewrite to fall through to). Pre-existing limitation per `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_lib/worker-fetch.ts`'s docblock; this brief inherits the same posture. (b) **The submit-then-redirect pattern is page-reload-based, not optimistic.** Operators see a brief blank-then-loaded transition between submit and the post-redirect SSR. Compared to the prior `<ActionForm>` flow's `useActionState` spinner + inline result banner, this is slightly less polished; brief explicitly chose this trade-off (matches legacy iPhone path that took 2 seconds end-to-end). (c) **`?upload_error=<msg>` persists in URL after the user dismisses by clicking elsewhere.** A hard-reload re-shows the banner. Future polish could clear the param via `router.replace()` from a small client island wrapping the banner, but this isn't worth a separate brief unless operators complain. (d) **No mobile smoke test executed in headless** — the brief's DoD includes "Smoke test: upload an HEIC photo from iPhone Safari to staging — should succeed in <5 seconds." Per CLAUDE.md headless constraint, this session does not deploy; operator runs the smoke test on next deploy. (e) **Bundle delta on damage-worker:** 1661.70 KiB → 1662.94 KiB uncompressed (+1.24 KiB) / 376.97 KiB → 377.28 KiB gzip (+0.31 KiB). Slightly above the brief's "+0.3-1 KiB" estimate — the `buildUploadRedirect` helper + the explicit per-branch redirect calls add a touch more than expected. Comfortably within CF's 3 MiB compressed limit. (f) **Bundle delta on apps/web:** post-Brief-37 `/admin/damage/[id]` is 3.1 kB / 108 kB First Load JS (Next build output). The apps/web client-bundle delta is dominated by removing `<ActionForm>`'s `useActionState`/`useEffect` plumbing from the upload card; net: down a small number of bytes vs. the pre-Brief-37 baseline. Server-side action count drops from 5 to 4 in the route. (g) **Brief 36 Part B's defense-in-depth try/catch on `uploadDocumentAction` is now retired alongside the action itself** — the underlying root cause (server-action multipart path) is excised, so the wrap is no longer load-bearing. The pattern remains on `editDocumentAction` (text-only metadata edit; doesn't need the bypass). (h) **`uploadDocumentAction` is gone from `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts`'s exports** — any future code that imports it gets a TypeScript error pointing at the change. Grep across `apps/web` confirms zero remaining imports post-deletion. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 8.517s (11 cached, 2 cache-miss — `@splash/web` + `@splash/damage-worker` ran fresh as expected). `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — `next build` compiled in 7.9s, all 12 routes generated; `/admin/damage/[id]` route bundle 3.1 kB / 108 kB First Load JS. `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run --outdir=./dist` succeeded — bundle 1662.94 KiB / 377.28 KiB gzip (+1.24 KiB / +0.31 KiB vs Brief 35 baseline). **Operator action items:** (i) on next staging deploy, smoke-test HEIC upload from iPhone Safari — should succeed in <5 seconds (matches the legacy 2-second baseline that motivated this brief); (ii) verify the `?upload_error=<msg>` banner renders correctly by intentionally uploading an unsupported file type (e.g., `.txt` or oversized 11 MB JPEG); (iii) verify the "Add document" anchor button at the top of the claim detail page smooth-scrolls to the upload card on mobile + desktop. | **completed** (2026-05-06) | mobile upload bug closed; legacy port complete | items 5d (upload pipeline this brief patches), 22 (anchor-button + smooth-scroll pattern), 36 (try/catch defense-in-depth landed in Part B; this brief addresses underlying cause) |

| 43 | Brief 43 | GM/RM/admin/super_admin equipment-related override modal on the two active-repair approval transitions ("Approved — Pending Quotes" and "Approved — In House — Parts Ordered"). **Files created:** `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_components/EquipmentOverrideModal.tsx` — single named export `EquipmentOverrideSubmit` (client island that renders the transition form's submit button + a modal-gated pre-flight). Modal asks "Was this damage equipment related?", segmented No/Yes radios with No defaulted, reveals an `equipment_piece` `<select required>` on Yes (6 options hard-forked from `EQUIPMENT_CHOICES` at `apps/damage-worker/src/render/claim-form.ts:18` — Top Brush / Side Wraps / Conveyor / Dryer / Wheel Blaster / Other; comment in the new file pins the worker as canonical so future edits coordinate both sides). Click handler intercepts the submit only when `modalEnabled && enabled` (otherwise the form submits normally), opens the modal, and on confirm appends `override_equipment_related` ("yes"/"no") + `override_equipment_piece` (when yes) hidden inputs to the parent `<form>` and calls `form.requestSubmit()` — re-uses the existing `<ActionForm>` + React 19 `useActionState` pipeline unchanged. ESC + click-outside both cancel without submitting. **Files modified:** `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts` — `handleStatusTransition` now reads `override_equipment_related` + `override_equipment_piece` from the form, validates (yes-only on the two target statuses, only no→yes flips, equipment_piece required + ≤200 chars; "no" or absent passes through unchanged), folds `equipment_related = 1` + `equipment_piece = ?` into the same UPDATE that lands the status change, then post-batch fires a new module-local `tryCreateMaintainXIfMissing` helper. The helper re-reads `claims.maintainx_workorder_id` via `getClaimById` (defense-in-depth dedupe — the loaded claim row's snapshot is pre-update, and a concurrent retry could have landed a WO meanwhile), constructs a transient post-UPDATE `ClaimRow` with `equipment_related=1`, `equipment_piece=overrideEquipmentPiece`, `claim_status=finalTo`, calls Brief 42's `createMaintainXWorkOrder` with the same fail-soft posture (8 s `AbortController` timeout, helper never throws past the handler), writes a `[maintainx]` `note`-typed activity-log entry on success/failure (success: "Work order #N created via approval-time override (mode: M)"; failure: "Override WO creation failed — {error} (status: S, mode: M)"). The status_change activity row's notes field gets a `[Equipment override] {actor} flipped equipment_related to yes during "{finalTo}" approval (equipment_piece: P)` sentinel suffix on the same status_change row — no new activity_type introduced. The transition response now includes optional `maintainx_attempted` + `maintainx_ok` flags (only when the override fired); apps/web `transitionAction` reads them and surfaces "Status updated to X, but the MaintainX work order couldn't be created — see activity log" on the failure path so the operator knows to check the timeline. `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/page.tsx` — `TransitionSection` + `TransitionForm` now accept `equipmentRelated: 0 | 1`; new module-local `EQUIPMENT_OVERRIDE_TARGETS` set holds the two target statuses; the inline `<button type="submit">` was swapped for `<EquipmentOverrideSubmit>` with `modalEnabled={equipmentRelated === 0 && EQUIPMENT_OVERRIDE_TARGETS.has(to)}`. `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts` — `transitionAction` reads `result.body` and emits the failure-toast message when `maintainx_attempted === true && maintainx_ok === false`. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Equipment-piece options hard-forked into the modal file** rather than hoisted into `@splash/types` — the brief explicitly delegated this to executor judgment. The list is short (6 entries), rarely changes, and the in-file comment pins the worker constant as canonical so future edits coordinate both sides. The cross-package alternative would have required a runtime export from `@splash/types` (which today is types-only) plus a re-export through both `@splash/damage-worker` (server) and `apps/web` (client) — three touch points instead of two. (2) **Modal-show condition is purely client-side**: the worker re-validates as defense-in-depth (rejecting yes-with-other-target and yes-when-already-yes), but the apps/web modal only opens when `claim.equipment_related === 0` AND target is one of the two active-repair statuses AND the button is enabled. Hidden inputs are appended via `appendHiddenInput` which dedupes by name — re-opening the modal after a previous confirm updates in place rather than appending duplicates. (3) **`form.requestSubmit(submitButton)` invoked with the button as the submitter** so the form's submit pipeline picks up the same React `formAction` it would on a native click — the `<ActionForm>` `useActionState` handler reacts to it identically. (4) **Post-batch dedupe re-read** in `tryCreateMaintainXIfMissing` short-circuits when `maintainx_workorder_id != null` already — covers the scenario where Brief 42's form-submit flow created a WO but the GM somehow approved with override anyway (shouldn't happen because the modal's `modalEnabled` check gates on the loaded `equipment_related === 0`, but a stale page state + concurrent submission could in theory race). The first writer wins via `updateMaintainXWorkOrderId`'s "UPDATE … WHERE maintainx_workorder_id IS NULL" semantics; the dedupe re-read avoids the wasted MaintainX POST. (5) **Override side-effect on the same status_change row** rather than a separate activity row — per brief Phase 1.5 ("don't introduce a separate activity_type"). The `[Equipment override]` sentinel + `[maintainx]` follow-up rows on the timeline tell the full story; the existing `note`-typed timeline entries cover the audit need. (6) **WO URL pattern + empirical probe** still inherited from Brief 42 — Brief 43 doesn't introduce new MaintainX call shape; the helper signature was stable before this brief landed. (7) **Modal styling uses inline tailwind classes** rather than ModalShell from `@splash/ui` — `ModalShell` is a 5-modal overlay primitive sized for the signup-worker's success/deny/warn cards, with hard-coded card width (max 420px, padded `40px 30px`, centered text). The override modal's content (left-aligned copy, segmented choice, conditional dropdown, two-column action row) didn't fit cleanly inside ModalShell's prescribed layout. The ad-hoc dialog implementation matches the visual posture of the existing confirm-delete banner pattern on the same page. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Empirical MaintainX probe still pending** — inherited from Brief 42; flipping `MAINTAINX_MODE` to `"production"` is still gated on the operator running the probe and confirming the WO ID extractor's fallback chain works. (b) **The override modal's "Yes" branch fires the MaintainX hook synchronously inside the request lifecycle** — adds up to 8 seconds (the AbortController timeout) to the transition's response time on the WO-failure path. apps/web's `<ActionForm>` shows the loading state via `data-pending="true"`; operators on a slow MaintainX response will see the button disabled for the duration. Acceptable for v1; future polish could move the hook to `ctx.waitUntil()` (out of scope per CLAUDE.md "no abstractions beyond what the task requires"). (c) **Worker's response-shape extension only emits `maintainx_attempted`/`maintainx_ok` on the override path** — non-override transitions return the same shape they always did, so existing apps/web message handling is unchanged for every other transition. (d) **The "Cancel" button + outside click + ESC all close the modal without firing the transition** — the underlying form is unchanged in DOM, so a subsequent click of the same submit button re-opens the modal in its initial state (No selected, no piece). (e) **No headless smoke test possible** — the brief asked to confirm "smoke-testing both paths in test mode produced exactly one MaintainX WO per claim (no duplicates from the dedupe gate)"; this requires a live MaintainX call which headless cannot exercise. Operator must run the smoke test post-deploy: (i) submit a customer claim with `equipment_related=1` (Brief 42 path), confirm one WO; (ii) on a separate claim, submit with `equipment_related=0`, then GM-approve into one of the two target statuses with the modal saying Yes, confirm one WO; (iii) attempt to submit the override modal twice on the same claim and confirm the dedupe gate prevents a second WO. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful (7.119s, cache hit on 11, fresh build on `@splash/web` + `@splash/damage-worker` as expected — only those two packages were modified). `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — `next build` compiled in 9.3s, all 12 routes generated; `/admin/damage/[id]` route bundle **3.1 kB → 4.11 kB / 108 kB → 109 kB First Load JS (+1.01 kB / +1 kB)** — `EquipmentOverrideSubmit` client island accounts for the delta. `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run` succeeded — bundle **1679.53 KiB → 1685.55 KiB uncompressed (+6.02 KiB) / 380.73 KiB → 381.86 KiB gzip (+1.13 KiB)** vs. Brief 42 baseline; comfortably within CF's 3 MiB compressed limit. The three new `[vars]` entries from Brief 42 still appear in the dry-run binding table. **Operator action items:** (1) Run the smoke test described in latent-issues (e). (2) The PA Parse JSON schema doesn't need updating — the override path doesn't extend the `claimData` payload (the WO ID lives on the claim row in D1, not on the transition's PA hook).
