Header docblock above the constant explains why on-the-fly is correct — generated columns require deterministic expressions and `julianday('now')` is non-deterministic, and a stored column would need a daily refresh cron for zero benefit at our scale (single arithmetic op per row at query time, negligible at hundreds-of-claims scale). **Phase 2 — damage-worker:** zero-change pass-through; `getClaimsList` already does `json(claims)` so `age_days` rides on the JSON response. Verified the `/manage/api/claims` consumer in apps/web is the only consumer; no other re-declared row type to update. **Phase 3 — apps/web:** new `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_components/AgePill.tsx` (single `<AgePill ageDays lifecycle>` export, `<span>` rounded-full pill, four-tier `tierFor` helper at thresholds 4 / 8 / 15 days; Open = colored escalation, Closed = static neutral so the column stays aligned without escalating terminal claims). Tier classes use existing splash tokens where they exist (`bg-gray-light/60 text-splash-navy/70` for neutral, `bg-splash-deny/20 text-splash-deny` for red) and standard Tailwind palette for amber/orange (`bg-yellow-100 text-yellow-900`, `bg-orange-100 text-orange-900`) — no token-set additions needed. `apps/web/app/admin/damage/page.tsx`: local `ClaimListRow` type extended with `& { age_days: number }`; the Submitted column cell wraps the date + pill in a `flex items-center gap-2` so the pill sits to the right of the YYYY-MM-DD text (Option B from the brief — append rather than add a column, keeps table column count stable and matches legacy layout). Pill carries a `title` attr with absolute day count for hover. Detail page intentionally untouched per Phase 3.4 (list-page glance is the operator's primary use case). **Why:** legacy `legacy/damagemanager.js:2901 renderAgeBadge` rendered a color-escalating pill on the manager's claims overview; the new monorepo port shipped only the YYYY-MM-DD date, forcing operators to mentally diff against today to spot stuck claims. Brief 68 restores the "this one's been sitting two weeks" visual scan. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` (13/13 packages green), `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run --outdir=.tmp-build-brief68` (1720.07 KiB raw / 389.39 KiB gzipped bundle, identical structure to Brief 67's bundle modulo the SELECT projection extension; cleaned up after), `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` (route `/admin/damage` 169 B First Load JS unchanged from baseline; the AgePill component compiles into the same RSC payload as the existing list cells). **Color thresholds:** chosen per the brief (4 / 8 / 15 days for amber / orange / red), not legacy's 3 / 7 (legacy used green / yellow / red at ≤2 / ≤6 / 7+). The brief's curve gives a slightly more permissive "amber" zone for typical multi-day quote-gathering and pushes "red" out to the two-week mark where finance escalation would normally have already kicked in. Operator can tune by editing the `tierFor` constants in `AgePill.tsx`. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) Closed-claim pill chosen as muted neutral (rather than skipping entirely) so the table column doesn't go uneven across mixed Open/Closed result sets; (2) "<1d" label for `age_days === 0` mirroring legacy's `Math.floor(...)` 0-day handling; (3) `Math.max(0, Math.trunc(ageDays))` defensive sanitation in case D1 ever returns a fractional or negative value (clock skew on submitted_at vs julianday('now') boundary). **Latent flag:** apps/web does not depend on `@splash/db-d1` (it's a worker-only package), so `ClaimsListRow` is duplicated in apps/web as a local intersection type rather than imported. Keep `CLAIMS_LIST_COLS` and apps/web's `ClaimListRow` aligned — same convention used by Brief 5a's original Pick<>. **Operator follow-up:** navigate `/admin/damage`, confirm pills render with correct color tiers; tune thresholds in `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_components/AgePill.tsx` if 4/8/15 isn't the right escalation curve. | Claude Code |
| 2026-05-07 | **Brief 67 completed:** Reporting page (`/admin/damage/reporting`) gains four enhancements operator flagged on 2026-05-07: (1) Cost column on the global Approved-by-damage-type table — `byDamageTypeApprovedSql` rewritten as a JOIN-based variant (`LEFT JOIN claim_photos … AND cp.deleted_at IS NULL`, `COUNT(DISTINCT c.claim_id)`, `COALESCE(SUM(CASE WHEN photo_type IN ('Quote','Receipt') AND amount IS NOT NULL THEN amount END), 0) AS cost`); (2) per-location drill-down via inline expandable rows — new `byLocationDrilldownSql` returns one row per (location_code, outcome_bucket, damage_type) using a 5-bucket CASE (`open / denied / approved / closed_approved / closed_other`); apps/web `_components/ByLocationTableClient.tsx` (`"use client"`, `useState<Set<string>>` keyed by location_code, mirrors Brief 53's audit-log pattern) renders four damage-type breakdown panels per row (Open / Closed / Approved-with-cost / Denied), aggregating `denied + closed_approved + closed_other` for the Closed view and `approved + closed_approved` for the Approved view; (3) Avg Days Open column on the By Location table between Denied and Cost — new `byLocationAvgDaysOpenSql` uses `AVG(julianday('now') - julianday(submitted_at))` filtered to `lifecycle_state = 'Open' AND deleted_at IS NULL`, rendered as `${Math.round}d` or `—` when null; (4) footer reworded to drop the v2 double-count limitation disclaimer per operator's 2026-05-07 decision that summing approved quotes + receipts is correct framing (receipts paid out + quotes committed = total exposure). Response shape extended: `by_location[].avg_days_open: number \| null`, `by_damage_type_approved[].cost: number`, new top-level `by_location_drilldown` array. D1 batch grew from 11 to 13 statements (same `baseBindings`). `DamageTypeTable` helper gains optional `showCost` prop. No schema change, no new env vars, no new endpoints. **Why:** operator's 2026-05-07 review of the Reporting page identified four gaps blocking pre-cutover sign-off — quote-amount visibility per damage type, per-location drill-down without filter changes, claim-age signal for stuck open claims, and corrected cost-framing copy. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` (13/13 packages), `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` (route `/admin/damage/reporting` 1.59 kB First Load JS), `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run` (1719.99 KiB raw / 389.35 KiB gzipped bundle, well under 3 MiB free-tier ceiling). **Decision:** chose 5-bucket SQL over 4-bucket — gives renderer exact control over Approved (`approved + closed_approved`) and Closed (`denied + closed_approved + closed_other`) views, matching the per-location table's column semantics. **Latent flag:** Closed drill-down panel intentionally overlaps with Approved + Denied panels (`closed_approved` in both Approved and Closed; `denied` in both Denied and Closed) — operator explicitly requested this redundancy ("yes, redundant by design"); future v2 candidate if stricter mutual-exclusion becomes useful. **Operator follow-up:** navigate `/admin/damage/reporting`, confirm new columns + drill-down render; spot-check global Approved-by-damage-type cost matches sum of per-location Approved drill-down costs. | Claude Code |
| 2026-05-07 | **Brief 66 completed:** RM revert paths from `Approved — Pending Quotes` and `Pending RM Quote Approval`. **Why:** operator confirmed 2026-05-07 (testing as RM dc_role across cortland/cicero/leray/binghamton/oswego) that an RM reviewing a GM-approved claim could not send it back without admin escalation — three "send back" buttons rendered greyed out with "Requires admin or higher". Use case: GM erroneously approves a claim, RM catches it during quote-gathering, RM needs to bounce it back to GM Review, RM Review, or deny outright. **Worker `apps/damage-worker/src/transitions.ts` (canonical state machine):** three existing rows in the "Admin escape hatches" block widened from `role: "admin"` → `role: "rm"` — `Approved — Pending Quotes → Pending GM Review`, `Approved — Pending Quotes → Pending RM Review`, `Pending RM Quote Approval → Pending GM Review`. Two new RM-allowed transitions added — `Approved — Pending Quotes → Closed — Denied` (positioned in the "From `Approved — Pending Quotes`" per-status block, after the existing two forward transitions) and `Pending RM Quote Approval → Pending RM Review` (positioned in the "From `Pending RM Quote Approval`" per-status block, after the existing forward + Closed — Denied transitions). All five send-back paths carry `requiresNote: true` + `clearApprovalDetails: true` to preserve Brief 20's invariant — the worker NULLs `approved_amount` / `approved_quote_id` / `parts_ordered` / `vendor_name` and resets gm/rm/ceo audit stamps as part of the same UPDATE so the detail page's Approval Details box doesn't render stale data on the reverted claim. The "Admin escape hatches" docblock extended with a Brief 66 note explaining that three rows shifted to RM per the 2026-05-07 operator decision; the remaining admin-only entries in that block (Parts Ordered → Pending GM Review, Submitted for Payment ↔ Check Request Submitted / Pending RM Quote Approval, Check Issued → Check Request Submitted, plus the `Pending RM Quote Approval → Approved — Pending Quotes` forward-revert) stay admin-only. **apps/web mirror `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_lib/transitions.ts`:** five matching edits to keep structural parity with the worker. The three widened entries kept their position in the apps/web "Admin escape hatches" section but lose the `(admin)` suffix on their labels — `Send back to GM Review (admin)` → `Send back to GM Review`, `Send back to RM Review (admin)` → `Send back to RM Review`. The two new entries landed in the per-status forward blocks: `Close — Denied` (label) for `Approved — Pending Quotes → Closed — Denied`, and `Send back to RM Review` (label) for `Pending RM Quote Approval → Pending RM Review`. Comment blocks added at every Brief 66 edit site cross-referencing the operator decision date and the worker file. **What stays admin-only:** the `Pending RM Quote Approval → Approved — Pending Quotes` path (forward step past where RM might want to revert from), every post-approval / finance-touched revert (`Approved — In House — Parts Ordered → Pending GM Review`, `Approved — In House — Repaired ↔ Approved — In House — Parts Ordered`, `Approved — Check Request Submitted → Pending RM Quote Approval`, `Approved — Submitted for Payment ↔ Approved — Check Request Submitted` / `Pending RM Quote Approval`, `Approved — Check Issued → Approved — Check Request Submitted`), and every `Closed —` state reopen. Admin oversight on those still warranted because work is in flight or finance has touched the row. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 ✓; `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run --outdir=.tmp-build` bundles cleanly at 1717.17 KiB / gzip 388.88 KiB (~+2 KiB over Brief 65 baseline from the added comments + two new transitions; `.tmp-build` cleaned up post-run); `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` 13/13 routes ✓. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (a) the three widened transitions stayed in the worker's "Admin escape hatches" block rather than relocating to the per-status forward blocks — moving them would split the comment block documenting the `clearApprovalDetails` rationale; the block docblock now explicitly notes that three rows are RM-allowed per Brief 66 with cross-reference to the operator decision date. (b) the apps/web mirror followed the same positioning to keep diff-friendly side-by-side review with the worker file. Side-by-side post-edit confirmation: every from/to pair has identical (role, requiresNote, clearApprovalDetails) values across the two files. **Out of scope (deferred):** widening RM access on the post-approval chain (Parts Ordered, Repaired, Check Request Submitted, Submitted for Payment, Check Issued); reopens from any `Closed —` state; new RM revert paths for `Closed — Approved/No Response`; GM revert access on Approved-family states. **Operator follow-up:** re-test the same RM user (4309ed6f-95f6-4927-b8e6-715d9aca5f95) on a claim in `Approved — Pending Quotes`; confirm Send back to GM Review / Send back to RM Review / Close — Denied render enabled (and the `Pending RM Quote Approval → Pending RM Review` button on appropriate-state claims). | Claude Code |
| 2026-05-07 | **Brief 65 completed:** Daily open-claims summary cron — bolted onto damage-worker as a sibling `scheduled` handler on the default export. **Why bolt-on (not a new worker):** all bindings (`env.DB`, Supabase env, MaintainX env) already exist; reuses `listClaims` from `@splash/db-d1`; one fewer worker to provision/secret-bind/observability-toggle. CF Workers support `{ fetch, scheduled }` on the same default export and observability differentiates by `eventType: scheduled` (Brief 63's block covers both automatically). **Implementation (3 modified files + 1 new):** (1) `packages/db-supabase/src/summary.ts` (NEW) — two helpers: `listSummaryRecipients(env)` reads `auth_unified` via PostgREST, filters server-side on `dc_role IN (gm,rm,admin,super_admin)`, orders dc_role asc + email asc for grep-able cron logs, fail-soft returns []; `fetchLocationRoster(env)` reads `pricing_simple` for `location_code, location_pretty, am_email, area_manager, rm_email, regional_manager`, collapses on first-seen by code (multiple package rows per location collapse via the location-level columns being identical post-trigger-sync), returns `Map<location_code, LocationRosterEntry>` for O(1) lookup. (2) `packages/db-supabase/src/index.ts` — re-export `./summary.js`. (3) `apps/damage-worker/wrangler.toml` — append `[triggers] crons = ["0 13 * * *"]` block (13:00 UTC = 8 AM ET) above `[observability.logs]`; comment block documents fail-soft posture and operator's wrangler-secret bind step. (4) `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts` — import the new helpers + `DamageRole` type; add `DAILY_SUMMARY_WEBHOOK_URL?: string` to the `Env` interface; default export becomes `{ fetch, scheduled }` with `scheduled(_controller: ScheduledController, env, ctx)` calling `ctx.waitUntil(runDailySummaryCron(env))`. **`runDailySummaryCron` flow:** (a) check webhook secret bound — if not, log + return; (b) parallel fetch recipients + locationsByCode + listClaims({lifecycle:"Open", limit:5000}); catastrophic failure logs and re-throws so CF marks the invocation failed (per-user errors stay swallowed); (c) for each recipient, filter (admin/super_admin: unrestricted; gm/rm: filter to dc_locations) → skip-on-empty → `buildDigestPayload` → `postDigest`; (d) accumulate sent/skipped_empty/failed_post counts; (e) final `console.log("[daily-summary] batch complete", {…})`. **`buildDigestPayload`** groups claims by RD email → RM email → location_code, materializes the hierarchy with counts rolled up at each level, sorts by count desc + name asc within each level, claims sorted submitted_at asc, age_days computed (floor at 0) from `as_of`, vehicle assembled as `"<year> <make> <model>"` with `"—"` fallback. Locations with null rd_email/rm_email bucket under sentinel name `(unassigned)`. **`postDigest`** uses `AbortSignal.timeout(15_000)` + Content-Type JSON; non-2xx and thrown both log and return false (caller increments failed counter). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **`name` field on `SummaryRecipient` is always null today** — the `auth_unified` view does NOT expose a name column (verified via `packages/db-supabase/src/auth-context.ts`'s `AuthUnifiedRow` shape). The brief explicitly anticipated this with "null otherwise"; PA's email template can fall back to the email's local-part for greetings, or operator can later extend the view to project `auth.users.raw_user_meta_data->>'full_name'` and the helper's interface stays compatible since the field is already nullable. (2) **Helper file is a new sibling `summary.ts`** rather than appended to `auth-context.ts` — brief explicitly allowed "(or sibling new file `summary.ts` if cleaner — executor's call)"; the sibling keeps the auth-context module focused on session shape. (3) **`listClaims` limit override = 5000** — passed `{lifecycle:"Open", limit:5000}` to override the helper's default 100. Realistic open-claims volume (~50/loc/year × 67 locations × ~0.5 still-open ≈ 1675) fits comfortably; a single fetch covers admin/super_admin's full view without pagination. v2 candidate: chunk by RD or page the claims list if super_admin volume eventually exceeds PA's inbound JSON limit (~4 MB). (4) **`scheduled(_controller: ScheduledController, …)` not `ScheduledEvent`** — TypeScript's `ExportedHandlerScheduledHandler` types the first arg as `ScheduledController` not `ScheduledEvent` (caught by `pnpm typecheck` on first attempt). (5) **In-code `SUMMARY_DC_ROLES` constant** — single edit point for opt-out at the role level. The brief's wording is "every gm/rm/admin/super_admin"; this constant documents that policy in code (and is referenced in CLAUDE.md's new Daily-summary glossary entry). (6) **Multi-row pricing_simple collapse via first-seen** — `pricing_simple` has multiple rows per location_code (one per package); the columns we read are denormalized FROM `locations` BY `trg_sync_pricing_simple` and so are identical across all rows for a code. First-seen collapse is correct and avoids a SELECT DISTINCT round-trip. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Operator must bind `DAILY_SUMMARY_WEBHOOK_URL`** via `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler secret put DAILY_SUMMARY_WEBHOOK_URL` after the next CF Workers Builds deploy lands; until then the cron logs `"[daily-summary] DAILY_SUMMARY_WEBHOOK_URL unbound — skipping"` and exits cleanly. (b) **Operator must build the PA flow** per the sample payload in the brief Outcome (HTTP request received → schema gen from sample → Send email V2 with To = `triggerBody()?['user']?['email']`, Subject = "Daily damage-claim summary — {N} open", Body = HTML with nested loops over regional_directors → regional_managers → locations → claims). (c) **No headless smoke test possible** — `wrangler dev --test-scheduled` runs locally but a true E2E requires the real D1 binding; defer to first 13:00 UTC tick post-deploy. (d) **Open-claim definition is `lifecycle: "Open"` for v1** — mirrors Brief 59's bucket (any claim_status NOT starting with `Closed —`). v2 candidate: subdivide into "Pending Review" (more actionable for GMs) vs "Approved in-flight" (waiting on parts/checks). (e) **Single 13:00 UTC tick for all recipients** — no per-user timezone preference today; v2 could read a `timezone` column from auth_unified or a user-prefs table. **Bundle delta:** dry-run **1715.21 KiB / gzip 388.18 KiB** (Brief 64 baseline ~1705 KiB / ~386 KiB; +~10 KiB raw / +~2 KiB gzip — within budget). **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful (4.071s after fix; first attempt failed with TS2322 on `ScheduledEvent` vs `ScheduledController`); `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run --outdir=.dryrun` bundled cleanly; `.dryrun` cleaned up afterward. **Operator follow-up:** (i) bind `DAILY_SUMMARY_WEBHOOK_URL` via wrangler secret post-deploy; (ii) build the PA flow (HTTP → email) with the sample payloads in the brief Outcome; (iii) verify the next 13:00 UTC tick produces emails for currently-active gm/rm/admin/super_admin users with open claims; (iv) flag any per-user opt-out requests for v2 (today's path is "remove their dc_role" or "edit `SUMMARY_DC_ROLES`"). | Claude Code (Brief 65 session) |
| 2026-05-07 | **Brief 64 completed:** One-line fix (with cascading cleanup) for Brief 61's `setDcRole` helper in `packages/db-supabase/src/users.ts`. **Latent defect (Brief 61):** the helper modeled its `damage_claim_user_roles` write after `setRole`'s `user_permissions` write and assumed an `email` column on the dc_role table. The actual schema is `(user_id, dc_role)` — email lives on `auth.users` and is joined by the `auth_unified` view at read time (consistent with the contract documented in `packages/db-supabase/src/auth-context.ts` and confirmed by Brief 011b's view-definition audit). **Symptom:** every Set DC Role submission 500ed with Postgres 42703 (`column damage_claim_user_roles.email does not exist`; CF requestId `5ZAAKPF23IPE2KMG` per operator's 2026-05-07 log). **Fix scope (extended beyond brief's literal Phase 1.1):** the brief asked to drop `email` from the upsert payload only, but the helper also had two `.select("user_id,email,dc_role")` calls — the unconditional before-snapshot read at users.ts:366 (which would 42703 BEFORE the upsert was reached) and the upsert RETURNING select at users.ts:421 (which is part of the same INSERT-ON-CONFLICT-RETURNING statement, also 42703). Both selects had to drop `email` for the function to succeed; the operator follow-up step in the brief — re-test the failing user and confirm a 200 response — is otherwise unreachable. Also dropped `email` from the local `DamageRoleRow` interface (no longer matches a real row shape with it included), updated the helper docblock with a Brief 64 schema note ("writes pass only `(user_id, dc_role)`; email lives on auth.users"), and corrected the sysadmin-worker `handleSetDcRole` docblock + the inline "Resolve email — damage_claim_user_roles.email is NOT NULL" comment to drop the bogus claim (the email arg is still resolved + passed to the helper for future audit-log surfacing; the helper just doesn't write it to the table). CLAUDE.md sysadmin/audit-log paragraph gains a Brief 64 schema note that future helpers touching `damage_claim_user_roles` / `damage_claim_user_locations` must NOT model their writes after `user_permissions` (which legitimately denormalizes email for fraud-detection lookups). **Lesson:** `setRole`'s payload shape isn't a template for the dc_role tables. The two domains have different denormalization rules — `user_permissions` carries email; `damage_claim_user_roles` does not. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Extended the fix beyond the brief's literal Phase 1.1** — the brief's Phase 1.4 said "No other change in this file" but the rationale clause specifically referred to `damage_claim_user_locations` writes, not the in-table `email` selects. Strict literal compliance would leave the function still 42703-failing on the first SELECT and the operator follow-up step unreachable; the brief's evident intent is "stop referencing a non-existent column," so all three references were dropped. (2) **Function signature kept** — `email: string` stays on the args type per brief Phase 1.2; callers continue to pass `session.email` for any future audit-log surfacing. (3) **Sysadmin-worker comment touched** — the brief's Phase 3.3 only called out CLAUDE.md updates, but leaving the misleading "damage_claim_user_roles.email is NOT NULL" comment in `handleSetDcRole` would propagate the same wrong assumption to the next reader; one-line correction. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **PostgREST behavior question** — the brief author identified the upsert as the failing call, which suggests the operator's CF log showed the error message tagged to the upsert specifically. If PostgREST's column-resolution short-circuits on the first SELECT (as PostgreSQL semantics suggest it should), the line-366 SELECT should have been the failing call — the discrepancy is unresolved here but doesn't affect the fix correctness. (b) **No headless smoke test possible** — operator must redeploy splash-sysadmin (CF Workers Builds on push to `main`), then re-exercise Set DC Role on the user that triggered the 500 and confirm a 200 with both `damage_claim_user_roles` and `damage_claim_user_locations` writes landing. (c) **Audit-log entries pre-Brief-64 do not exist** — failed submissions never wrote any rows; the audit-log filter `target_type = damage_claim_user_roles` will have zero rows until the first successful invocation post-fix. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` passes (13/13, 3.504s, 6 cache hits + fresh rebuilds on `@splash/db-supabase` and downstream consumers). `pnpm --filter @splash/sysadmin-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run --outdir=.tmp-build` bundles cleanly at **760.66 KiB / gzip 143.35 KiB** (Brief 61 baseline 760.70 / 143.35 — essentially identical, since the diff removes a few lines from a single helper). `.tmp-build` directory removed afterward. **Diff size:** ~6 functional lines removed (3 references to `email` dropped from the helper + 1 interface field dropped) plus ~10 lines of docblock/comment rewrite across 2 files. **Operator follow-up:** re-test Set DC Role on the same user that triggered requestId `5ZAAKPF23IPE2KMG` and confirm a 200 response. | Claude Code (Brief 64 session) |

| 2026-05-07 | **Brief 62 completed:** One-line fix for `getMaintainXLocationId`'s two-step Supabase join in `packages/db-supabase/src/locations.ts`. **Latent defect (Brief 42):** the helper's step-2 query joined on `locations.site` (location name like "Oswego") instead of `locations.site_number` (the actual integer business key, surfaced as text in `pricing_simple.site` by `trg_sync_pricing_simple` — e.g., "147"). Same bug class Brief 49 fixed for `getLocationContactInfo`. **Symptom:** every MaintainX WO created since Brief 42 shipped — both the customer-submission path (Brief 42) and the GM-side equipment override modal path (Brief 43) — had a blank Location column in MaintainX. Operator screenshot 2026-05-07 surfaced the bug across WOs 148490 / 148492 / 148496 / 148512 (and presumably every WO between Brief 42's deploy and this fix). The damage worker's null-guard at `apps/damage-worker/src/maintainx.ts:133` made the bug surface as a missing field rather than a 4xx — `body.locationId` was simply omitted from the WO POST when the lookup returned null. **Fix:** change the URL search-param key from `site` to `site_number` in the step-2 `locations` query; the rest of the helper (regex slug guard, fail-soft on fetch throw / non-2xx / empty result / missing maintainx_id, finite-number guard) is unchanged. Docblock and inline step-2 comment updated to call out the join-key correction and cross-reference Brief 49 + the parallel `getLocationContactInfo` fix. CLAUDE.md MaintainX integration block gains a "join key" note. **Why not Brief 49's single-query pattern?** `pricing_simple` doesn't carry `maintainx_id` — that column lives only on `locations`. So step-2 has to stay; we just fix the join key. Adding `maintainx_id` to `pricing_simple` (with a sync trigger) would let us collapse to one query, but that's schema-change territory and the join-key fix is sufficient + contained. **Out of scope:** backfilling Location on existing WOs (the damage-worker doesn't track previously-created WOs in a way that lets us bulk-update them; backfill would be a manual operator script against the MaintainX API). Defer unless finance needs the historical attribution. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` passes (13/13); `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run --outdir=.tmp-build` bundles cleanly at 1705.76 KiB / gzip 385.99 KiB; `.tmp-build` cleaned up afterward. **Diff size:** 1 functional line + ~14 lines of docblock/inline-comment rewrite, all in `packages/db-supabase/src/locations.ts`. **Operator follow-up:** submit a test claim with `equipment_related=1` post-deploy and confirm the resulting MaintainX WO carries the correct Location attribution. | Claude Code |

Operator confirmed 2026-05-06 the gap should have been closed already. **Implementation (3 new files + 4 modified):** (1) `packages/db-supabase/src/users.ts` — new `DcRole` type alias re-exporting `@splash/types/claims` `DamageRole`; new `setDcRole(client, args)` helper that reads current state for both tables, applies the change atomically (delete-both for `role: null`, upsert role + replace locations otherwise), and returns `{ before, after }` snapshots for the audit log. For `super_admin`/`admin`, the locations DELETE still runs but the INSERT is skipped — prevents stale-data leakage on downgrade. Multi-picker entries are de-duped before insert. (2) `apps/sysadmin-worker/src/index.ts` — new `handleSetDcRole`; new module constants `VALID_DC_ROLES`, `DC_ROLE_MAX_LOCATIONS`, `DC_ROLE_PATH_RE`. The dispatcher gains a regex match for `POST /sysadmin/api/users/{userId}/dc-role` (the only dynamic-path endpoint on this worker — every other endpoint matches an exact path Set; chose surgical regex match over a router refactor to keep the diff focused). Validation: role allow-list, UUID-shape on path userId, `LOCATION_CODE_RE` per location_code, max 50 entries. For `gm`/`rm`, location_codes is required; for super_admin/admin/null it's silently dropped. Audit-log writes use `action: "set_dc_role"` and `target_type: "damage_claim_user_roles"`. The audit-log allow-lists were extended in three places: `ALLOWED_AUDIT_ACTIONS` gains `"set_dc_role"`, `ALLOWED_AUDIT_TARGET_TYPES` gains `"damage_claim_user_roles"`, and `USER_TARGET_TYPES_CSV` gains `damage_claim_user_roles` so the audit-log `audit_user_id` filter surfaces DC-role rows. (3) `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/LocationCodeMultiPicker.tsx` — new `"use client"` sibling to Brief 39's single-select picker. Multi-select typeahead hits the same `GET /sysadmin/api/pricing-simple/locations?q=` endpoint; selected entries render as removable `bg-splash-blue` chips above the input. Submit shape: one `<input type="hidden" name={name} value={code}>` per selected location_code so the action reads `formData.getAll("location_codes")` as `string[]`. On-mount resolution call for any pre-filled `defaultValues` so existing dc_locations would surface as pretty-labelled chips on first render — fail-soft, falls back to raw code if resolution fails. (4) `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/SetDcRoleFields.tsx` — small `"use client"` wrapper that owns the chosen role's `useState` and renders the locations picker conditionally. Visible for `gm`/`rm`; hidden with inline hint copy for `admin` / `super_admin` ("super_admin and admin bypass location scoping — no locations needed.") and `null` ("This will revoke all /admin/damage access."). The picker stays a separate client island handed in as a `locationPicker` ReactNode prop. (5) `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/SetDcRoleCard.tsx` — server component containing an `<ActionForm>` (Brief 19 pattern). User picker + DC role select + locations multi-picker, posting to the new `setDcRoleAction`. Card mounted on `/admin/sysadmin?mode=users` immediately after `<SetRoleCard>` for visual symmetry. (6) `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/actions.ts` — new `setDcRoleAction` server action. Builds `{ role, location_codes }` JSON body and POSTs to `/sysadmin/api/users/{userId}/dc-role` via the existing `sysadminPostJson` helper (path takes the dynamic userId as URL segment, encoded with `encodeURIComponent`). Empty `location_codes` is sent verbatim for super_admin/admin/null; the worker ignores the field for those roles and rejects empty arrays for gm/rm. Result message reflects role + chosen-locations count. (7) `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_sections/UserOperations.tsx` — imports `<SetDcRoleCard>`, renders it after `<SetRoleCard>`. Header comment updated from "Five user-management cards" to "Six". (8) `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_sections/AuditLogPanel.tsx` — `ALLOWED_ACTIONS` and `ALLOWED_TARGET_TYPES` extended in the same positions as the worker-side allow-lists. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Dynamic path dispatch via regex** — the worker's other endpoints all use exact-path Sets; added a single `DC_ROLE_PATH_RE` and `isOwnedDcRolePost` short-circuit rather than refactoring every endpoint to a router pattern. (2) **`UUID_RE` and `LOCATION_CODE_RE` re-used** — both consts already exist in the file (audit-log handler + create-location handler); reused them. (3) **`SetDcRoleFields` is a separate client wrapper** — opted for show/hide rather than a disabled state because the locations field is *strictly* irrelevant for super_admin/admin (the worker ignores it). (4) **Multi-picker dedupes silently** before insert — defense-in-depth at helper level handles any race or programmatic re-submit. (5) **Always wipe `damage_claim_user_locations`** when setting role to super_admin/admin (not just when clearing) — matches the brief's "stale-data leakage on downgrade" guidance. (6) **Audit-log `before` / `after` shapes** are `{ role, location_codes }` objects (not raw row arrays) — stable to render in the existing AuditLogPanel diff view. (7) **No Create User integration** (brief Phase 5 explicitly out-of-scope) — operator runs Create User → Set DC Role as a two-step flow. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Operator backfill required** — every user created or role-changed via the sysadmin tool pre-Brief-61 currently has empty `damage_claim_user_roles` / `damage_claim_user_locations` and sees no claims. The new tool is the supported way to remediate. (b) **`damage_claim_user_roles.email` column assumed** — the brief specifies the upsert row shape `(user_id, email, dc_role)`; the helper writes it verbatim. If the live table schema doesn't carry `email`, the upsert will throw and the `set_dc_role` audit row never lands. Operator's first invocation on a real user is the empirical confirmation. (c) **Multi-picker on-mount resolution issues N requests** for N pre-filled defaultValues — dormant since the card today doesn't pre-fill, but if a future "edit existing assignment" UX wires defaultValues, the helper's per-code fetch could be batched into a single `or=(eq.code1,eq.code2,...)` request. (d) **No headless smoke test possible** — operator must redeploy splash-sysadmin (CF Workers Builds on push to `main`) and apps/web, then exercise the new card from `/admin/sysadmin?mode=users` to confirm: gm with no locations is rejected; super_admin with submitted locations succeeds, locations are dropped, and any pre-existing dc_locations rows are wiped; clearing access deletes both rows; the Activity log surfaces a `set_dc_role` row with before/after dc_role + location_codes side-by-side via Brief 53's full-width diff panel. (e) **Bundle deltas** — sysadmin-worker dry-run **760.70 KiB / gzip 143.35 KiB** (Brief 54 baseline 755.79 / 142.42; +4.91 KiB / +0.93 KiB gzip — expected from the new handler + helper + audit-list extensions). apps/web `/admin/sysadmin` route bundle **9.2 kB / 114 kB First Load JS** (Brief 53 baseline 8.38 kB / 113 kB; +0.82 kB / +1 kB FLJ from the new `<LocationCodeMultiPicker>` + `<SetDcRoleFields>` client islands). Comfortably within budget. | 2026-05-06 | **Brief 60 completed:** The Reporting page's "By Damage Type" section gained a third column. Brief 59 shipped two side-by-side tables (Approved + Denied) — both terminal-outcome breakdowns. Operator's 2026-05-06 follow-up: the operationally most useful slice is "what's currently in flight" — the Open pipeline — because it answers "where's the volume coming from this period?" without waiting for claims to close. **Why:** terminal-outcome breakdowns lag the actual operating picture; managers need to see in-flight damage-type volume to triage. **Implementation (one new SQL query + one response field + one apps/web grid swap):** (1) `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts` `getReporting` — added a sibling `byDamageTypeOpenSql` next to the existing `byDamageTypeApprovedSql` / `byDamageTypeDeniedSql` blocks. The query is appended to the same `env.DB.batch` array (now 11 statements instead of 10) so total reporting wall-time picks up at most one D1 round-trip's worth of latency on the parallel batch. The new `byDamageOpen` mapping fold (n → count) is identical to the existing approved/denied folds. (3) `apps/web/app/admin/damage/reporting/page.tsx` — `ReportingResponse` TypeScript interface gains `by_damage_type_open: Array<{ damage_type: string; count: number }>`; the `By Damage Type` section grid changes from `md:grid-cols-2` to `md:grid-cols-3`; the existing `<DamageTypeTable>` helper (already factored out in Brief 59) is invoked three times in **Open / Approved / Denied** order. **Order rationale:** Open first because that's the "what's coming in" view operators glance at most often; on mobile (single-column stack) the order matters more, and Open surfaces above the terminal lookbacks. Approved + Denied keep their relative order. The footnote at the bottom of the page was untouched — it talks about cost-aggregation methodology, not the breakdown count, so it didn't need to change. **Files modified:** 2 — `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts`, `apps/web/app/admin/damage/reporting/page.tsx`. Plus `BRIEFS/INDEX.md` (Brief 60 row appended) and this file. **Files created:** none. **Out of scope (per brief):** sub-segmenting the Open column by stage (New / Pending GM / Pending RM / Approved-but-not-paid) — single Open bucket is the v1 ask; v2 if operator wants finer granularity. Adding an Open KPI tile or per-location Open column — those already exist on the Reporting page from Brief 59. Reordering or restyling the existing Approved/Denied tables beyond what's needed to fit a third sibling. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Filter expressed as `lifecycle_state = 'Open'`** rather than re-deriving from `claim_status NOT LIKE 'Closed —%'` — the column is already exposed and Brief 59's KPI tile + per-location pivot use the same predicate; consistency wins. (2) **Brief's suggested per-location intersect (`AND lifecycle_state = 'Open'` rather than approved-style claim_status pattern) ships verbatim** so the new column counts the same set of claims that the Open KPI counts. (3) **No new helper component** — the brief's Phase 2.2 said "factor out a DamageTypeTable helper if Brief 59 inlined the two tables" but Brief 59 already extracted the helper at the bottom of the page. Reused as-is. (4) **Order Open / Approved / Denied** matches the brief's specified order; preserved verbatim including the rationale comment about mobile single-column stack. (5) **`by_damage_type_open` is the SECOND key in the response object** (between `by_location` and `by_damage_type_approved`) so a future reader sees it grouped with its siblings. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Approved + Denied subset relationship to Open** — by design, an Open claim can later transition to Approved (counted by Approved column once it terminates) or Denied. The three columns are NOT mutually exclusive over time, but for any given snapshot `submitted_at BETWEEN from AND to`, an individual claim row contributes to exactly one of {Open, Approved-family-closed, Denied-closed} based on its current `claim_status`. Sum of (Open + Approved + Denied) ≤ total claims for the window because some closed states (e.g., `Closed — Withdrawn`) are neither Approved-family nor Denied. | 2026-05-06 | **Brief 54 completed:** `update_packages_bulk` audit-log entry now writes a real `before` snapshot instead of the legacy `before: null`. Brief 53's full-width diff panel surfaced the gap — the rendering layer was correct but the upstream `before` payload had never been captured for bulk requests. Fix is one new fetch block + a typed interface in `apps/sysadmin-worker/src/index.ts handleUpdatePackagesBulk`: between the per-entry validation loop and the per-row PATCH loop, a single `pkg=in.(pkg1,pkg2,…)` GET against `pricing_simple` fetches the current state of every row about to be patched (focused select list `pkg`, `pkg$`, `single`, `sort` — only the four fields the bulk endpoint can touch). Result is typed as `BulkBeforeRow[]` (new module-scoped interface alongside `BulkUpdateEntry`/`BulkRowResult`) and folded into the `logSysadminAudit` call as `before: { location_code, rows: [...] }`, mirroring the `after` shape's `location_code`+list pattern but with `rows` (current state) rather than `updates` (patches). The `pkg$` column-name's `$` requires PostgREST double-quoting in select lists; URL uses `&select=pkg,%22pkg%24%22,single,sort`. **Fail-soft:** the GET is wrapped in try/catch; on fetch throw or non-2xx status, `beforeSnapshots` stays null and the audit-log entry falls back to the legacy `before: null` shape — audit-log degradation never blocks the bulk update itself. `Array.isArray` narrowing on the parsed JSON ensures malformed Supabase responses (e.g., error object instead of array) don't produce a malformed audit entry. **Cost:** one extra Supabase round-trip per bulk request, regardless of `entries.length` — `in.()` returns all matching rows in a single GET. Bulk cap is 20 rows; well under PostgREST query-length limits. **Files modified:** 1 — `apps/sysadmin-worker/src/index.ts` (new `BulkBeforeRow` interface, new before-snapshot fetch block ~L1454, audit-log `before:` field rewritten to use the snapshot, docblock above the handler updated to describe the new audit shape). Plus standard updates to `BRIEFS/INDEX.md`, `BRIEFS/QUEUE.md`, this file, and the brief itself. **Out of scope (per brief):** per-row before-snapshots inside the patch loop (pre-loop GET is simpler + atomic-ish + consistent with Brief 26's single-row pattern); server-side diffing of changed-only fields (rendering layer's `DiffBlock` handles that visually); backfilling historical `update_packages_bulk` rows (data is gone); other `before: null` writes (`create_user`, `create_location`, `grant_tool` when `was_new`, `reset_password` — inserts have no meaningful before state). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **`BulkBeforeRow` declared at module scope** alongside the existing two bulk-shape interfaces — keeps the three colocated, grep-able. (2) **`(await beforeResp.json()) as unknown` + `Array.isArray` narrowing** rather than direct `as BulkBeforeRow[]` cast — defends against Supabase returning a non-array shape under unexpected conditions. (3) **Empty `rows: []` is preserved as a non-null snapshot** — `before: { location_code, rows: [] }` is informationally distinct from `before: null` (fetch-failed) and helps the operator distinguish "no rows matched" from "we couldn't read them". (4) **`encodeURIComponent` on each `pkg`** in the `in.(...)` list mirrors the per-row PATCH URL's existing posture; defends against legacy pkg names with special chars. (5) **No new endpoint, no schema change, no new env var** — the audit-log column was already `jsonb` accepting null; this brief just makes `before` actually populated for bulk. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Two-step audit cost** — bulk request now N+1 Supabase calls instead of N. +5% wall-time at the 20-row cap; +14-25% for the common 4-7 row request. Acceptable for audit fidelity. (b) **Race window** between the snapshot GET and the per-row PATCH — another operator PATCHing the same `(location_code, pkg)` via the single-row endpoint would mean `before` reflects a state that didn't exist at PATCH time. Multi-operator concurrent edits on the same location are rare. (c) **`before` payload size** ~80-200 bytes for 20 rows × 4 fields; well within `jsonb` row limits. (d) **`pkg$` URL-encoding correctness** — `%22pkg%24%22` decodes to `"pkg$"` (PostgREST double-quoted column-name syntax). Operator's smoke test (rows have `pkg$` field present, not undefined) is the empirical confirmation. (e) **No headless smoke test** — operator must redeploy and re-run a small bulk edit, then click View on the new audit row to confirm BEFORE/AFTER side-by-side renders. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful (1.714s, 12 cache hits + fresh `@splash/sysadmin-worker` rebuild — only modified package); `pnpm --filter @splash/sysadmin-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run --outdir=.tmp-build` succeeded — Total Upload **755.79 KiB / gzip 142.42 KiB** (Brief 52 baseline 755.11 / 142.30 → +0.68 KiB / +0.12 KiB gzip; expected delta from new fetch block + interface + audit reshape + docblock). `.tmp-build` cleaned up. **Operator follow-up:** after splash-sysadmin redeploys (CF Workers Builds on push to `main`), re-run a small bulk edit on any underscored location_code (`batavia_ii`, `batavia_veterans`, etc.) via Update Package's bulk-edit UI; navigate to `/admin/sysadmin?mode=tables`; find the new `update_packages_bulk` row; click View → confirm the diff opens with BEFORE and AFTER side-by-side instead of AFTER-only. | Claude Code (Brief 54 session) |
| 2026-05-06 | **Brief 53 completed:** `/admin/sysadmin` Activity log diff column rewired so its expansion renders as a full-width sibling `<tr><td colSpan={5}>` underneath the primary row, instead of squeezing the JSON inside the ~30-40px-wide DIFF cell. Operator confirmed 2026-05-06 that large diffs (e.g., the recent `update_packages_bulk` on williamsville, a multi-row payload) were rendering as a vertical column of single characters under the prior `<details>/<summary>` toggle — effectively unreadable. **Implementation:** one new `"use client"` component (`apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/AuditRowExpandable.tsx`, ~70 lines) holds per-row `useState<boolean>` for expansion; `AuditLogPanel.tsx` keeps its server-component shape and passes the four primary cell contents (When / Actor / Action / Target) plus a server-rendered `DiffBlock` element to the client island as React-node props. Server-side helpers (`relativeTime`, `TargetCell`, `DiffBlock`, `jsonOrDash`) stay on the server, so `Date.now()`-derived relative time strings don't hydration-mismatch. **Layout:** primary row carries only the toggle button (▶ View / ▼ Hide with `aria-expanded`); expansion row renders BEFORE / AFTER as side-by-side `<pre>` blocks on md+ viewports (single full-width column on mobile or when only one side has data), each capped at `max-h-96` with `overflow-auto` so huge diffs scroll within their pre rather than pushing the rest of the table off-screen. Notes (when present) render below the diff, preserving the prior `<details>` block's behavior. Empty case (both before/after null) shows an italic "No diff payload recorded for this entry." hint. **Border treatment:** primary row drops its bottom border when expanded (no separator between primary + expansion); expansion row carries the bottom border to separate from the next audit entry; expansion row gets a `bg-sudsy-blue-soft/40` tint to visually anchor the diff to its parent. **Multiple rows expand independently** because each row owns its own boolean state — no parent-managed Set required. **Toggle button** carries `aria-expanded={isExpanded}` and the arrow indicator flips inline (▶ → ▼) so the affordance is visually obvious. **Files created:** 1 — `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/AuditRowExpandable.tsx`. **Files modified:** 1 — `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_sections/AuditLogPanel.tsx` (deleted inline `DiffCell` with its `<details>` renderer; added server-side `DiffBlock` helper; rewrote `AuditRow` from a `<tr>` returner to an `AuditRowExpandable` invoker passing four cell nodes + diff content; added new component import). Plus standard updates to `BRIEFS/INDEX.md`, `BUILD_STATE.md`, and the brief itself. **Out of scope (per brief Section "Out of scope"):** worker-side change, API change, separate diff modal, color-coded added/removed diff highlighting (a future jsondiffpatch / react-diff-viewer integration could ship as v2 if operators ask). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Per-row local `useState<boolean>` rather than a panel-level `Set<string>`** — the brief mentioned a Set as the "likely existing pattern" but the prior code didn't track expansion state at all (it used native `<details>`), so there's no incumbent Set to preserve. Per-row state is simpler, meets the "multiple rows independently expandable" requirement, and avoids threading state-setter callbacks through the row mapper. (2) **Server-rendered `DiffBlock`** rather than moving JSON formatting to the client — keeps `JSON.stringify` work off the client bundle, and the only thing the client island actually toggles is a boolean. (3) **Cell contents passed as React-node props** rather than passing raw row data into the client component — the helper functions (`relativeTime`, `TargetCell`, `jsonOrDash`) are pure rendering utilities that run server-side; passing pre-rendered React nodes through the prop barrier keeps them server-side and the relative time string is "frozen" at request render. (4) **Empty-payload hint** ("No diff payload recorded for this entry.") added as a new shape — the prior `<details>` block rendered an empty grid when both before/after were null, so an operator clicking View on a no-op row saw nothing. The new hint is more informative without changing which rows have payload data. (5) **Notes preserved below the diff grid** — the prior `<details>` block included `notes` as a final paragraph; kept verbatim in the new layout under the BEFORE/AFTER grid. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **No headless smoke test** — operator must verify on the next CF Workers Builds redeploy that clicking View on the williamsville `update_packages_bulk` row expands the diff to full width with legible side-by-side BEFORE/AFTER. The brief's Phase 4 smoke-test guidance enumerates the test cases (open / verify side-by-side render / multiple-rows-independent / collapse). (b) **Bundle delta** — `/admin/sysadmin` route bundle grew from 7.06 kB → 8.38 kB / 113 kB First Load JS (+1.32 kB / +1 kB FLJ). Acceptable for the readability win; the new client island is the only addition. (c) **`AuditRowExpandable` returns a Fragment** wrapping two `<tr>`s — React in Next.js 15 / React 19 handles this cleanly inside a `<tbody>`, but if a future refactor wraps each row in a new component layer, fragments-of-`<tr>`s should not be wrapped in a `<div>` (would break HTML table semantics). The new component's leading comment block flags this. (d) **Hydration time formatting** — `relativeTime(row.occurred_at)` is computed once on the server during render; the string is shipped to the client island as a static React node and stays "5m ago" until the next page render. Operator cares about absolute timestamps via the `title` attr on the When cell, so this is consistent with Brief 30's posture. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful (5.725s, 12 cache hits + fresh `@splash/web` rebuild — the one package modified). `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — `next build` compiled in 5.1s, all 11 routes generated; `/admin/sysadmin` route 8.38 kB / 113 kB First Load JS (was 7.06 kB / 112 kB pre-Brief-53). Middleware bundle 34.1 kB unchanged. **Operator follow-up:** after CF Workers Builds redeploys apps/web on push, navigate to `/admin/sysadmin?mode=tables`, find the williamsville `update_packages_bulk` row in the Activity log (substring filter on `update_packages_bulk` action or `pricing_simple` table), click View → confirm the diff opens as a full-width row underneath with BEFORE / AFTER side-by-side; click ▼ Hide → confirm the row collapses; expand two rows simultaneously → confirm independence. | Claude Code (Brief 53 session) |
| 2026-05-06 | **Brief 52 completed:** Latent defect in Brief 26's sysadmin pricing-simple search sanitization patched. `handleSearchPricingSimple` (Brief 26 ~L901) and `handleSearchPricingSimpleLocations` (Brief 39 ~L954) both sanitized user-supplied `q` with `escaped = raw.replace(/[%_,()*]/g, "")` — a strip set covering PostgREST `or()` separators (`,` `(` `)` `*`) and ILIKE wildcards (`%` `_`). The `_` in that set was over-defensive: the query is wrapped in `%...%` for ILIKE, and treating an internal `_` as a single-char wildcard is behaviorally indistinguishable from matching it literally for the operator's substring-search intent. Stripping it corrupted any query containing a literal underscore (e.g., `batavia_ii` → `bataviaii` → `%bataviaii%` matched zero rows). **Why it was dormant until now:** picker-only typing in the original Brief 26 use case (operators typing `batavia`, `binghamton`, `122`) never put a `_` in `q`. Brief 36's bulk-edit UI (`UpdatePackageCard`) introduced an auto-fetch step that passes the FULL canonical `location_code` (often underscored: `batavia_ii`, `batavia_veterans`, `cherry_hill`, etc.) to the search endpoint after a row is picked — that's where the bug bites. Operator confirmed 2026-05-06 that picking Batavia II correctly identified the location_code (4 packages reported in the picker preview) but the auto-fetch returned 0 rows and the UI surfaced "No packages found at this location" even though all 4 packages existed. **Fix:** dropped `_` from the strip regex in both handlers (now `/[%,()*]/g`); pattern is now identical between them. The two surrounding docblocks were updated to spell out the new semantics and cross-reference Brief 52 so a future reader doesn't "restore" the underscore strip thinking it's a SQL-injection guard (it never was — PostgREST is parameterized; the strip exists to prevent the user-supplied substring from accidentally constructing a different `or(…)` clause or expanding into multi-char wildcard semantics). **Files modified:** 1 — `apps/sysadmin-worker/src/index.ts` (two regex literals + two docblock paragraphs). **Files created:** none. **Files deleted:** none. **Out of scope (intentionally not patched per brief):** `handleSearchLocations` at line 1550 (Brief 27 locations editor — same strip-set pattern; the brief explicitly defers any other strip-set sites to their own briefs because `_` impact varies per consumer) and the audit-log `actor_email` filter at line 1893 (Brief 30 — same strip-set; out of scope; email addresses in this org don't contain `_` anyway, so dormant). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Build-equivalent validation substitution** — the brief's Definition of Done called for `pnpm --filter @splash/sysadmin-worker build`, but the worker's `package.json` has no `build` script (workers don't compile; wrangler bundles at deploy time, like every other worker in this monorepo). Substituted `pnpm --filter @splash/sysadmin-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run --outdir=.tmp-build` as the build-equivalent: it runs the same bundling pipeline `wrangler deploy` would but without uploading. Cleaned up the temp output dir afterward. This matches the pattern Briefs 46/47/48/49 took for damage-worker dry-run validation. (2) **Docblock cross-reference to Brief 52** — added "(Brief 52 fix to Brief 26's defect)" to the inline comment so the historical context is grep-able from inside the file. The comment also explicitly explains why `_` is NOT in the strip set anymore and what the operator-intent semantics are, so a defensive future executor doesn't add it back thinking it's a hardening pass. (3) **No new endpoint, no schema change** — per the brief's "Out of scope," a dedicated `/sysadmin/api/pricing-simple/by-location-code` endpoint would be cleaner architecturally (the auto-fetch step is logically a "list packages by location_code" call, not a substring search), but the existing search endpoint with the underscore fix handles the case correctly and the brief explicitly defers the cleaner endpoint as more invasive than the bug warrants. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **`handleSearchLocations` (line 1550)** is the same code shape and likely affected by the same defect class — if an operator searches Brief 27's locations editor by full `location_code` (e.g., `batavia_ii`), they'll see 0 results until that endpoint gets the same fix. The current operator workaround is to type a non-underscore substring (`batavia`); the brief explicitly defers patching it to its own brief because the impact pattern needs to be confirmed first. Empty parent directories `apps/web/app/signup/[location]/` and `apps/web/app/signup/` also removed. **Files unchanged (verified per brief Phase 1.2 + 1.3):** `apps/web/wrangler.toml` (no `/signup/*` route claim, per CLAUDE.md constraint #6 production routes stay commented; the staging catch-all `staging.splashcarwashes.info/*` outranks-by-most-specific-match against the per-worker `signup/*`/`q/*`/`join/*` patterns and so doesn't need explicit `/signup/*` exclusion); `apps/web/next.config.mjs` (the dev rewrite map already explicitly comments that `/signup/*`, `/q/*`, `/join/*` customer routes aren't proxied through apps/web per decision 8 — there's no entry to scrub); `apps/web/middleware.ts` (matcher list `/admin/:path*`, `/sysadmin/:path*`, `/change-password`, `/login`, `/logout` excludes `/` already, so the root-page redirect runs in the page component itself before any /admin/* middleware fires — no loop risk). **Behavior matrix:** unauth `/` → 307 `/login`; authed-with-valid-cookie `/` → 307 `/admin/dashboard`; authed-with-EXPIRED-cookie `/` → 307 `/admin/dashboard` → middleware re-validates and 308s to `/login?required=true` (one extra hop on stale-cookie cases, acceptable trade for keeping `/` cookie-presence-only). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Cookie name inlined as the literal string `"sb-access-token"`** rather than imported from `@splash/auth`'s `ACCESS_TOKEN_COOKIE` constant. `@splash/auth` is server-only Node and breaks Edge runtime; `apps/web/middleware.ts` already takes the same posture (inline `const ACCESS_TOKEN_COOKIE = "sb-access-token"` at top of file with a comment noting the duplication is intentional). | 2026-05-06 | **Brief 45 completed:** `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_components/EquipmentOverrideModal.tsx` now renders its `<div role="dialog">…</div>` block through `createPortal(…, document.body)` instead of inline. **Why:** Brief 43 shipped a working UI but operator confirmed 2026-05-06 that the modal's Confirm only triggered the underlying transition POST ~1/5 times — the other 4/5 attempts produced no transition write, only the post-`setOpen(false)` `router.refresh()` GET. **Root cause (per the brief's diagnosis):** the modal's `<form onSubmit={handleConfirm}>` was nested inside the parent transition `<form>` in the live DOM, because `EquipmentOverrideSubmit` is rendered INSIDE the parent transition form (so `submitButton.form` resolves correctly via the React `buttonRef`) and the conditional modal block is its sibling under a React fragment — when `open === true` the dialog DOM mounted as a sibling of the button, which is a sibling of the parent form's submit pipeline, but the dialog itself contains a `<form>` so the inner form ended up inside the outer one. Browser recovery from invalid nested-form HTML is implementation-defined: sometimes the inner form is detached/inert and events bubble correctly to the outer form (the 1/5 success), sometimes the inner form's submit handler interferes with the outer form's React 19 `useActionState` dispatch and the action never runs (the 4/5 failure). **Fix:** wrap the dialog block in `createPortal(…, document.body)` so the modal DOM lives outside the parent form. The submit button (`buttonRef`) stays in the parent form so `submitButton.form` continues to resolve; only the dialog's DOM moves. SSR-guarded via `typeof document !== "undefined"`. **Files modified:** `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_components/EquipmentOverrideModal.tsx` (one file, ~10 lines changed: added `import { createPortal } from "react-dom"`, wrapped dialog in `createPortal(…, document.body)`, extended leading comment block with the portal rationale + a "do not simplify back" warning so a future reader doesn't reintroduce the bug), `BRIEFS/INDEX.md`, `BUILD_STATE.md`, `BRIEFS/brief-045-equipment-modal-portal-fix.md`. **Files created:** none. **Files deleted:** none. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **No retreat to `submitButton.click()`** — the brief listed this as a possible follow-up if the portal fix alone didn't resolve flakiness (Phase 2.2). I'm landing the portal-only fix first per the brief's primary scope; if the operator's smoke test still shows non-determinism (e.g., 4/5 succeed), Brief 46 can revisit. The brief's analysis predicts portal alone is sufficient because the React state ordering of `setOpen(false)` → `requestSubmit` was never the bug — the DOM-level interference was. (2) **Inner JSX indentation is mildly inconsistent** post-edit (the `<form>` and its descendants kept the original 10-space indent, while the wrapping `<div role="dialog">` is now indented 12 spaces inside `createPortal(…,`). Decision: leave the inner block verbatim per the brief's "the contents of the modal's `<form>` … stay verbatim. Only the wrapping changes." Re-indenting 90 lines would inflate the diff for cosmetic-only reasons; TypeScript/JSX whitespace-insensitive parser doesn't care. (3) **No worker-side changes** — damage-worker is not redeployed by this brief; the bug was purely client-side. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Operator must verify 5/5 submissions reach the worker after the next apps/web CF Workers Builds deploy** — headless cannot exercise the live browser interaction; only the operator can confirm the portal fix actually resolves the flakiness. The brief explicitly flags this as a Phase 2 smoke-test action item. (b) **Future modals rendered inside parent forms should portal-by-default** — recording the gotcha here so future briefs adding a similar pattern (e.g., a confirm-cancel dialog with its own `<form>` inside a transition or upload form) don't recreate this bug. The "render the dialog block via `createPortal`" rule applies any time a child component renders both a submit button (which has to live inside the parent form to be a submitter) AND a dialog containing its own form/event handlers (which need to NOT nest inside the parent form). (c) **No worker-side or PA Parse JSON impact** — Brief 45 is purely a client-side DOM-positioning fix; the override-related FormData payload, the worker validation, and the activity-log shape are all unchanged from Brief 43. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful (4.11s, cache hit on 12, fresh build on `@splash/web` only — only one package modified); `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — `next build` compiled in 5.9s, all 12 routes generated; `/admin/damage/[id]` route bundle 4.11 → 4.15 kB (+0.04 kB) / 109 kB First Load JS unchanged (`createPortal` is already in `react-dom`, so the bundle delta is just the conditional shape change). **Operator action items:** (1) After CF Workers Builds redeploys apps/web on push, find a claim with `equipment_related === 0` in "Pending GM Review" status, click the approve button → modal opens → Yes → equipment piece select → Confirm. Verify in damage-worker CF Observability that a POST/PATCH fired to the transition endpoint AND the claim's `claim_status` updated AND the activity log got two entries (`status_change` + `[maintainx]` `note`). Repeat 5 times. If 5/5 succeed the brief is fully validated. If <5/5, file a Brief 46 with the `submitButton.click()` follow-up per Brief 45 Phase 2.2. | Claude Code (Brief 45 session) |

Server-side validation: yes-only on the two target statuses, only no→yes flips (rejecting yes→yes-with-override avoids accidentally defeating the dedupe gate), piece required + length-capped. When the override fires, `equipment_related = 1` and `equipment_piece = ?` are folded into the same UPDATE that lands the status change (atomic with the status_change activity-log INSERT in `env.DB.batch([updateStmt, activityStmt])`). The status_change activity row's notes field gets a `[Equipment override] {actor} flipped equipment_related to yes during "{finalTo}" approval (equipment_piece: P)` sentinel suffix on the same row — no new activity_type introduced (D1 CHECK constraint still rejects new types per Brief 42's same finding). After the batch commits, a new module-local `tryCreateMaintainXIfMissing` helper fires Brief 42's `createMaintainXWorkOrder` with the same fail-soft posture (8 s `AbortController` timeout, helper never throws past the handler, `[maintainx]` `note`-typed activity-log entry on success/failure). The helper re-reads `claims.maintainx_workorder_id` via `getClaimById` BEFORE issuing the POST so a concurrent writer that already landed a WO short-circuits this path (defense-in-depth dedupe on top of `updateMaintainXWorkOrderId`'s "UPDATE … WHERE maintainx_workorder_id IS NULL" semantics). The transition response now optionally includes `maintainx_attempted` + `maintainx_ok` flags (only on the override path) so apps/web can distinguish the create-failed branch. **Phase 2 — apps/web modal:** new `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_components/EquipmentOverrideModal.tsx` exports `EquipmentOverrideSubmit` — a client island that renders the transition form's submit button + a modal-gated pre-flight. Modal asks "Was this damage equipment related?" with segmented No/Yes radios (No defaulted) and reveals a `<select required>` of 6 equipment-piece options (Top Brush / Side Wraps / Conveyor / Dryer / Wheel Blaster / Other — hard-forked from `EQUIPMENT_CHOICES` at `apps/damage-worker/src/render/claim-form.ts:18` with an in-file comment pinning the worker as canonical). Click handler intercepts only when `modalEnabled && enabled`; otherwise the form submits normally. On confirm, `appendHiddenInput` writes (or updates in place) `override_equipment_related` ("yes" or "no") + `override_equipment_piece` (when yes) hidden inputs onto the parent `<form>`, then `form.requestSubmit(submitButton)` re-uses the existing `<ActionForm>` + React 19 `useActionState` pipeline unchanged. ESC + click-outside both cancel without submitting. `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/page.tsx` — `TransitionSection` + `TransitionForm` now accept `equipmentRelated: 0 | 1`; new module-local `EQUIPMENT_OVERRIDE_TARGETS` set holds the two target statuses; the inline `<button type="submit">` was swapped for `<EquipmentOverrideSubmit>` with `modalEnabled={equipmentRelated === 0 && EQUIPMENT_OVERRIDE_TARGETS.has(to)}`. **Phase 3 — server action:** `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts transitionAction` already forwards every form field via `damagePostForm` (FormData → URLSearchParams), so the override hidden inputs reach the worker without plumbing changes. Added one read of `result.body` to detect `maintainx_attempted === true && maintainx_ok === false` and emit "Status updated to X, but the MaintainX work order couldn't be created — see activity log" on the failure path. **Files modified:** `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts`, `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/page.tsx`, `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts`, `BRIEFS/INDEX.md`, `BRIEFS/QUEUE.md`, `BUILD_STATE.md`, `BRIEFS/brief-043-gm-equipment-related-modal-on-approve.md`. **Files created:** `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_components/EquipmentOverrideModal.tsx`. **Files deleted:** none. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Equipment-piece options hard-forked into the modal file** rather than hoisted into `@splash/types` — the brief explicitly delegated this to executor judgment. The list is short (6 entries), rarely changes, and an in-file comment pins the worker as canonical so future edits coordinate both sides. The cross-package alternative would have required converting `@splash/types` from types-only to runtime-export and a re-export through both worker and apps/web — three touch points instead of two. (2) **Modal-show condition is purely client-side** (`equipmentRelated === 0` + target ∈ {two statuses} + button enabled). Worker re-validates as defense-in-depth (rejecting yes-with-other-target and yes-on-already-yes). The `appendHiddenInput` helper dedupes by name so re-opening the modal after a previous confirm updates in place rather than appending duplicates. (3) **`form.requestSubmit(submitButton)` invoked with the button as the submitter** so React 19's form-action pipeline picks up the `useActionState` formAction identically to a native click. (4) **Defense-in-depth dedupe re-read** in `tryCreateMaintainXIfMissing` short-circuits when `maintainx_workorder_id != null` already — the loaded claim row's snapshot is pre-update, and a concurrent retry could in theory land a WO meanwhile. (5) **Override side-effect logged on the same status_change row** rather than as a separate activity row — per brief Phase 1.5 ("don't introduce a separate `equipment_overridden` activity_type"). (7) **Worker response shape extension is conditional** — `maintainx_attempted` + `maintainx_ok` only emit on the override path; non-override transitions return the same shape they always did. (2) **Activity log uses existing `note` activity_type** with `[maintainx]` prefix — the brief asked for new `maintainx_workorder_created` / `maintainx_workorder_failed` action types, but the D1 CHECK constraint on `claim_activity.activity_type` rejects values outside the existing `"status_change" \| "note" \| "document_added"` union (per the comment at `packages/types/src/claims.ts:75-79`: "An audit-log annoyance but not a bug — preserved here to avoid the cost of a D1 CHECK-constraint rebuild migration"). The legacy code already overloads `note` (and `document_added`) for adjacent semantics. The prefix `[maintainx]` makes the entries grep-friendly. (3) **`ClaimInsert` was NOT extended with `maintainx_workorder_id`** despite the brief's "Read first" line implying it should be. The column always starts NULL on initial insert (we don't have a WO ID at writeClaimBatch time) and is filled via `updateMaintainXWorkOrderId` post-MaintainX. Brief 43's GM modal will also use the UPDATE path (the row already exists at modal time). Adding the field to ClaimInsert would be no-op churn (always passed null). The brief's Phase 4.1 sample code matches this — it uses `updateMaintainXWorkOrderId`, not an extended writeClaimBatch. (4) **Supabase helper added as a SIBLING** to `getActiveLocationByCode` rather than extended onto it — different concerns (slug-resolution at form load vs. id-lookup for WO creation), different return shapes, different call frequencies (slug resolution fires on every `/claims/{slug}` page load + every claim submission; maintainx lookup fires only on equipment_related=1 submissions). The brief explicitly delegated this choice to the executor. (5) **The hook is INSIDE the writeClaimBatch try block**, after `getActiveLocationByCode` resolution. Rationale: (a) `insert` is in scope, avoiding hoisting; (b) errors from `updateMaintainXWorkOrderId` or `logActivity` are caught by the existing D1 catch (logged, swallowed); (c) `d1Success = true` is set before the hook, so a hook throw doesn't retroactively flip it. (6) **Transient `ClaimRow` constructed from `insert`** rather than re-fetching via `getClaimById`. Saves one D1 read per equipment-related submission. The constructed shape uses `lifecycleForStatus(insert.initial_status)` for `lifecycle_state` (the `'Open'` literal hardcoded in writeClaimBatch's SQL is what the row gets, but expressing it via the helper future-proofs against ever changing the default). (7) **WO URL pattern `https://app.getmaintainx.com/workorders/{id}` is unverified** but rendered anyway — the brief flagged this for empirical confirmation; if the pattern is wrong the link 404s but the WO ID is still visible in the link text. (8) **Bundle delta on damage-worker**: 1667.47 KiB → 1679.53 KiB uncompressed (+12.06 KiB) / 378.06 KiB → 380.73 KiB gzip (+2.67 KiB). Helper module ~6 KiB source + transient ClaimRow construction + activity log writes. Comfortably within CF's 3 MiB compressed limit. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Empirical MaintainX probe is deferred to operator** — headless cannot hit live MaintainX. Operator should run a one-off `wrangler dev` POST against `/claims-api/submit-claim` with `equipment_related=1` and `MAINTAINX_MODE=test` to confirm the WO is created, the response shape matches one of the three extractor candidates, and the assignee receipt lands in Josh's MaintainX inbox. **The brief's Definition-of-Done explicitly requires "assignee receipt confirmed by Josh (test mode) seeing one WO in his MaintainX inbox after the probe"** — this is the gate before flipping `MAINTAINX_MODE` to `"production"`. (b) **WO URL pattern is unverified** — `https://app.getmaintainx.com/workorders/{id}` is the brief's plausible default; if MaintainX uses a different path (e.g., `/dashboard/workorders/{id}` or `/work-orders/{id}` with a hyphen), edit the `href=` attr in `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/page.tsx`. (c) **R2 submission JSON written at step 4 doesn't pick up the late-arriving `maintainxWorkorderId`** — the field is set after the unconditional R2 save (which fires before writeClaimBatch). PA payload (step 6) DOES carry it. Re-saving the R2 JSON after MaintainX returns would close the gap if forensic recovery from R2 ever needs the WO ID; flagged here for completeness, no follow-up brief queued. (d) **PA Parse JSON schema needs operator-side update** to include `maintainxWorkorderId` (number, nullable) so the SharePoint flow can surface the WO link alongside Brief-41's `damageType`/`damageOther`. PA silently drops unknown fields, so the SharePoint write still succeeds; downstream PA expressions referencing `body('Parse_JSON')?['maintainxWorkorderId']` return null until the schema is rediscovered. (e) **Activity log entries appear as plain notes in the UI** — the manager detail page's RecentNotesBox + activity timeline render `note`-typed rows with no special styling for `[maintainx]` prefixes. If operations wants distinct visual treatment, that's a UI-only follow-up. (f) **`getMaintainXLocationId` doesn't cache** — the lookup is two REST round trips per equipment_related=1 submission. Equipment_related claims are a small fraction of submissions; caching would be premature. The brief explicitly OK'd "no caching for v1". (g) **Test WOs created during this session: zero** — operator probe will create at most one. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 25.681s (13 cache-miss as expected — types changes invalidate everything downstream); `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — `next build` compiled in 20.7s, all 12 routes generated; `/admin/damage/[id]` route bundle 3.1 kB / 108 kB First Load JS (zero delta vs. Brief 41 — server-rendered Field row, no new client island); `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run` succeeded — bundle 1679.53 KiB / 380.73 KiB gzip (+12.06 KiB / +2.67 KiB gzip vs. Brief 41 baseline). **Operator action items:** (1) `wrangler secret put MAINTAINX_API_KEY` if not already set on `splash-damage` (brief states the operator confirmed the secret on 2026-05-06 — verify with `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler secret list`). (2) Empirical probe: run `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler dev`, POST a synthetic claim with `equipment_related=1`, capture the MaintainX response body, confirm WO ID extraction works, confirm the WO appears in Josh's inbox under "test" mode. Document the actual response shape so Brief 43 can reuse the parser. (3) Verify the WO URL pattern by clicking a created WO link from the manager detail page. (4) Flip `MAINTAINX_MODE` to `"production"` at cutover (manual `wrangler.toml` edit + `wrangler deploy` OR CF Workers dashboard override). (5) Update the PA Parse JSON schema to include `maintainxWorkorderId` (number, nullable). (6) Brief 43 (GM-side modal) is now unblocked. | Claude Code (Brief 42 session) |
