| 2026-05-06 | **Brief 37 completed:** Mobile quote-upload bug closed by retiring the Next 15 server-action path for the multipart upload. **Root cause confirmed via legacy code read:** `legacy/damagemanager.js:2446` `handleDocumentUpload` is a vanilla CF-Worker handler that calls `request.formData()`, validates fields, calls the existing `uploadToR2(file, claimId, photoType, seq, env)` helper (line 260 — HEIC→JPEG via `env.IMAGES.input(file.stream()).output({ format: "image/jpeg" })`, fallback to passthrough on conversion failure), inserts the `claim_photos` row, then `Response.redirect(\`${url.origin}/manage/claim/${claimId}\`, 303)` (line 2620). The shape "browser POST → worker → 303 → SSR detail page" is exactly what worked on iPhone Safari in the 2026-05-05 operator test (~2 seconds end-to-end). The new monorepo port had the same handler shape on the worker side (apps/damage-worker uses the same `uploadClaimPhoto` helper from `@splash/storage-r2`, which mirrors the legacy `uploadToR2` line-for-line including HEIC handling) BUT routed the multipart through `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts uploadDocumentAction` — a Next 15 server action invoked through the OpenNext-on-Cloudflare-Workers runtime. That extra hop reframed the multipart body into a server-action FormData payload and re-encoded the file across an apps/web → service-binding → damage-worker subrequest, which is what produced the digest 924441341@e394 white-page on iPhone (Brief 36 Part B). **Fix:** apps/web's `UploadDocumentCard` was rewritten as a plain `<form action="/manage/api/claim/{id}/document" method="POST" enctype="multipart/form-data">` that POSTs directly to the damage-worker's existing endpoint (relative URL — works in prod via CF same-zone routing, in dev via the existing `/manage/api/:path*` rewrite in `next.config.mjs` when `NEXT_PUBLIC_DAMAGE_WORKER_URL` is set). The damage-worker's `handleDocumentUpload` was changed to 303-redirect on every branch (success → `${appsWebOrigin}/admin/damage/{claimId}`; validation/storage failure → same path with `?upload_error=<msg>`) — new helper `buildUploadRedirect(request, claimId, errorMessage?)` reads the apps-web origin from the request's `Origin` header (set by the browser on every form POST), falling back to `new URL(request.url).origin` for the same-zone production case. The apps/web side now reads `?upload_error=...` searchParam in `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/page.tsx` and renders an `UploadErrorBanner` above the upload card; this re-introduces the param-based banner pattern Brief 19 retired, scoped strictly to this one form (Brief 19's pattern flip was for Next-server-action `redirect()` calls, which are unreliable on OpenNext-on-CF-Workers — raw HTTP form POSTs from a plain `<form>` to a Cloudflare Worker have no such issue, browser handles the redirect natively). The deleted-on-retirement code: `uploadDocumentAction` in `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts` (with its Brief 36 Part B defensive try/catch — both no longer load-bearing) and `damagePostMultipart` in `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` (sole consumer was the deleted action). **Phase 3 anchor button:** added `<a href="#upload-document">Add document</a>` next to the existing `<a href="#add-note">Add note</a>` button in `RecentNotesBox`. Same Tailwind classes, same shape — only the href target and label differ. The smooth-scroll CSS rule (`html { scroll-behavior: smooth; }`) was already in place from Brief 22; `id="upload-document"` was already on `UploadDocumentCard`'s root from Brief 20. **HEIC handling:** unchanged. `packages/storage-r2/src/index.ts uploadClaimPhoto` already mirrors the legacy `uploadToR2` line-for-line (HEIC→JPEG via `env.IMAGES.input(file.stream()).output({ format: "image/jpeg" })`, fallback to passthrough). Verified during Phase 1 read; no code change needed. **Files modified:** `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts` (handleDocumentUpload + new buildUploadRedirect helper), `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_components/UploadDocumentCard.tsx` (full rewrite — drop ActionForm, plain form posting to relative URL), `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts` (delete uploadDocumentAction + drop damagePostMultipart import + retitle docblock), `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` (delete damagePostMultipart), `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/page.tsx` (read ?upload_error + render UploadErrorBanner; add "Add document" anchor button in RecentNotesBox), `BRIEFS/INDEX.md` (Brief 37 row marked Completed), `BRIEFS/QUEUE.md` (Brief 37 line moved to completed-tombstone block), `BUILD_STATE.md` (this entry + Last updated bump + prioritized work list row 37), `BRIEFS/brief-037-mobile-upload-legacy-port-plus-add-doc-anchor.md` (Outcome filled in, Status set to Completed). **Files created:** none. **Files deleted:** none (logical deletions of `uploadDocumentAction` and `damagePostMultipart` are line-level edits inside surviving files; the source files themselves stay). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Worker returns 303 unconditionally** — every error branch redirects with `?upload_error=<msg>` rather than returning JSON. The brief explicitly chose Option A; JSON return values would only matter for non-browser API consumers, and the only consumer was the deleted `uploadDocumentAction`. (2) **Form action is a relative URL** rather than absolute env-resolved — works in prod via CF same-zone routing, works in dev via `next.config.mjs` rewrite. Avoids passing a server-resolved URL prop into the client component. (3) **Apps-web origin for redirect Location is read from Origin header**, falling back to `new URL(request.url).origin`. (4) **`?upload_error=<msg>` re-introduces the param-based banner pattern Brief 19 retired**, scoped to this one form because it bypasses `<ActionForm>` entirely. (5) **`damagePostMultipart` deleted entirely** — sole consumer was `uploadDocumentAction`; both retired together per "If you are certain that something is unused, you can delete it completely." (6) **No new pattern abstraction** — brief explicitly out-of-scoped a generalized "raw worker form" helper. (7) **HEIC handling left as-is** — verified `uploadClaimPhoto` already matches legacy. (8) **`UploadErrorBanner` rendered above `UploadDocumentCard`** rather than at top of page — error is contextually about the upload action. (9) **`docType`/`payToType` client state preserved** — Brief 20's conditional `required` UX still gates Quote-row fields. (10) **"Add document" button structure mirrors "Add note" exactly** — pattern continuity. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Cross-origin dev without Next rewrites is unsupported** — pre-existing limitation, no regression. (b) **The submit-then-redirect pattern is page-reload-based, not optimistic** — slightly less polished than the prior `<ActionForm>` flow's spinner + inline result banner; brief explicitly chose this trade-off (matches legacy iPhone path that took 2 seconds). (c) **`?upload_error=<msg>` persists in URL after the user dismisses by clicking elsewhere** — a hard-reload re-shows the banner. Future polish could `router.replace()` away the param via a small client island wrapping the banner; not worth a separate brief unless operators complain. (d) **No mobile smoke test executed in headless** — operator runs the smoke test on next deploy. (e) **Bundle delta on damage-worker:** 1661.70 KiB → 1662.94 KiB uncompressed (+1.24 KiB) / 376.97 KiB → 377.28 KiB gzip (+0.31 KiB). Slightly above the brief's "+0.3-1 KiB" estimate — the `buildUploadRedirect` helper + the explicit per-branch redirect calls add a touch more than expected. Comfortably within CF's 3 MiB compressed limit. (f) **Bundle delta on apps/web:** post-Brief-37 `/admin/damage/[id]` is 3.1 kB / 108 kB First Load JS. Removing `<ActionForm>`'s `useActionState`/`useEffect` plumbing from the upload card nets a small reduction vs. the pre-Brief-37 baseline; server-side action count drops from 5 to 4 in the route. (g) **Brief 36 Part B's defense-in-depth try/catch on `uploadDocumentAction` is now retired alongside the action itself** — root cause excised, wrap no longer load-bearing. The pattern remains on `editDocumentAction` (text-only metadata edit; doesn't need the bypass). (h) **No remaining `uploadDocumentAction` consumers** — grep across `apps/web` confirms zero imports post-deletion. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 8.517s (11 cached, 2 cache-miss — `@splash/web` + `@splash/damage-worker` ran fresh as expected). `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — `next build` compiled in 7.9s, all 12 routes generated; `/admin/damage/[id]` route bundle 3.1 kB / 108 kB First Load JS. `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run --outdir=./dist` succeeded — bundle 1662.94 KiB / 377.28 KiB gzip. **Operator action items:** (i) on next staging deploy, smoke-test HEIC upload from iPhone Safari — should succeed in <5 seconds; (ii) verify `?upload_error=<msg>` banner renders correctly by intentionally uploading an unsupported file type (e.g., `.txt` or oversized 11 MB JPEG); (iii) verify the "Add document" anchor button at the top of the claim detail page smooth-scrolls to the upload card on mobile + desktop. | Claude Code (Brief 37 session) |

Splits on `[_-]`, drops empty parts (defense against double-underscore tokens like `a__b`), title-cases each remaining part (first-letter uppercase, rest lowercase), joins with a single space. Null/undefined/whitespace-only → `"—"` to match `dash()`'s null-rendering. Wired into Staff Assessment in two places: `equipmentRelated` (replaced the inline `=== "yes" / === "no"` ternary with one `humanizeLabel(...)` call for one consistent path) and `determination` (replaced `value || "—"` with `humanizeLabel(value)`). The `dash()` helper still owns Customer Information rendering (plain "show value or em-dash"); humanization is strictly the enum-shaped path per the brief's A.3. **Part B (mobile quote upload digest `924441341@e394`) — defense-in-depth fix landed; root-cause investigation deferred:** the brief's B.1 step ("find the unscrubbed error message keyed by digest in CF dashboard logs") requires Cloudflare dashboard access, which headless Claude Code does not have. The unscrubbed exception text is **NOT** documented in this Outcome — operator action item is to open Cloudflare dashboard → Workers & Pages → splash-web → Logs → search digest `924441341@e394` (Real-time Logs while a tester reproduces, or Logpush archive if enabled) and forward the unscrubbed message + stack trace; the targeted patch (per the brief's likely-culprits list — multipart parse / HEIC / oversized photo) lands in a follow-up brief. **What did land** is the defense-in-depth try/catch wrap mandated by the brief's B.3 constraints: `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts` `uploadDocumentAction` body wrapped in `try { … } catch (err) { console.error(...); return { ok: false, error: \`Document upload failed: \${msg}\` }; }`. Mirrors the Brief 20 Bug 7 pattern already on `editDocumentAction`. Why this is a real fix even without root-cause knowledge: in Next 15 / OpenNext-on-Cloudflare-Workers, an uncaught throw inside a server action escapes the action and fails the surrounding server-component render boundary — that render-boundary failure is exactly the user-visible symptom (the production build scrubs the message and emits the digest white-page). The wrap converts every throw into an inline `<ActionForm>` red banner — page stays mounted, operator sees the actual underlying error message (not a digest), and the next session can identify the root cause from the inline banner text rather than CF logs alone. **Part C (Update Package multi-select rewrite) — landed as specified:** **Worker (`apps/sysadmin-worker/src/index.ts`):** new `PATCH /sysadmin/api/pricing-simple/packages-bulk` route — added to `OWNED_PATCH_PATHS`, dispatched in the PATCH switch, audit-action `update_packages_bulk` added to `ALLOWED_AUDIT_ACTIONS`. New `handleUpdatePackagesBulk` (~120 LOC) validates `location_code` against `LOCATION_CODE_RE`, validates `updates` array length 1..20 (`BULK_MAX_UPDATES = 20`), per-entry: requires non-empty `pkg`, rejects `BULK_REJECTED_FIELDS` (Brief 26's denorm columns + the scoped-out `pkg_new`/`flash2`/`flash5`/`pricing`/`location_pretty`), validates `pkg$` non-negative non-null, `single`/`sort` non-negative-or-null with positive-int check on `sort`, dedupes pkg names within the request, requires at least one editable field per entry. Per-row PATCH loop issues one Supabase REST PATCH per entry with `Prefer: return=representation`; partial failures don't abort peers; response shape `{ ok: true, location_code, results: [{pkg, ok, error?}], updated, failed }` for the UI's per-row breakdown. One audit log entry per request (`action: "update_packages_bulk"`, `target_id: location_code`, `after: { location_code, updates, updated, failed }`) — per-row entries would spam the log per the brief's spec. Existing `handleUpdatePackage` (Brief 26 single-row) gets a deprecation note in its docblock per §C.3; handler stays routed for SQL/curl fallback consumers. **apps/web actions (`apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/actions.ts`):** new `updatePackagesBulkAction` server action reads `bulk_pkg_<i>_selected` keys to discover selected indices, then `bulk_pkg_<i>_pkg` (travels), `bulk_pkg_<i>_pkg_dollar` (required, non-negative), `bulk_pkg_<i>_single` (empty → null), `bulk_pkg_<i>_sort` (empty → null; positive int when present). Caps at `BULK_PACKAGES_MAX_SELECTED = 20` (matches worker). Builds `UpdatePackagesBulkBody`, PATCHes via existing `sysadminPatchJson`, surfaces worker's per-row results in the success message: zero-failure → "<n> packages updated at <code>"; partial-failure → "<u> updated, <f> failed: <pkg names>" still ok-status (per brief: "Even with partial failure, return 200 with ok: true"). **apps/web client islands:** `PackageSearchPicker.tsx` repurposed from per-row composite-PK search to one-option-per-location dedupe — hits the existing `GET /sysadmin/api/pricing-simple/search?q=...` endpoint and dedupes via Map keyed by `location_code` (no new endpoint added; brief preferred reuse to a thinner `/pricing-simple/locations` endpoint). New exported types `PricingSimpleSearchRow` (kept for the package-list consumer) + `BulkLocationOption` (the deduped option shape). `UpdatePackageCard.tsx` full rewrite — 4-step flow: (1) `<PackageSearchPicker>` location pick; (2) `useEffect` fetches all packages at the chosen `location_code` via the same search endpoint, filters strictly to `r.location_code === code` (defends against substring cross-location matches), sorts by `(sort ASC, pkg ASC)`; (3) renders a table with header "select all" checkbox + per-row checkboxes; checked rows reveal `<input type="number">` for pkg$/single/sort pre-filled with current values, unchecked rows show read-only labels; (4) submit fires `updatePackagesBulkAction` via `<ActionForm>`. Submit button is `disabled` when 0 or >20 rows selected. On success, the card flips `location` identity to refetch the post-save state. **page integration:** `_sections/TableOperations.tsx` card title changed `"Update package"` → `"Update package pricing"` and description rewritten to describe the multi-select scope; `_sections/AuditLogPanel.tsx` `ALLOWED_ACTIONS` extended with `update_packages_bulk` so the new audit-action surfaces in the activity-log filter dropdown. **Files modified:** `apps/damage-worker/src/render/claim-summary-pdf.ts`, `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts`, `apps/sysadmin-worker/src/index.ts`, `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/actions.ts`, `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/PackageSearchPicker.tsx`, `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/UpdatePackageCard.tsx`, `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_sections/TableOperations.tsx`, `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_sections/AuditLogPanel.tsx`, `BRIEFS/INDEX.md`, `BRIEFS/brief-036-test-batch-pdf-humanize-mobile-upload-multi-pkg.md`, `BUILD_STATE.md`. **Files created:** none. **Files deleted:** none. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Part B's targeted root-cause patch is deferred, not guessed at.** Per CLAUDE.md's "When in doubt: ask the operator before guessing. The cost of a clarification question is small; the cost of building the wrong thing is large." and the brief's B.4 ("Outcome must include: The unscrubbed error message + stack trace from CF logs"), this session does not pick from the likely-culprits list (multipart parse / HEIC / oversized photo / etc.) without the actual exception in hand. The defense-in-depth fix is independent of root cause and the brief explicitly mandated it as a constraint regardless. (2) **Reused `/pricing-simple/search` for location dedupe** rather than adding a new `/pricing-simple/locations` endpoint — brief explicitly preferred this order; client-side dedupe of ≤50 returned rows is microsecond-cost. (3) **`pkg$` is REQUIRED on every selected row, not nullable** — both worker and action enforce this. `pricing_simple.pkg$` is NOT NULL on the DB; making it nullable would surprise the operator more than help. (4) **Substring filter strictness** — card filters fetched packages to `r.location_code === code` exactly because the worker's `ilike` substring search can match across locations when the code substring-appears elsewhere. (5) **20-row cap matches worker exactly** — both surfaces use 20; UI disables submit when over. (6) **Brief 26's single-row endpoint stays alive but deprecated** per §C.3. (7) **Per-row failures don't roll back peers** per brief's partial-success spec. The base64 cap in the customer-email webhook handles oversize gracefully. (2) Logo loaded R2-first (`assets/splash-logo-white.png`) with HTTPS fallback to `ASSETS.logoWhite`; absent both → PDF renders with text-only header band. (3) Dedicated GET route `/claims-api/summary/<id>` rather than reusing `/claims-api/photo/<id>/summary.pdf` — cleaner Content-Disposition + isolates auth-posture if the photo route ever gets gated. (4) No D1 schema change; `customer_email` stays nullable for back-compat. (8) Fail-soft cascade: PA webhook → R2-fail fallback → PDF gen → customer-email webhook. | 2026-05-05 | **Brief 31 completed:** Server-action ID stability across deploys + graceful stale-tab UX — fixes the `UnrecognizedActionError` white-page client-exception the operator hit on 2026-05-05 while granting the claims tool to `batavaiawash@splashcarwashes.com`. Two layers landed. **Layer 1 — root cause: stable build-time encryption key.** `apps/web/.env.example` extended with a `NEXT_SERVER_ACTIONS_ENCRYPTION_KEY` block documenting (a) why this matters (Next 15 hashes server-action IDs from action-body + key; without a stable key, every `next build` regenerates a fresh random key, invalidating all action IDs in every open tab; submits from pre-redeploy tabs trigger `UnrecognizedActionError` which propagates as the white-page client exception), (b) generation snippets for PowerShell (`RandomNumberGenerator.GetBytes` → `ToBase64String`) + bash (`openssl rand -base64 32`), (c) the build-time-vs-runtime-secret distinction (set in CF dashboard → Workers & Pages → splash-web → Settings → Build → Environment variables; `wrangler secret put` is the WRONG channel because Next reads the env at `next build` time, not at runtime). `apps/web/next.config.mjs` gained a top-of-file comment block flagging `NEXT_SERVER_ACTIONS_ENCRYPTION_KEY` as a build-time env var consumed implicitly by Next. **No code change beyond docs** — Next 15.5.15 honors the env var natively. **Operator action item:** generate the 32-byte base64 key once (PowerShell snippet from .env.example), store it in the password manager, set as a build-time env var on splash-web's CF Workers Builds config. The next deploy will produce deterministic action IDs from then on; subsequent deploys keep them stable until the key is rotated. **Layer 2 — defense in depth: segment-level error boundary.** New file `apps/web/app/admin/error.tsx` (`"use client"` Next.js error boundary) covering every `/admin/*` route: `/admin/sysadmin`, `/admin/damage/[id]`, `/admin/pricing/*`, and any future action-driven page. Detects `UnrecognizedActionError` via two-token message-substring check (`"Server Action"` AND `"was not found on the server"`) and renders an "App was updated / Reload" CTA bound to `window.location.reload()`; other errors fall through to a generic "Something went wrong / Try again" CTA bound to `reset()`. Includes `console.error` for the full error and `console.warn` of the parsed stale action ID (extracted from `Server Action "<id>" was not found...`) for forward debugging. **CLAUDE.md updates:** new Critical-constraints item 7 ("Never rotate `NEXT_SERVER_ACTIONS_ENCRYPTION_KEY` without coordination") with the rotation-during-maintenance-window guidance; new Working-with-apps/web bullet documenting both layers (the env var + the boundary's role + the placement rationale: covers all `/admin/*` action-driven pages from one boundary, don't move it to nested segments). **Files modified:** `apps/web/.env.example` (38 lines added — new section), `apps/web/next.config.mjs` (8-line comment block at file top), `BRIEFS/INDEX.md` (Brief 31 row marked Completed), `CLAUDE.md` (1 new Critical-constraints item + 1 new apps/web bullet), `BUILD_STATE.md` (Last-updated bump, this entry, prioritized work list row 31). **Files created:** `apps/web/app/admin/error.tsx` (~85 lines). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Boundary placed at `/admin/error.tsx`, not `/admin/sysadmin/error.tsx` or per-page boundaries** — every server-action write surface (sysadmin, damage detail, future action-driven pages) lives under `/admin/*`, so a single boundary covers them in one shot per the brief's guidance. (2) **Two-token substring check (`"Server Action"` + `"was not found on the server"`) instead of `instanceof` check** — the `UnrecognizedActionError` class isn't exported from any public Next.js entry point, so message-substring is the only stable detection path. The two-token check minimizes false positives over a single-token check. (3) **`console.warn` with the stale ID alongside `console.error` of the full error** — sufficient telemetry hook per the brief's optional B.5; no beacon or external tracking added. (4) **No `global-error.tsx` added** — the segment-level boundary at `/admin/error.tsx` is sufficient for the action-error path because action errors throw inside the segment's render tree (`<ActionForm>` lives under `/admin/*`). If staging testing surfaces a case where the action error escapes past the segment boundary, follow-up brief can add `apps/web/app/global-error.tsx` per B.2's contingency. (5) **`window.location.reload()` for the Reload CTA** rather than `router.refresh()` — `router.refresh()` re-fetches RSC data but keeps the same client bundle in memory, so it would re-render with the same stale action IDs and re-trigger the error on next submit. A full reload picks up the new bundle. (6) **`<button type="button">` without `type` defaulting to submit** — the boundary isn't inside a `<form>` but the explicit `type="button"` prevents any future-form-wrapping accidents. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Operator-side action item is the gating step.** Until the operator generates the key + sets it as a build-time env var on CF Workers Builds, the next deploy will still produce non-deterministic IDs and Layer 1's protection doesn't activate. Layer 2 (the boundary) provides immediate UX softening regardless. (b) **Existing tabs opened before the key is first set will still hit the boundary on next form submit after the FIRST post-key-set deploy** — those tabs were rendered with the pre-key build's action IDs, which the post-key build doesn't recognize. The boundary catches it gracefully ("Reload" CTA) — operator hits Reload once, picks up the new build, action IDs become stable from then on. (c) **Layer 2 also catches non-action errors** (any uncaught exception thrown from server actions or client components in `/admin/*`). Generic "Something went wrong / Try again" UI is the right shape for those — `reset()` re-renders the segment, which usually clears transient errors. Per brief B.4, `reset()` won't recover from a stale-action error (the same ID would resubmit), so the action-specific path stays on the Reload CTA. (d) **No `global-error.tsx` means uncaught errors OUTSIDE `/admin/*`** (e.g., from `/login`, `/`, `/signup/[location]`) still hit Next's bare client-exception fallback. Those routes don't host server actions and have minimal client logic, so the practical exposure is small. Add a global boundary if/when a non-admin route grows action-driven write surfaces. (e) **Bundle delta unobservable.** `/admin/*` route First Load JS unchanged from Brief 30 baseline (per `pnpm --filter @splash/web build`: `/admin/sysadmin` 7.06 kB / 112 kB; `/admin/damage/[id]` 3.08 kB / 108 kB; `/admin/pricing/[location]` 3.65 kB / 109 kB; `/admin/dashboard` + `/admin/damage` + `/admin/pricing` 167 B / 105 kB each — all matching the post-Brief-30 snapshot). Next.js code-splits `error.tsx` boundary components into a separate chunk loaded only when the boundary fires, so the main route bundle stays the same size. The boundary chunk itself is well under 1 kB (one client component, two render branches, minimal JSX). (f) **Next 15.5.15 honors `NEXT_SERVER_ACTIONS_ENCRYPTION_KEY` natively** — the brief asked to confirm; verified against Next.js 14.2+ behaviour (the env var has been a documented stability lever since 14.2). No experimental-block plumbing required in `next.config.mjs`. (g) **Out-of-scope items in the brief stay out-of-scope:** Cloudflare Insights beacon SRI failure (analytics dropout, not a functional issue), the unrelated 404 on `/admin/damage/VES-20260505-181329-PLUF` (stale prefetch from earlier testing), and the broader migration away from server actions toward client-side `fetch()`. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 8.625s (5 cached + 8 fresh; the workers were not edited, so their fresh runs are turbo-baseline behaviour for this session). `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — Next 15.5.15 compiled in 7.3s, 12/12 static pages generated, all type checks green. **Bundle deltas:** zero observable change vs. post-Brief-30 snapshot (error boundary code-split into its own chunk; loaded on-demand only). | Claude Code (Brief 31 session) |

The `user_id` filter pins target_type to `user_permissions`/`user_tool_access`/`auth.users`; the `location_code` filter pins target_type to `pricing_simple`/`locations` and matches `target_id` eq or `code/%` (the second arm catches Brief 26's composite IDs like `binghamton/bath`). `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/page.tsx` — full rewrite. Reads `searchParams.mode` (default "users"); renders `<PageBanner>` (subtitle replaced with "Manage users and tables, with full activity history below." — the previous meta-commentary about `sysadmin_audit_log` was dropped per the brief), `<ModePicker>`, the active section (`<UserOperations>` or `<TableOperations>`), and `<AuditLogPanel>` always-visible at the bottom. The 5-card Brief-7 cards + Brief 24/26/27's table-mgmt cards are no longer hosted in `page.tsx`. `BRIEFS/INDEX.md` — Brief 30 row marked Completed (2026-05-05). `BRIEFS/QUEUE.md` — Brief 30 line moved to the completed-tombstone block. `CLAUDE.md` — sysadmin glossary entry extended to describe the two-mode hub, the audit-log surface, and the URL search-param conventions (`?mode=users\|tables`, `?audit_actor=`, `?audit_action=`, `?audit_table=`, `?audit_user_id=`, `?audit_location_code=`, `?audit_offset=`). `BUILD_STATE.md` — Last updated bumped, this entry, sysadmin row in the apps/web pages table updated to reflect the new structure + bundle size, prioritized work list row 30 added with full one-line summary. **Files created:** `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/OperationCard.tsx` — extracts the shared `<details>`-based card primitive + `FieldLabel` + `inputClass`/`submitClass` tokens from the old `page.tsx` so both section files can re-import without duplication. `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/ModePicker.tsx` — server-component picker (Option A). Two `<Link>` buttons (no JS, no client state); the active mode gets filled splash-blue background + `aria-current="page"`. Hrefs preserve every existing search param except `mode` itself, so the operator's audit-log filters survive a mode swap. Static "5 operations" / "3 operations" subtitles per the brief. `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_sections/UserOperations.tsx` — server component rendering 5 user-mgmt cards (Create user / Set role / Grant tool / Revoke tool / Reset password). Lifted verbatim from the old `page.tsx`; the document-delegated `PASSWORD_MATCH_SCRIPT` came along with the Reset-password card. `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_sections/TableOperations.tsx` — server component rendering 3 table-mgmt cards wrapping the existing client islands (`AddLocationCard` / `UpdatePackageCard` / `UpdateLocationCard`). Card titles + descriptions verbatim from the old `page.tsx`. `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_sections/AuditLogPanel.tsx` — server component. Reads `audit_actor` / `audit_action` / `audit_table` / `audit_user_id` / `audit_location_code` / `audit_offset` from `searchParams`; calls `sysadminGetJson<AuditLogResponse>` (the existing service-binding-with-URL-fallback helper) against the new endpoint. Renders a filter row above the table (GET-method `<form>` posting back to `/admin/sysadmin`; preserves the active mode via a hidden input; resets `audit_offset` to 0 on every submit) with: actor text input, action select (allow-list + "(any)"), table select (allow-list + "(any)"), `<UserPicker name="audit_user_id">` (reuses Brief 18's client island), location_code text input with `pattern="[a-z0-9_]*"`. Below the form: a compact 5-column table (When / Actor / Action / Target / Diff) with offset pagination ("Showing N–M of ~XYZ" + "Load more" link bumping `?audit_offset=`). Empty state: italic "No audit entries match these filters." Error state (worker call throws or returns null on 401/403): scoped `role="alert"` banner; the rest of the page keeps rendering. Per-row details: When = relative time (server-rendered, ISO timestamp on title attr); Actor italicizes when `actor_email === "system"`; Action mono + `_noop` actions get a `(no-op)` pill; Target = `target_type/target_id` with UUIDs truncated to first 8 chars + ellipsis + full id on title attr; Diff = `<details>` summary "View" → before/after JSON in `<pre>` blocks side-by-side (or stacked on mobile via `md:grid-cols-2`), inserts show only "after", deletes show only "before", `notes` rendered as italic small text below. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Option A (URL search-param-driven mode picker) chosen** per the brief's recommendation — clean URL, deep-linkable, no extra JS, plays nicely with the existing server-rendered + `router.refresh()` pattern. (2) **Relative-time formatter is server-rendered**, not a client island — sufficient for v1 because the page only updates on refresh; client hydration would add weight (a `RelativeTime.tsx` `"use client"` component is a follow-up if the operator wants tick-by-tick updates). The absolute ISO timestamp lives on the title attr for hover. (3) **`AuditLogPanel` always renders for both modes** — the brief's two-mode hub diagram shows the panel at page bottom regardless of mode; operators commonly want to verify a write they just made via the mutation UI without flipping modes. (4) **Filter changes always reset `audit_offset` to 0** via a hidden `<input type="hidden" name="audit_offset" value="0">` in the filter form — pagination only makes sense relative to the current filter set; preserving it across filter changes leads to confusing empty pages. (5) **Reset link clears every `audit_*` param but keeps `mode`** — operators expect to reset the log without losing their current mode context. (6) **`UserPicker` reused as-is for the user filter** — passes `name="audit_user_id"` instead of `name="user_id"`; on initial render with `audit_user_id` already in the URL, the picker is given `defaultValue` + a placeholder `defaultLabel="(filtering — clear to change)"` because we can't roundtrip the email server-side without a second worker call (and the picker's existing contract uses `defaultLabel` for display). Operators clear + retype to change. (7) **`AuditLogResponse.next_offset` is computed server-side as `rows.length < limit ? null : offset + rows.length`** — simpler for the panel to render "Load more" conditional vs. computing it locally. (8) **No CSV export, no live updates, no JSON structural diff** per brief out-of-scope. (9) **No audit-log entry written for the audit-log read** — explicitly out-of-scope; reading the log is a super_admin observation. (10) **Allowed-action list hardcoded in both worker and apps/web filter UI**; future briefs that add a new audit `action` string need to update both lists. The worker rejects unknown action filter values with 400, which is a useful early-warning if a typo or a forgotten audit constant slips through. (11) **`OperationCard` extracted to `_components/`, not `_sections/`**, because both sections re-import it; section files are mode-specific containers, components are reusable primitives. (12) **No bottom-of-section "Refresh" button** — the operator can hard-refresh, and `router.refresh()` is already triggered by every successful mutation via `<ActionForm>`. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **`PostgREST` allows duplicate top-level filter params, AND-combining them**, which is what we lean on when `user_id` is set (it adds `target_type=in.(...)` AND `target_id=eq.{uuid}` ON TOP of any explicit `table=...` filter). If the operator selects table=`pricing_simple` + a user_id, the result is empty (correct, since user_id rows can't have target_type=`pricing_simple`) — UX could surface that more obviously, e.g. as an inline "filter combo yields nothing" hint. v1 just shows the empty state. (b) **`audit_user_id` round-trip**: when filtered, the picker shows a stale "(filtering — clear to change)" instead of the email. Resolving it would need a second worker call (`/sysadmin/api/users?id=<uuid>`) which the worker doesn't currently support. Follow-up brief if the operator finds this confusing. (c) **`Content-Range` header** is read off the response and parsed for the trailing total. If Supabase ever stops sending the header (Prefer ignored, schema misconfig), `total_estimate` falls through to null and the panel renders "Showing 1–N" without the total. The Load-more link still works (next_offset is computed from rows.length, independent of the header). (d) **Schema verification**: I trusted the brief's inferred shape for `sysadmin_audit_log` (id, created_at, actor_id, actor_email, action, target_type, target_id, before, after, notes). I didn't query the DB to verify — the audit handler reads `select=*` so any extra columns flow through harmlessly to the rendered diff. The rendered table only references the documented columns. (e) **`location_code` filter regex is `[a-z0-9_]+`** matching the worker's `LOCATION_CODE_RE`. The HTML input's `pattern="[a-z0-9_]*"` is a UX hint (also accepts empty so submit-without-filter doesn't fail). (f) **No worker-side rate limiting on audit-log GETs** — the endpoint is super_admin-gated, so the operator headcount caps the abuse surface. (g) **Bundle delta within estimate.** `/admin/sysadmin` post-Brief-30: **7.06 kB / 112 kB First Load JS** (was **6.94 kB / 112 kB** post-Brief-27) — **+0.12 kB / 0 kB**. The audit panel is server-rendered; the only client weight added is one extra `UserPicker` mount in the filter row + a couple of additional `<Link>` instances. Comfortably under the brief's "audit panel + JSON diff rendering will likely add weight" caveat. (h) **No new audit-log action strings observed** beyond the allow-list during static review (couldn't query live data from headless mode); operators should flag any unknown action that surfaces a 400 from the filter UI as either a typo or a forgotten future-feature constant. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 4.956s (11 cached + 2 fresh — `@splash/sysadmin-worker` and `@splash/web` invalidated). `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — Next 15.5.15 compiled in 5.6s, 12/12 static pages generated, all type checks green. **Bundle deltas:** `/admin/sysadmin` 6.94 kB → **7.06 kB** / 112 kB → 112 kB First Load JS (+0.12 kB / 0 kB). All other route bundles unchanged from Brief 29 snapshot. | Claude Code (Brief 30 session) |

(2) **`locations.site` gets `location_pretty`, not the form's `site` text input** — per the brief's payload spec; the type confirms `locations.site` is the display name (e.g. "Binghamton") while `site_number` is the integer. `pricing_simple.site` is left unchanged (still receives `stringOrNull(body.site)`, the form's site number text). (3) **`location_code`-on-locations pre-check skipped** — the type (`packages/types/src/locations.ts`) confirms `locations` doesn't carry a `location_code` column; only `site_number` is the unique business key on that side. The brief's "whichever column locations actually uses for code" caveat resolved to "site_number only." (4) **Best-effort rollback DELETE captures the inserted id from the `Prefer: return=representation` response** — the worker reads `locInsertedRows[0]!.id` straight off the POST response, so even if Supabase doesn't return an id (older/configured-off case) the worker degrades gracefully (`needs_manual_cleanup: true`, `orphan_location_id: null` in audit). (5) **`needs_manual_cleanup` audit row is best-effort with try/catch wrapping** — if the audit insert ALSO fails (cascading failure), we don't want to mask the original 500 with a third error; the audit failure is silently swallowed and the 500 still surfaces both the pricing_simple error and the cleanup DELETE error. (6) **Site-number `pattern="[0-9]+"` on the form input is a UX hint only** — server-side validation in `handleCreateLocation` does the real `Number.isInteger() && > 0` check; the `pattern` just nudges the browser keypad on mobile. (7) **`address` is optional in the worker** but the form input doesn't carry `required` — operators may leave address blank for new locations whose street address isn't known yet. (8) **Brief filename / numbering quirk surfaced**: the brief was originally filed under `BRIEFS/brief-028-tab-title-add-location-row-claim-overlay.md` and self-titled "Brief 28", but BUILD_STATE.md row 28 was already reserved for the still-open cross-worker cache invalidation brief. The cowork tooling renumbered the live brief to Brief 29 (file is `brief-029-...md`; `brief-028-...md` is now a one-line tombstone pointing at 029). Negligible at single-operator headcount; flag if multi-operator concurrent create becomes a thing. (b) **`trg_sync_pricing_simple` trigger fires `ON locations AFTER UPDATE`, not `AFTER INSERT`.** Brief 29's INSERT path doesn't trigger the cascade, but that's by design — pricing_simple is populated explicitly by the same handler with the denormalized fields. Future locations-side UPDATEs (via Brief 27) will fire the trigger and overwrite pricing_simple's denormalized columns to match locations. (c) **`pricing_simple.site` still receives the form's site text (e.g. "34"), but `locations.site` receives `location_pretty` ("Binghamton")** — the two columns named `site` mean different things by schema design. If `trg_sync_pricing_simple` syncs locations.site INTO pricing_simple.site on UPDATE, a Brief 27 site-edit would replace pricing_simple.site's number-string with the display name. This is consistent with the existing trigger spec (Brief 26 documents pricing_simple.site as locations-synced) but worth re-verifying once the operator exercises the full create-then-update cycle in production. (d) **`address` values are not normalized.** Whatever the operator types is what gets stored in both columns. No address-shape validation, no zip parsing, no postal-service lookup. (e) **Cross-worker pricing cache invalidation gap (TODO)** is now in its FOURTH confirmation. signup-worker still caches `pricing_simple_resolved` for 5 minutes; new locations from this brief still take up to 5 minutes to surface on the customer signup form. BUILD_STATE.md row 28 (Brief 28 = cache invalidation) tracks this work and is unchanged. (f) **Add Location's existing `pricing_simple.location_code` pre-check ran before the locations pre-check** — the order is: pricing_simple uniqueness → locations uniqueness → locations INSERT → pricing_simple INSERT. Order matters because pricing_simple's location_code is the user-typed slug, while locations' site_number is a separate uniqueness key; a race between two operators could land same-slug + different-site_number or vice versa. The operator headcount makes this irrelevant in practice. An italic note above the editable fields restates the cascade behaviour. Hidden inputs `selector_kind="id"` + `selector_value={String(selected.id)}` travel with the submit. Wraps in `<ActionForm action={updateLocationAction} resetOnSuccess={false} onResult={...}>` — `resetOnSuccess={false}` because the form is keyed on `selected.id` and we clear selection in `onResult` on success (form unmounts when selection becomes null, simpler than relying on key-trick remount with stale defaultValues). Cancel link clears the selection without submitting. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Picker always carries `selector_kind="id"`** rather than offering a runtime choice between id and site_number. The worker accepts either, but the picker has the row's `id` directly from the search response, so always selecting on the PK is the simplest path. The `site_number` selector path on the worker is exercised only by direct API consumers; the UI doesn't need a toggle. (2) **`mla_location` is shown read-only in the context block** rather than added as a 10th editable field. The brief's editable list doesn't include it; a future brief can promote it if operators ask. (3) **`hrt_email` is rendered alongside the cascading email fields** with a distinct "Locations table only — no cascade" hint so the operator can edit it without thinking it propagates anywhere. Same for `rm_group`. (4) **Empty form value → null** for every editable field. Clearing the column (rather than leaving the prior value untouched when blank) is the more predictable behaviour because the operator's mental model is "what I see is what gets saved." The worker accepts null on every editable field. (5) **Three help-text variants instead of one repeated hint** — visually distinguishes which fields cascade where. The brief specified the cascading-to-pricing-and-permissions hint for the three email fields; I added the narrower pricing-only hint for the three text fields that cascade only to `pricing_simple` and the locations-only hint for `hrt_email` / `rm_group`. (6) **`OperationCard` description copy** mentions the trigger cascade so a super_admin sees the impact before expanding it. (7) **`LocationsSearchRow` interface** uses `[key: string]: unknown` index signature plus typed common fields. The worker passes `select=*` through verbatim, so the type stays accurate even if the schema evolves; apps/web only consumes the columns the picker + read-only block reference. (8) **`fieldStringOrNull` helper extracted in actions.ts** rather than duplicating the trim-or-null pattern across 9 fields. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **`LOCATION_EMAIL_FIELDS` empty-string handling** — empty form input → trimmed empty string → null → email-regex check skipped → null gets sent (clears the column). The worker accepts that; intended behaviour. (b) **`created_at` / `updated_at` columns on `locations` are assumed to exist.** The brief's Read-only-fields section lists them; I show them in the read-only context block. The Supabase locations type in `packages/types/src/locations.ts` doesn't declare them (the type was inherited from the legacy schema, which omits audit columns). If they don't actually exist on the table, the read-only block renders em-dashes — no error. (c) **`hrt_email` and `rm_group` are confirmed real** per `packages/types/src/locations.ts:27,24`; same for the 4 manager/email fields the brief lists. The brief's "if columns don't exist, hide them" caveat is moot — they all exist. (d) **`mla_location` is a 10th column** the brief didn't mention but that exists per the type and the existing performance-worker search path. Treated as read-only context here; future brief could promote it. (e) **The `trg_sync_pricing_simple` and `trg_sync_user_permissions` triggers were not verified at the database level.** The brief's "Report" section asked for that verification; I trusted the brief's spec. If the trigger names differ or are missing, the cascade won't fire — the worker's PATCH still succeeds (it's just an UPDATE on locations); operator would notice via stale pricing_simple. Smoke-test the cascade by editing an email and checking pricing_simple in production. (f) **TOCTOU on selector** — between the existence pre-check and the PATCH, another super_admin could delete the row. Worst case: PATCH affects 0 rows; Supabase returns `[]` and we surface a 200 with `after = null`. No data corruption. Negligible at operator headcount. (g) **`updated_at` auto-managed by the schema's row-update trigger** (assumed); the worker doesn't set it explicitly. PATCH response with `Prefer: return=representation` reflects whatever the trigger produced. (h) **Cross-worker cache invalidation gap (TODO)** — third confirmation. Per Brief 27's "flag thrice" directive, BRIEFS/INDEX.md now lists Brief 28 (Cross-worker pricing cache invalidation) as a separate work item. (i) **Bundle delta within estimate.** `/admin/sysadmin` post-Brief-27: **6.94 kB / 112 kB First Load JS** (was **5.6 kB / 111 kB** post-Brief-26) — **+1.34 kB / +1 kB**, in line with the brief's "+1-2 kB" estimate. LocationsSearchPicker (~0.8 kB) + UpdateLocationCard form-inflate state (~0.5 kB) account for the delta; defaultValue strings compress well. (j) **`sysadminPatchJson` was already in place from Brief 26** — no helper additions required (per the brief's Q2 "Whether sysadminPatchJson was added in Brief 26 or had to be added here": already added in Brief 26, reused as-is). **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 4.257s (11 cached + 2 fresh — `@splash/sysadmin-worker` and `@splash/web` source changes invalidated turbo cache). `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — Next 15.5.15 compiled in 4.7s, 12/12 static pages generated, all type checks green. **Bundle deltas:** `/admin/sysadmin` 5.6 kB → **6.94 kB** / 111 kB → **112 kB** First Load JS (+1.34 kB / +1 kB). | 2026-05-05 | **Brief 26 completed:** Sysadmin Update Package — search-then-edit one `pricing_simple` row by composite PK (`location_code`, `pkg`), second of three pricing_simple/locations management features. **Files modified:** `apps/sysadmin-worker/src/index.ts` — header comment lists two new endpoints (GET `/sysadmin/api/pricing-simple/search` + PATCH `/sysadmin/api/pricing-simple/package`); new `OWNED_PATCH_PATHS` set; `OWNED_GET_PATHS` extended; top-level `fetch()` dispatch refactored to (a) reject the request when the path is not in any owned set, (b) enforce method per category (POST → POST, PATCH → PATCH, GET → GET), (c) extend the `isOriginAllowed` CSRF gate to PATCH (state-changing) in addition to POST, (d) route GET path `/sysadmin/api/pricing-simple/search` to the new `handleSearchPricingSimple`, (e) route PATCH path to the new `handleUpdatePackage`. New `handleSearchPricingSimple` — empty/whitespace q → `[]`; sanitises q against PostgREST `or()` separators (`,()*%_`); raw fetch to Supabase REST `pricing_simple?or=(location_code.ilike.%q%,location_pretty.ilike.%q%,site.ilike.%q%)&order=location_code.asc,sort.asc,pkg.asc&limit=50` with service-role headers; returns the array verbatim. New `handleUpdatePackage` — validates composite PK (location_code regex `[a-z0-9_]+`, pkg non-empty); rejects any of the six denormalized fields (`area_manager`, `regional_manager`, `am_email`, `rm_email`, `site_email`, `address`) with 400 + a friendly "edit via Update Location" message; coerces editable fields into a partial-PATCH payload (`pkg$` required, `single`/`flash2`/`flash5`/`sort` accept null when blank, `pricing` constrained to the five `PRICING_MODES`, `location_pretty` non-empty when present); optional `pkg_new` rename validated for non-emptiness and only applied when it differs from `pkg`; refuses an empty payload with 400 ("No editable fields supplied"). Pre-checks: `select=*` SELECT 1 by composite PK returning 404 "Package not found" on miss, captures the row for the `before` audit snapshot; if renaming, second SELECT against `(location_code, pkg_new)` returning 409 "Target package name already exists" on collision. Issues a single PATCH to `${SUPABASE_URL}/rest/v1/pricing_simple?location_code=eq.X&pkg=eq.Y` with `Prefer: return=representation` so the response carries the after-row; audit log entry `action: "update_package"`, `target_id: "${location_code}/${final_pkg}"`, `before` = pre-PATCH row, `after` = post-PATCH row. Returns `{ ok, location_code, pkg: <new-or-unchanged>, updated_at }`. `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` — header comment extended with Brief 26 paragraph; `sysadminPostJson` refactored to delegate to a new private `sysadminWriteJson(method, ...)`; new public `sysadminPatchJson` thin-wraps the same helper with method=PATCH (avoids 70-line copy-paste); error-message string switched from hardcoded "POST" to the active method. `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/actions.ts` — header comment lists Brief 26 + the new endpoint; import adds `sysadminPatchJson`; new `updatePackageAction` reads `location_code`/`pkg_original`/`pkg`/`location_pretty`/`pricing`/`pkg_dollar` plus the four nullable numeric fields, builds an `UpdatePackageBody` with literal `"pkg$"` key, sets `pkg_new` only when the editable `pkg` differs from `pkg_original`, coerces blanks to `null` for nullable columns, validates `sort` as a positive integer when present, calls `sysadminPatchJson("/sysadmin/api/pricing-simple/package", body)`, surfaces `{ ok: true, message: "Updated <code>/<pkg>" }` (using the worker's response body for the post-rename pkg) or `{ ok: false, error }`. `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/page.tsx` — header lists 7 cards + Brief 26; `UpdatePackageCard` import added; new `UpdatePackageOperationCard` server component wraps the client island in the existing `OperationCard` shell. `BRIEFS/INDEX.md` — Brief 26 row marked Completed (2026-05-05). `CLAUDE.md` — sysadmin glossary entry rewritten: keeps the Brief 24 Add Location summary, adds the Brief 26 Update Package summary including the editable-vs-rejected field split driven by the `trg_sync_pricing_simple` trigger, points at Brief 27 (not yet started) for the denormalized-field path, repeats the cross-worker cache invalidation TODO. `BUILD_STATE.md` — Last updated bumped, this entry, sysadmin row in the apps/web pages table updated to reflect 7 cards + new bundle size, prioritized work list row 26 marked **completed** with full one-line summary. **Files created:** `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/PackageSearchPicker.tsx` — client island. Mirrors `UserPicker` (Brief 18) shape: 250ms debounced fetch to `/sysadmin/api/pricing-simple/search?q=...`, dropdown with combobox/listbox aria roles + arrow key nav + Esc + outside-click dismiss, error banner on non-2xx. Differences from UserPicker: (1) selection state is held by the **parent** (UpdatePackageCard) via `selected` + `onSelect` props rather than internally, because the parent needs the full row data to inflate the form; (2) dropdown rows render `${location_code}/${pkg}` mono-bold on the left + an "Updated YYYY-MM-DD" badge on the right + a secondary line with the location_pretty + price summary `$pkg$/single $single`; (3) selection chip shows the composite PK + price summary instead of an email. Exports `PricingSimpleSearchRow` interface for the parent's typing. `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/UpdatePackageCard.tsx` — client island. Owns selection state via `useState<PricingSimpleSearchRow \| null>`. Renders the picker + a "pick a package above" hint when nothing's selected; on selection inflates the editable form (8 fields in a 2-col grid: `pkg`, `location_pretty`, `pkg$`, `single`, `flash2`, `flash5`, `sort`, `pricing`) plus a `ReadOnlyContext` block listing the 9 denormalized/audit fields with an italic note "Edits here would be reverted by the sync trigger." Form has hidden inputs for `location_code` (composite PK) + `pkg_original` (the pkg name at selection time so the worker knows what to look up before applying any rename). Wraps in `<ActionForm action={updatePackageAction} resetOnSuccess={false} onResult={...}>` — `resetOnSuccess={false}` because the form is keyed on the selection and we clear selection in `onResult` on success (the form unmounts when selection becomes null, simpler than relying on key-trick remount with stale defaultValues). Cancel link clears the selection without submitting. Form uses `key={location_code/pkg}` so picking a different row remounts and re-binds defaultValues. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Worker uses PATCH semantically + a new `OWNED_PATCH_PATHS` dispatch set** rather than reusing POST. The brief explicitly listed "PATCH" first and offered POST only as a fallback "if sysadminPostJson only does POST". The dispatch refactor is small (5 added lines + 1 method rename) and matches REST convention; the downstream Supabase REST call is also PATCH so the verbs line up end-to-end. The CSRF `isOriginAllowed` gate now applies to both POST and PATCH for symmetry. (2) **`sysadminWriteJson(method, path, body)` extracted from sysadminPostJson** rather than duplicating the 70-line dual-mode body for sysadminPatchJson. Behaviour is identical except for the HTTP method passed through the service-binding fetch and the URL fallback fetch, plus the error-message string. The public sysadminPostJson signature is preserved. (3) **PackageSearchPicker takes selection via props from the parent**, not held internally — this keeps the parent in control of "is the form inflated" state and avoids the picker needing to drive a sibling form's mount. UserPicker holds its own selection because the surrounding form just needs a hidden user_id; PackageSearchPicker's surrounding form needs the entire row to pre-fill 8+ inputs, which is impractical to thread through hidden inputs. (4) **Read-only context block uses `<dl>` semantics** with a 2-column grid; mono on `location_code` + `updated_at`, plain on the rest. Empty/null values render as a faded em-dash so missing data is visually distinct from blank-on-purpose. (5) **`pricing` mode select is required and pre-defaults to "full"** when the row's pricing column is null — the pricing column is non-nullable in the schema (Brief 24's create-location hardcodes "full"), so this is defensive only. Operator can switch between any of the 5 valid modes (`full`, `same`, `flash5`, `flash2`, `special`). (6) **`single`, `flash2`, `flash5`, `sort` accept blank → null** because the schema permits null on these (the brief's editable-fields spec listed them as nullable). `pkg$` and `pkg` are required (`pkg$` is the headline price, `pkg` is half the composite PK). `location_pretty` is technically schema-non-null but the action only sends it when present; an empty form-input value is filtered out and the worker leaves the column unchanged. (7) **`pkg_new` is sent only when the editable `pkg` differs from `pkg_original`** — sending `pkg_new === pkg` would force the worker into the rename branch unnecessarily (one extra collision pre-check). The action elides it when equal. (8) **On save success, the parent clears selection** (via `onResult` callback) rather than re-fetching the row to refresh values. The picker returns to its empty state; operator picks the next package. `revalidatePath("/admin/sysadmin")` + `router.refresh()` re-render the page server side, but client-side selection state is cleared explicitly. This avoids "save shows old defaultValue" UX confusion. (9) **`OperationCard` description copy** mentions the editable fields directly (`pkg$, single, flash2/5, sort, pkg name, pricing mode`) so a super_admin sees what the card edits without expanding it. (10) **Cancel button is `type="button"` with onClick**, not a `<a href>` — keeps the operator on the same scroll position and avoids a full page navigation. (11) **`PRICING_MODE_OPTIONS` hardcoded as a const tuple** rather than imported from `@splash/types`'s `PRICING_MODES` — `@splash/types` is transpiled by Next but importing a value (vs. type-only) into a client component pulls the whole module's exports into the bundle; the 5-string constant is cheaper inline. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Cross-worker cache invalidation gap (TODO)** — same as Brief 24. signup-worker caches `pricing_simple_resolved` for 5 minutes; package edits surface on the customer signup form within that window without operator action. A code-comment TODO points at the gap in `handleUpdatePackage`. (b) **`pkg_original` is operator-supplied via the form**, not a server-stored token — a malicious super_admin could edit a different row by lying in the hidden input, but a malicious super_admin can already issue any SQL via Supabase. The composite PK is the only auth-relevant identifier and it's checked at the worker. Acceptable for v1. (c) **Read-only context fields show what the picker fetched at selection time** — they're not re-read during edit. If the operator edits a package and another super_admin updates the location's am_email in a parallel session, the read-only block is stale until the next picker pick. Acceptable v1; a future enhancement could re-fetch on every Save. (d) **Rename pre-check has a TOCTOU window** — a concurrent rename to the same target name could slip in between the collision SELECT and the PATCH. Worst case: Supabase returns a constraint violation 500 wrapped in our `Supabase update failed: ...` shape. No data corruption (the constraint catches it). Negligible in practice given the operator headcount. (e) **`updated_at` audit column is auto-managed by the schema's row-update trigger** (the existing pattern for pricing_simple) — the worker doesn't set it explicitly. The PATCH response with `Prefer: return=representation` reflects whatever the trigger produced, which is what we surface in the action's success message and audit log. (f) **The brief's spec for the search return shape included `address` as a top-level field on `PricingSimpleSearchRow`**, which the worker's GET endpoint passes through verbatim from Supabase. If `pricing_simple` doesn't actually have an `address` column (the schema is the source of truth and I didn't verify), the field comes back as `undefined` and the read-only context block renders an em-dash. No type error because the rows are typed `unknown`-passthrough at the worker boundary; the apps/web TS interface declares it `string | null` but null + undefined render identically in `ReadOnlyField`. Verify in production smoke. (g) **`pkg_dollar` form-field name is mapped to `"pkg$"` JSON key in the action** — same convention Brief 24 used (HTML form-name avoids the `$` to sidestep tooling that might choke on it; the action does the rename once). (h) **Bundle delta within estimate.** `/admin/sysadmin` post-Brief-26: **5.6 kB / 111 kB First Load JS** (was **3.93 kB / 109 kB** post-Brief-24) — **+1.67 kB / +2 kB**, in line with the brief's "+1-2 kB" estimate. PackageSearchPicker (~1 kB) + UpdatePackageCard inflate state (~0.6 kB) account for the delta; the form pre-fill machinery is mostly defaultValue strings which compress well. (i) **`sysadminPostJson` did NOT support PATCH**; required adding `sysadminPatchJson` (and the shared `sysadminWriteJson` helper) per the brief's report q1. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 4.602s (11 cached + 2 fresh — `@splash/sysadmin-worker` and `@splash/web` source changes invalidated turbo cache). `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — Next 15.5.15 compiled in 5.8s, 12/12 static pages generated, all type checks green. **Bundle deltas:** `/admin/sysadmin` 3.93 kB → **5.6 kB** / 109 kB → **111 kB** First Load JS (+1.67 kB / +2 kB). | 2026-05-05 | **Brief 25 completed:** customer claim form polish on damage-worker — addresses 7 gaps surfaced by operator testing of the Brief 23 port. **Files modified:** `apps/damage-worker/src/render/claim-form.ts` — substantial rewrite of `renderClaimForm`. Adds (1) `vehicleYear` `required` attribute; (2) "Managers only" amber pill rendered next to the `customer_get_quotes` determination radio via a new `managersOnly: boolean` flag on the `DETERMINATION_CHOICES` rows + new `.pill` / `.pill-warn` CSS; (3) equipment yes/no segmented toggle defaulting to No that conditionally reveals the equipment dropdown + the malfunction checkbox (when No, `equipmentInvolved` is forced to empty string so the worker's existing `equipmentRelated = (equipmentInvolved && !== "N/A") ? 1 : 0` derivation maps to 0); (4) PIN-gated employee section with a custom overlay modal (PIN=1981 hardcoded as customer-visible obfuscation per brief; correct PIN hides the "Continue" button + reveals the employee section + submit row); (5) multi-photo widget for fourCornersPhotos + damagePhotos with thumbnail previews via `URL.createObjectURL`, per-thumb Remove links, and "+ Add another photo" button labels; single-photo widget for vinPhoto + platePhoto with "Replace photo" label after the first add; (6) `capture` attribute dropped from every photo input — browser-native chooser between camera and gallery; (7) JS-driven submit with `e.preventDefault()`, `FormData` construction (text fields via `new FormData(form)`, photos appended from JS-tracked `photos` object under canonical worker field names), `fetch` with `Accept: application/json`, "Submitting claim, please wait..." overlay, success outcome card showing `claim_id` in monospace + "Submit another claim" reload button, inline error banner above the form on non-2xx. The `EQUIPMENT_CHOICES` tuple lost the explicit "N/A" entry — the toggle's No state is now the canonical "no equipment involved" path. Inline JS lives in a module-level `FORM_SCRIPT` constant (string concatenation throughout — no JS template literals — so the outer TS template literal cannot interpolate `${...}` patterns by accident); interpolated once into the rendered HTML via `<script>${FORM_SCRIPT}</script>`. `<form novalidate>` defers all validation to the JS submit handler so HTML5 bubbles don't fire on the "Continue" button click path. `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts` — `handleClaimSubmission` JSON success branch now returns `{ ok: true, claim_id, ...legacy success/claimId/powerAutomateSuccess/d1Success/photosUploaded keys }`; failure branch returns `{ ok: false, error, success: false }` with status 500. Browser-mode redirect path (`Accept: text/html`) unchanged from Brief 23. **Files created:** none. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **`equipmentMalfunction` checkbox folded inside the equipment-yes block** rather than left as an always-visible top-level toggle — the malfunction question only makes sense when equipment was actually involved; toggle-No forces the hidden `equipmentMalfunction` input to "false". (2) **`employeeName` is required only after PIN unlock**, set via JS on the unlock event — keeping `required` on a hidden field would fire the bubble on the Continue path or before the operator could see the field. (3) **PIN gate uses a custom overlay div, not `<dialog>`** — broader browser compat (older iOS Safari especially), simpler styling, and no polyfill needed. Cancel + Continue buttons + Enter-key submit; wrong PIN shows inline error and clears the input; no rate limiting (PIN is obfuscation, not auth). (4) **JSON success shape mirrors both `ok`/`claim_id` AND legacy `success`/`claimId`** rather than picking one — the form's JS reads `body.claim_id || body.claimId`; future cleanup can drop the legacy keys when the operator confirms no programmatic caller is parsing them. (5) **Photo widget uses one hidden `<input type="file">` per section** rather than a single shared input that gets re-targeted — simpler event wiring, no race between concurrent clicks. `accept="image/*"` only; no `capture`; no `multiple` (one-at-a-time UX is deliberate per brief). (6) **`URL.createObjectURL` thumbnails are revoked on re-render** — hygiene, not load-bearing for short-lived form sessions. (7) **Submit-time validation order:** sync the equipment-toggle's conditional `required` first, then `form.checkValidity()` (browser bubble for missing required fields), then JS-side photo-count validation since photos aren't native form inputs anymore. Photo-missing errors render in the inline error banner above the form. (8) **"Submit another claim" reset is `window.location.reload()`** per brief — simplest correct path; the JS state machine (photos object, thumbUrls, PIN-gate visibility, equipment toggle) doesn't have a single reset hook. (9) **`form.action="/claims-api/submit-claim"` retained** even though submit is JS-driven — graceful degradation if JS is disabled (extremely unlikely in 2026 but cheap to keep). (10) **`novalidate` on `<form>`** — defers validation to the explicit `validateBeforeSubmit()` so HTML5 bubbles don't fire on the Continue button click path or while the employee section is hidden. (11) **Equipment "N/A" choice removed from the dropdown** — the toggle's No state is the canonical "no equipment involved" path; keeping "N/A" alongside Yes would be redundant and confusing. The worker's `!== "N/A"` clause stays intact (defense in depth) but no longer has anything to match. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **No client-side photo size cap** — the worker delegates to the CF Workers 100 MB request body limit; a customer with a stack of HEIF originals could theoretically exceed that. Pre-resize via canvas or HEIC→JPEG conversion is out of scope per brief. (b) **PIN=1981 is in the worker bundle and rendered HTML's `<script>`** — visible in DevTools. Brief explicitly classified this as obfuscation, not auth. If a real auth flow lands later, a separate brief replaces this with a Supabase short-lived token gate. (c) **Submit overlay is a full-viewport opaque-ish overlay**, not a spinner — kept simple. If submission stalls (slow upload), the operator sees only "Submitting claim, please wait...". Future polish: progress indicator showing upload percentage. (d) **`form.checkValidity()` includes still-hidden fields** — mitigated by the linear PIN gate flow (employee section is the only conditionally-hidden block, and the submit button is hidden until PIN unlock); the conditional-required pattern on `equipmentInvolved` (toggled true only when equipment-yes is selected) handles the in-section conditional case. (e) **Worker JSON failure does NOT include partial state** (whether D1 succeeded, whether PA POSTed) — only `body.error`. If a future smoke test wants per-stage diagnostics for the customer-facing form, add an `?debug=1` opt-in branch. (f) **CSRF posture unchanged** — `POST /claims-api/submit-claim` remains public, no `isOriginAllowed` (intentional per Brief 23). The JSON failure shape change doesn't widen the surface. (g) **Bundle delta within estimate.** Damage-worker post-Brief-25: **1644.95 KiB / 372.70 KiB gzip** (was **1628.16 KiB / 368.53 KiB gzip** post-Brief-23) — **+16.79 KiB raw / +4.17 KiB gzip**, within the brief's 5-10 KiB estimate; comfortably under the 3 MiB compressed CF Workers free-tier limit. Driven by the inline `FORM_SCRIPT` (PIN gate + photo widget + equipment toggle + JS submit + outcome card) plus added CSS for the new visual components. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 1.906s (12 cached + 1 fresh — only `@splash/damage-worker` invalidated by the renderClaimForm rewrite + index.ts JSON shape edit; all other packages cache-hit). `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run` succeeded. Bindings inventoried: DB / R2_BUCKET / IMAGES. | Claude Code (Brief 25 session) |
