on 2xx writes a `logSysadminAudit` entry with `action: "create_location"`, `target_type: "pricing_simple"`, `target_id: location_code`, `before: null`, `after: { location_code, location_pretty, package_count }` and returns `{ ok: true, location_code, package_count }`; new module-local `nonNegativeNumber` helper (accepts numbers or numeric strings, rejects NaN/Infinity/negatives). `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/page.tsx` — header comment lists 6 cards; `AddLocationCard` import added; new `AddLocationOperationCard` server component wraps the client island in the existing `OperationCard` shell. `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/actions.ts` — header comment extended; new `createLocationAction` reads location-level fields + iterates the 7 standard package indices reading `pkg_<i>_include`/`pkg_<i>_pkg`/`pkg_<i>_pkg_dollar`/`pkg_<i>_single`/`pkg_<i>_flash2`/`pkg_<i>_flash5`/`pkg_<i>_sort`, skips rows whose include checkbox isn't checked, builds the JSON body with literal `"pkg$"` key, POSTs via `sysadminPostJson("/sysadmin/api/pricing-simple/create-location", body)`, surfaces `{ ok: true, message: "Location created: <code> (<count> packages)" }` or `{ ok: false, error }`; helper `STANDARD_PACKAGES` constant + `fieldNumber` + `fieldOptionalNullable` added. `BRIEFS/INDEX.md` — Brief 24 row marked Completed (2026-05-05); Briefs 25 + 26 added as Not started. `BRIEFS/QUEUE.md` — brief-024 line commented out. `BUILD_STATE.md` — bumped Last updated; this entry; pages table sysadmin row updated to reflect 6 cards + new bundle size; prioritized work list rows added for 24/25/26. `CLAUDE.md` — sysadmin glossary entry extended to mention Brief 24's Add Location feature + the cross-worker cache-invalidation TODO. **Files created:** `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/AddLocationCard.tsx` — client island. `useState<boolean[]>` tracks per-row include state (default-on for the four standard-priced rows: bubble_bath/ultra_bath/bath/express; default-off for ext_exterior/extreme/works); the include checkbox's `onChange` flips the corresponding entry, which drives both `disabled={!isIncluded}` and `required={isIncluded}` on the per-row price inputs (mirrors the conditional-required pattern from UploadDocumentCard via Brief 20). Location-level fields (location_pretty, location_code with `pattern="[a-z0-9_]+"`, site, area_manager, regional_manager, site_email/am_email/rm_email) render as a 2-column grid. Packages render as a 7-row grid with desktop columns Include/Package/pkg$/single/flash2/flash5/sort; mobile collapses to 1 column. Default values pre-fill flash2 = "2.00" and flash5 = "5.00" on every row, sort 1-4 on the four standard-priced rows. Hidden `<input name="pkg_<i>_pkg">` carries the package name (read-only display). Submit wrapped in `<ActionForm action={createLocationAction}>` so the page-level UX matches the existing five cards (inline success/error, router.refresh on ok). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Worker uses raw `fetch` to Supabase REST**, not the supabase-js client, for the bulk insert. Brief explicitly specified the REST POST shape with `Prefer: return=representation` and the array body; raw fetch makes the literal `"pkg$"` JSON key unmistakable in code review. The audit log write below it still uses `createServiceClient(env)` for parity with the rest of the worker. (2) **Pre-check uniqueness on `location_code` only**, not the full composite (location_code, pkg) PK. The PK constraint catches inserts with duplicate (code, pkg) anyway; the pre-check exists so the UI can render a friendly 409 "Location code already in use" instead of letting Supabase return its own constraint-violation text. The race window between pre-check and insert is harmless (a second concurrent create with the same code 500s on PK violation; no data corruption possible because the array POST is atomic). (3) **`nonNegativeNumber` accepts numeric strings** (e.g., "12.50") in addition to JS numbers — the apps/web client island's `<input type="number">` actually submits a string in `FormData` even though it renders a number widget; the action coerces in `fieldNumber`, but defensive coercion at the worker boundary is cheap. (4) **`sort` validation requires positive integer** — the brief said "positive integer; if blank coerce to null"; non-integer floats reject with 400. (5) **Email regex is intentionally simple** (`/^[^@\s]+@[^@\s]+\.[^@\s]+$/`); the brief allowed "or similar simple regex". Captures common typos without overreach (RFC 5322 is impractical; this is shape-only). (6) **Empty/whitespace optional fields coerce to null** via `stringOrNull` (worker) + `fieldOptionalNullable` (action) so the JSON body either includes a real value or omits the field entirely (the worker also accepts null for parity). (7) **`pkg$` literal column name preserved end-to-end:** TS interface `PricingSimpleInsertRow["pkg$"]`, JSON body literal `"pkg$"` key, FormData field name `pkg_<i>_pkg_dollar` (sanitized for HTML — `$` is legal in form names but ESM modules and most HTML5 validators treat it cleanly while bracket-notation in JS handles it; using `pkg_dollar` in form names sidesteps any tooling that might choke on `$`). The action maps `pkg_<i>_pkg_dollar` → `"pkg$"` exactly once at the JSON-body step. (8) **`pricing: 'full'` is hardcoded server-side**, never reads from body — operator has no way to create a location at any other initial mode (matches brief). (9) **`createLocationAction` returns the worker's `location_code` + `package_count` from the response body** (not just echoing the input); falls back to inputs if the worker's response shape is wrong. The success message is `"Location created: <code> (<count> packages)"`. (10) **Disabled price inputs on unchecked rows** rather than removing them from the DOM — keeps the column-aligned grid layout stable when toggling. The action treats `pkg_<i>_include = null` (unchecked) as "skip row entirely"; whether the disabled inputs submit a value is irrelevant. (11) **No client-side row count display** ("3 of 7 packages selected") — the worker already reports the count back in the success message; an inline counter would just duplicate. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Cross-worker cache invalidation gap (TODO).** signup-worker caches `pricing_simple_resolved` for 5 minutes via `caches.default`; sysadmin-worker has no way to bust that cache today. New locations created via this brief surface on the customer signup form within 5 minutes (cache TTL) without operator intervention. The brief flagged this and listed "manual cache-clear button" as a Brief item — not added to INDEX.md as a numbered brief yet (operator decision); a code-comment TODO in `handleCreateLocation` points at the gap. (b) **Custom (non-standard) package names not supported in v1.** The 7 standard packages (bubble_bath/ultra_bath/bath/express/ext_exterior/extreme/works) are baked into AddLocationCard. Operator who needs an ad-hoc package can use a one-shot SQL or wait for Brief 25 (Update Package). The form's help text mentions this. (c) **Edit-after-create flow** is Brief 25's responsibility — Brief 24 is create-only. The card has no "Modify location" affordance. (d) **`getActiveLocationByCode`-equivalent for pricing_simple does NOT exist** in `@splash/db-supabase`'s `pricing.ts`; the worker's pre-check is inlined raw fetch. Adding a typed helper (`locationExistsByCode(client, code) -> boolean`) is a small follow-up and would also benefit Brief 25's search-by-location_code path. Not added in this brief to keep scope tight. (e) **No CSV/Excel bulk import** — out of scope per brief. (f) **No soft-delete / archive flow** — out of scope. (g) **`PricingSimpleRowWithRawPrices` in `packages/types/src/pricing.ts` carries 'NAME UNVERIFIED' caveats from the original port** that no longer apply — `pkg$/single/flash2/flash5` are now confirmed real columns by virtue of Brief 24's INSERT contract. A small docs cleanup brief could remove those caveats; not done here to keep the scope tight. (h) **`logSysadminAudit` accepts `actor` as a `{ id, email }` object** matching `AuthUser`-lite; the worker's `actor` local already has that shape from the top-of-`fetch` `auth.session` extraction, so passing it directly works (no adaptation needed). (i) **Bundle delta:** `/admin/sysadmin` 161 B → 3.93 kB / 105 kB → 109 kB First Load JS (+3.77 kB / +4 kB). Driven by AddLocationCard's React-state include toggles (7 rows × 1 useState slot + onChange handler each; the React state machinery itself is the dominant cost, not the markup). The brief's estimate of "+1-2 kB" was low; the seven independently-toggleable rows + the conditional `required`/`disabled` propagation pushed it higher. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 5.684s (10 cached + 3 fresh — apps/sysadmin-worker, apps/web, and the propagated turbo invalidations); `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — Next 15.5.15 compiled in 5.9s, 12/12 static pages generated, all type checks green. **Bundle deltas:** `/admin/sysadmin` 3.93 kB / 109 kB First Load JS (was 161 B / 105 kB pre-Brief-24 in this build snapshot). All other routes unchanged. | Claude Code (Brief 24 session) |
| 2026-05-05 | **Brief 23 completed:** customer claim form ported to damage-worker — closes decision 9. **Files created:** `apps/damage-worker/src/render/claim-form.ts` — exports `renderClaimForm`, `renderThanksPage`, `renderClaimNotFound`, and `htmlResponse`. Single-page port of the legacy two-section form (legacy multi-step JS, animated bubbles, drag-drop file zones, custom toggle switch, JS modals, and staff-password gate dropped per brief's "keep simple but correct" guidance). Splash-blue gradient header preserved, sourcing the logo via `ASSETS.logoWhite`. Inline `<script>` mirrors the equipment-malfunction checkbox into a hidden `equipmentMalfunction` input so the worker continues to read the legacy `"true"/"false"` string verbatim. EQUIPMENT_CHOICES + DETERMINATION_CHOICES inlined from legacy. **Files modified:** `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts` — route-table comment updated to list the two new GETs and the dual-mode POST; two new public-dispatch branches inserted between `/claims-api/photo/*` and `/manage/api/*`; `handleRenderClaimForm` + `handleRenderThanks` added after `dispatchManageApi`. Both validate the slug via `getActiveLocationByCode` and 404 with friendly HTML on miss. `handleClaimSubmission` made dual-mode: top-of-handler `browserMode = request.headers.get("Accept")?.includes("text/html")` flag drives `Response.redirect(..., 303)` on success (target `/claims/{slug}/thanks?id=<claimId>`) and on the catch branch (target `/claims/{slug}?error=<encoded>`). JSON callers (no `text/html` in Accept) continue to receive the existing JSON shape unchanged. Error-path slug recovery uses Referer header regex `/^\/claims\/([^/]+)/` because `request.formData()` is single-shot and may have already failed. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Field names match what `handleClaimSubmission` actually reads, not what the brief enumerated.** Brief listed `customerMailingAddress`, `damageDescription`, `preexistingDamage`, `staffNotes`, `submittedBy`; worker reads `mailingAddress`, `issueDescription`, `preExistingDamage`, `employeeName` (no `staffNotes` — built worker-side from `customerTold` + `customerDemeanor`). Brief explicitly told me the worker is source of truth ("Field names MUST match what handleClaimSubmission reads"); the brief's enumerated list was a recall miss. Form posts the legacy field names verbatim and hits the worker's existing parse pipeline cleanly. All 24 fields audit-matched against the worker's reads — zero drift. (2) **JSON ↔ 303 dual-mode keyed off `Accept` header**, not a `redirect=html` form field — Accept is what every browser sends automatically and JSON callers send `Accept: application/json`. Edge case: a programmatic caller with browser-shaped Accept gets redirected — flagged latent (no such caller in monorepo today). (3) **303 (See Other) over 302** for the POST→GET-on-success idiom — forces GET on the redirect target, dodges duplicate-submit-on-Back-button re-POST footgun. (4) **Thanks page is a separate `GET /claims/{slug}/thanks` route**, not inline POST response — bookmarkable URL with `?id=<claim-id>`, survives reload, dodges the back-button re-POST. (5) **Invalid slug renders 404 with friendly HTML** rather than 200 — differentiates "I made a typo" from "I'm logged in" for any external healthcheck the operator might wire up. HTML body is identical regardless. (6) **Required attrs match brief spec exactly:** customerName / customerPhone / vehicleMake / vehicleModel / issueDescription / determination / employeeName + the four photo inputs (legacy parity). Optional: vehicleColor / licensePlate / mailingAddress / customerTold / customerDemeanor / customerEmail / vehicleYear / membershipNumber / preExistingDamage. equipmentInvolved kept required for legacy parity (brief didn't enumerate it). (7) **`equipmentMalfunction` is a UI checkbox + hidden input** mirrored by a tiny inline `<script>`; worker reads the hidden `"true"/"false"` value. Only JS in the page. (8) **Visual port is simplified.** Splash-blue gradient header + white card + light-blue gradient page kept; bubbles / multi-step / drag-drop zones / JS modals dropped. Net code drop: ~700 lines of JS eliminated; ~250 lines HTML/CSS retained. (9) **Error-bounce slug recovery via Referer header** rather than `request.clone().formData()` — clone after consume throws, and the catch branch is most often hit precisely because `formData()` itself failed. Fallback slug `"unknown"` renders the not-found card; rare. (10) **URL-bounce caps:** `?error=` 240 chars, `?id=` 64 chars. Defends against malicious or malformed bounce content; `escHtml` defends against the rest. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **`isOriginAllowed` not enforced on `POST /claims-api/submit-claim`** — public endpoint, intentional (legacy parity). Customer posts from arbitrary device-bookmarked URLs without referrer policy. CSRF risk negligible since no auth state to forge from cross-origin script. Worth noting for a future CSRF-posture brief. (b) **`getActiveLocationByCode` runs on every form GET, every thanks GET, and every POST submit** for `location_pretty` resolution. Three D1 reads per claim flow. Future optimization: cache active-locations table at module load. (c) **`location_pretty` posted by the form is not authoritative** — the worker re-resolves at POST time. Defense against DevTools edits is automatic. (d) **`equipmentMalfunction` parses `String === "true"`** — anything else is false. New form + legacy form emit lowercase; non-form callers should be aware. (e) **Programmatic caller with `text/html` in Accept would get 303 redirect** instead of JSON. No such caller today. Mitigation if it surfaces: `?json=1` query opt-out. Out of scope. (f) **Browser-mode error path caps message at 240 chars.** Realistic worker errors are short; cap is a safety bound. (g) **No 1MB / 100MB upload-size guard at form level** — legacy didn't have one either; CF Workers caps at 100 MB. Customers exceeding that see a CF error page, not the form's banner. **`handleClaimSubmission` response reconciliation (Report q1):** browser submit → 303 redirect; programmatic JSON caller → existing JSON shape. **Where renderDamageForm landed (Report q2):** `apps/damage-worker/src/render/claim-form.ts` (extracted module — too large to inline cleanly even after simplification). **Field-name audit (Report q3):** all 24 form fields match the worker's reads at index.ts:1117-1146 (full table in the brief Outcome). **Bundle-size delta (Report q4):** post-Brief-23 damage-worker bundle is **1628.16 KiB / 368.53 KiB gzip**. No clean pre-Brief baseline was captured for an exact delta — the brief's 10-30 KiB gzip estimate is consistent with the observed payload. Comfortably under CF's 3 MiB compressed free-tier limit. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 2.707s (12 cached + 1 fresh — only `@splash/damage-worker` invalidated). `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run` succeeded. Bindings inventoried: DB / R2_BUCKET / IMAGES. Staging routes for `staging.splashcarwashes.info/claims/*` were already wired in Brief 16 — no wrangler.toml change. | Claude Code (Brief 23 session) |
| 2026-05-05 | **Brief 22 completed:** recent-notes sub-box added inside `/admin/damage/[id]`'s SummaryCard with a jump-to-add-note anchor button. **Files modified:** `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/page.tsx` — `SummaryCard` props extended with `activity: ClaimActivityRow[]`; new server-rendered `RecentNotesBox` sub-component renders after `ApprovalDetails` using the same `mt-4 rounded-splash-md border border-gray-light bg-sudsy-blue-soft/30 p-4` idiom. Box header carries the "Recent notes" label on the left and a small `<a href="#add-note">Add note</a>` styled as a white-bg + splash-blue-text + splash-blue-bordered button (mirrors the no-access-card "Back to Dashboard" idiom from /admin/sysadmin). Empty state: `<p className="text-sm italic text-splash-navy/60">No notes yet.</p>`. Populated state: up to 3 notes (filtered by `activity_type === "note"`, sorted desc by `created_at` with id-desc tie-break, sliced to 3) rendered as a `<ul>` of `<li>` items — timestamp (mono `YYYY-MM-DD HH:mm` via existing `formatDateTime`), actor name (bold), note text (`whitespace-pre-line`). When notes exist, a "View all activity ↓" link anchors to `#activity` at the bottom-right. `AddNoteCard` wrapper `<div>` gains `id="add-note"`. `ActivityTimelineCard` wrapper `<div>` gains `id="activity"`. Top-of-file section comment updated: section 3 (SummaryCard appends Recent notes), section 7 (timeline carries `id="activity"`), section 8 (add-note carries `id="add-note"`). `apps/web/app/globals.css` — added `html { scroll-behavior: smooth; }` with a Brief 22 comment. Affects every anchor jump site-wide; harmless on routes without anchors. **Files created:** none. RecentNotesBox is implemented inline as a server component within `page.tsx` (single-use, mirroring `ApprovalDetails`/`AuditStamps` siblings). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Placement: inside the SummaryCard at the bottom**, after ApprovalDetails — best serves the brief's "alongside the claim summary" goal and reuses the existing sub-box visual idiom rather than introducing a third stacked card above the Move-forward section. (2) **"Add note" button styling: white-bg + splash-blue text + splash-blue border** — secondary affordance treatment that doesn't compete with the primary splash-blue-fill submit buttons in the transitions section or the AddNoteCard's own submit at the bottom. (3) **"View all activity ↓" anchor link added** as the optional brief feature; renders only when notes exist (no point pointing at an empty timeline). (4) **Sort defensively even though the worker SELECTs ORDER BY created_at DESC, id DESC** — `ActivityTimelineCard` itself defensively re-sorts (page.tsx:1162-1166), so RecentNotesBox matches that posture rather than depending on a fetch-order invariant the worker layer could change without the UI layer noticing. (5) **`whitespace-pre-line` on note text** — matches the timeline's own note rendering; preserves multi-line notes typed into the textarea. (6) **`scroll-behavior: smooth` applied globally on `html`** rather than scoped to a class on the anchor target. The CSS property only takes effect on the scrolling element (typically `html` or `body`); putting it on the target itself has no effect. The brief offered the scoped fallback but it wouldn't actually work as written. Global is the simplest correct path; the app uses very few anchor jumps so side effects are minimal. (7) **`id="activity"`** added even though only the optional "View all activity ↓" link consumes it today — tiny markup; future briefs can deep-link into the timeline without re-touching this file. (8) **No client island** — notes list, jump button, and timeline-anchor link are all server-rendered HTML/CSS; browser-native smooth scroll + plain `<a href="#…">` anchors avoid any "use client" need. Bundle delta is zero. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Empty-state UX placement.** When the claim has zero notes, the box still renders the title + "Add note" button alongside the "No notes yet." line. The brief's empty-state spec was interpreted as "instead of an empty bulleted list", not "instead of the entire box including the jump button" — the jump button is more useful when the claim has zero notes than when it has many. (b) **`View all activity ↓` link in populated state only.** With zero notes the link is hidden — but the operator can still scroll, and the empty-notes case already implies a sparse claim. (c) **Notes list cap is 3, hardcoded.** Future briefs that want "5 most recent" or a per-user pref can lift the constant. (d) **`formatDateTime` slice trick** preserves the worker's DB-side ISO without timezone shift; works for both "YYYY-MM-DD HH:mm:ss" (D1) and "YYYY-MM-DDTHH:mm:ss(.sss)Z" (Postgres) shapes since both have HH:mm at offsets 11-15. (e) **Smooth-scroll global rule** affects every anchor jump site-wide. Only this brief's two anchors (#add-note, #activity) consume it today. If the app ever ships a feature where instant jump is preferable, wrap that surface in a container with `scroll-behavior: auto` to override. (f) **Bundle size unchanged at 3.08 kB / 108 kB First Load JS** for `/admin/damage/[id]` — RecentNotesBox is server-only markup, the anchor-link CSS is plain Tailwind, and the smooth-scroll rule is in globals.css (already part of the layout). No client JS delta. (g) **`RecentNotesBox` lives next to `ApprovalDetails` / `AuditStamps`** in the same file, following the existing function-per-section pattern. If `page.tsx` ever splits into per-card files (low priority — file is tractable today), this block moves cleanly with no shared state. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 3.831s (12 cached + 1 ran fresh — only `@splash/web` source changed). `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — Next 15.5.15 compiled in 5.7s, 12/12 static pages generated, lint + type checks green. **Bundle deltas:** `/admin/damage/[id]` 3.08 kB / 108 kB First Load JS — **unchanged** from Brief 21 snapshot. All other routes unchanged. | Claude Code (Brief 22 session) |
| 2026-05-05 | **Brief 21 completed:** dcRole gating restored on `/admin/damage/[id]` with the show-disabled pattern + Brief 18 diagnostic cleanup + contact_status "Not Started" pill suppression. **Files modified:** `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/page.tsx` — dropped the Brief 18 workaround `validTransitions = transitionsFrom(claim.claim_status)` in favor of a richer compute that maps each valid-from-status transition to `{ transition, enabled, disabledHint }`. `enabled` is `dcRole !== null && t.allowedRoles.includes(dcRole)` (super_admin always passes — `rolesAtLeast()` includes it on every transition). New helper `transitionDisabledHint(t, dcRole)` produces hint copy: `"Requires a damage role assignment"` when dcRole is null, `"Pending final approval"` when label === "Submit for Payment" (operator wording), else `"Requires <minRole> or higher"` where minRole is the lowest hierarchy member present in `allowedRoles`. `TransitionSection` and `TransitionForm` props gained `enabled` + `disabledHint`. The form's action header now renders the hint as `text-xs italic text-splash-navy/60` below the to-status mono. The submit button now toggles between the splash-blue active class and a muted `bg-splash-navy/20 text-splash-navy/50 cursor-not-allowed` class via the `disabled` attribute. Markup and required-input fields are otherwise unchanged — the worker re-validates dcRole on POST as defense-in-depth, so the visible required attrs on disabled rows are harmless (the form can't submit). DcRoleDebugLine component definition + render site removed; `dcRole` is still computed for the gating math. The `claim.contact_status` truthy-only render gate now also rejects `"Not Started"` — comment block points at the legacy intent (customer-contact lifecycle that the new worker never writes) and flags it as a future feature candidate. Header comment block updated: dropped the Brief 18 reverted-filter section, added a Brief 21 note about show-disabled. `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts` — removed `logActionEntry` + `logActionResult` helper functions and the `DamagePostResult` type import. Removed every call to those helpers in transitionAction, addNoteAction, uploadDocumentAction, editDocumentAction, deleteDocumentAction (10 call sites total). Header comment updated: brief list became "5c + 5d + 19 + 20 + 21" and the Brief 18 diagnostic logging paragraph deleted. Brief 20's defensive try/catch on editDocumentAction is retained (separate concern — E394 cover, not diagnostic). `BRIEFS/INDEX.md` — Brief 21 row added marked Completed (2026-05-05). `BUILD_STATE.md` — bumped Last updated; this entry; prioritized work list row added. `CLAUDE.md` — extended the apps/web subsection with a one-line note that damage detail transitions are show-disabled when caller's dc_role doesn't match. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **The hint text on disabled rows is `text-xs italic text-splash-navy/60`** — italic distinguishes it from the to-status mono line above it without competing for attention with the action label, and `splash-navy/60` is the same opacity used elsewhere on the page for secondary text (e.g., empty-transitions message). (2) **Disabled submit button uses `bg-splash-navy/20` background + `text-splash-navy/50` foreground + `cursor-not-allowed`** — keeps the same shape and padding as the active button so the row layout doesn't reflow on enable/disable; muted color matches the rest of the page's disabled-affordance treatment. (3) **Required input fields stay rendered + `required` on disabled rows** — the brief explicitly said "Render each row with the existing form/markup, but when enabled === false: Submit button disabled + hint." The `required` attribute is harmless when the button can't submit (HTML5 validation only fires on submit attempt). Considered wrapping in `<fieldset disabled>` for full input disable but that would have changed the visual styling of the inputs as well; minimal change wins. (4) **`transitionDisabledHint` is name-based for the "Submit for Payment" branch** — explicitly noted in the function comment + brief that if the label changes in `transitions.ts` the hint silently won't fire. Acceptable v1; flagged in code comment + outcome below for future maintainers. (5) **`contact_status` legacy comment** — chose a 5-line block explaining the legacy intent + suggesting a future tracking feature, rather than a single-line "// hidden until used" — operator confirmed in the brief that this should hint at future work, not just suppress. (6) **Diagnostic helper removal includes the `DamagePostResult` type import** — was only consumed by `logActionResult`, dead after removal. Kept the `result.ok` checks themselves (they predate Brief 18 and gate the action's return value). (7) **No restoration of a smaller "transitions are gated" hint at the section level** — every disabled row carries its own hint, which is more discoverable than a section-level note. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **`transitions.ts` table integrity:** I scanned the 33 entries for empty `allowedRoles` or missing labels; every row has both. The role hierarchy `["gm", "rm", "admin", "super_admin"]` is reproduced in `transitionDisabledHint` and must stay in sync if `_lib/transitions.ts` ever adds a new role tier (no current plan to). (b) **Show-disabled is a UX hint, not access control.** Worker re-validates on POST. Hiding destructive transitions entirely (e.g., the Closed-→-Pending reopen, the Approved-→-Pending revert paths) was considered but kept as show-disabled per brief — operators learn what's possible at this status even when they can't act. If an operator wants a higher-bar treatment for the "(admin)" reopens, that's a separate brief. (c) **dcRole-null mystery from Brief 11b is resolved by operator-side data**, not a worker-side fix — the row in `damage_claim_user_roles` populated `auth_unified` as expected once Brief 11b removed the `/api/me` 'bad origin' gate. The Brief 18 workaround (drop the filter + DcRoleDebugLine) was always meant as temporary; restoring proper gating in Brief 21 is the resolution. (d) **`required` attributes on inputs of disabled rows.** With the button disabled the form can't submit and HTML5 won't validate; if a future change re-enables the form via JS or a different submit path, the required validation will fire on bare placeholder text. Acceptable trade-off because the brief specified "existing form/markup" with only the submit disabled. (e) **Bundle size for `/admin/damage/[id]` unchanged at 3.08 kB / 108 kB First Load JS** despite removing the diagnostic helpers — those helpers were server-only (no `"use client"`), so they never shipped to the client bundle. The DcRoleDebugLine was also a server function. The visible delta is in the server-side bundle (slightly smaller worker), not in the route's client First Load JS. Owns the `<details>` open state via useState (vs. the prior server-rendered `<details>`); on a successful save (via ActionForm's new `onResult` callback) sets open=false so the panel collapses (Bug 8 fix). Quote-row required attrs match the upload card. Cancel button now closes locally instead of navigating. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Worker response shape: rename `was_new`/`was_present` → `changed`** rather than retain both. (2) **No-op audit row uses distinct action names** (`grant_tool_noop` / `revoke_tool_noop`) instead of reusing the success action with a `notes` discriminator — clean SQL filtering and unambiguous when scanning the log. (4) **Activity log note: `[Reset approval details on revert]` suffix** appended to whatever the operator typed (or used as the entire note when they didn't type one) — keeps the timeline self-describing without requiring a new activity_type and the D1 CHECK rebuild that would entail. (5) **Stamps reset is part of clearApprovalDetails** — the brief listed gm/rm/ceo audit stamps as part of the clearing scope; bundled with the approval-detail nulls in a single UPDATE SET clause so it's atomic. Future stamp-only reverts (none today) would need a separate flag. (6) **ActionForm `onResult` callback rather than per-call useActionState replication** — extending the shared wrapper is cheaper than duplicating its state machinery in DocumentEditDetails. The ref-based callback storage means the parent doesn't have to memoize the handler. (7) **Edit form: required attrs enforce Quote shape on save**, not inheritance from the existing row. The form pre-fills with defaultValue so an unmodified save re-submits the existing values; an explicit clear is treated as a misconfiguration and the worker rejects with a useful 400 (surfaced inline). Operators can't accidentally clear amount on a Quote row anymore. (8) **Defensive try/catch on editDocumentAction only** — the brief specifically called out E394 on edit. The other four damage actions follow the same shape and don't need wrapping today, but the pattern is now established if the same symptom appears elsewhere. (9) **The dcRole transition filter on the damage detail page stays dropped** (Brief 18 condition); not restored here. The DcRoleDebugLine also stays. Both belong to a separate cleanup brief, not Brief 20. (10) **Bug 4 hint card replaces the entire form** for that transition row, not just the disabled state — eliminates "why is this greyed out" confusion entirely. The action's label + target status are still rendered so the operator knows what they're being asked to enable. (11) **CLAUDE.md addition is deferred** — Brief 20's no-op-detection pattern is reusable but the only place it currently applies is sysadmin's grant/revoke. Codifying it in CLAUDE.md without a second reference instance feels premature; flagged for future extraction if a similar pattern lands elsewhere. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Receipt-row Quote-shape validation gap.** The worker now strictly enforces Quote required fields; Receipt rows stay loose (amount/pay_to_type/vendor optional). Per brief, this is intentional — Receipts don't drive the check-request PDF — but a Receipt with a vendor pay-to and no vendor_address is silently allowed. Cleanup brief candidate if Receipts ever start driving downstream flows. (b) **Edit form: `vendor` field on a Quote-customer row is no longer marked required by the UI**, but remains optional on the worker side too. If pay-to is `customer`, vendor is informational-only. Symmetric across upload/edit. (c) **`canMutateDocument` UI gate** in apps/web/app/admin/damage/_lib/permissions.ts is unchanged; DocumentEditDetails only renders when the gate passes. (d) **router.refresh() after edit-save** still fires from ActionForm's existing useEffect — Brief 20's `onResult` is additive, not replacing. The `<details>` close-on-success happens in parallel with the refresh; no race because both target the same React tree update cycle. (e) **`<details>` open state via React** vs. native HTML — switching from server-rendered `<details>` to controlled means open/close transitions go through React state. | 2026-05-04 | **Brief 19 completed:** server-action ActionResult + router.refresh() pattern landed on damage detail and sysadmin. Operator's first end-to-end staging test (post-Brief 18) showed actions completing on the server (DB updates, worker logs OK) but the browser sitting on the pre-action URL until manual reload — the Next 15 server-action `redirect()` response wasn't reliably propagating as a visible client navigation in the OpenNext-on-Cloudflare-Workers runtime. Pricing admin was unaffected because it uses direct client `fetch()` + `setState`. Brief 19 flips damage and sysadmin to the `useActionState` + `router.refresh()` pattern that matches Next 15 + React 19 docs for inline post-action feedback. **Files created:** `apps/web/app/admin/_components/ActionForm.tsx` — shared `"use client"` wrapper exporting `ActionResult` type and `<ActionForm>` component. `useActionState(action, null)` dispatches the action; `useEffect` calls `router.refresh()` on every fresh `ok` result; success/error rendered inline as `<p role="status">` / `<p role="alert">` under the form; `resetOnSuccess` (default true) keys the `<form>` off `ok:<message>` so React remounts the subtree on success and clears uncontrolled inputs; `encType` passthrough so multipart forms work; `data-pending` attribute on the form for downstream styling hooks. **Files modified:** `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts` — all five actions (transition, addNote, uploadDocument, editDocument, deleteDocument) signature changed to `(prevState, formData) => Promise<ActionResult>` and bodies return typed results instead of `redirect()`. `errorRedirect` helper + `next/navigation` import gone. `revalidatePath` calls retained on success. Brief 18's `[damage-action]` diagnostic logging retained. Per-action success messages are specific (e.g. `"Status updated to <to_status>"`, `"<doc_type> uploaded"`). `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/page.tsx` — wrapped TransitionForm, AddNoteCard, DocumentEditForm, ConfirmDeleteBanner's delete form, and UploadDocumentCard with `<ActionForm>` (passing `encType="multipart/form-data"` on upload). Dropped `actionError` searchParam reading + page-level `<ActionAlert>` banner + its function definition. `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/actions.ts` — same shape change for create/setRole/grant/revoke/reset; success messages match brief spec (`"User created: <email>"`, `"Role updated"`, `"Granted <tool>"`, etc.). `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/page.tsx` — wrapped all five OperationCard forms; dropped `?action_error`/`?action_success` searchParam handling + page-level banners + `PageProps`/`firstParam` (no longer needed). PASSWORD_MATCH_SCRIPT rewritten to use event delegation on `document` instead of binding to element references at parse time — necessary because `<ActionForm>` remounts the form on success and the old script would have stale references. `CLAUDE.md` — new "Server actions: useActionState + router.refresh() pattern (Brief 19)" subsection under "Working with apps/web", inserted before "Service bindings (Brief 17)". Documents the rationale, four-step pattern (signature/body/wrapper/drop searchParam), and the inline-script gotcha. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) `<ActionForm>` is the only client island; no per-form bespoke wrappers. (2) Specific per-action success messages (parallel structure between damage and sysadmin) over a generic "Saved" — operator gets a distinguishable inline toast for each operation. (3) `resetOnSuccess` left at default true everywhere — the multipart upload, note textarea, transition forms, and document edit form all benefit from clearing on success; the reset password card was the closest call (security smell to keep plaintext passwords in DOM after success), and remounting clears them. (4) PASSWORD_MATCH_SCRIPT changed to event delegation rather than dropping `resetOnSuccess` on the reset password card — event delegation is the more durable fix and survives any future remount. (5) `revalidatePath()` retained on every success — without it, `router.refresh()` could serve stale cached HTML. (6) Brief 18's diagnostic logging stays in the damage actions; not Brief 19's job to remove. (7) The dcRole transition filter on the damage detail page stays dropped (Brief 18 condition); not restored here. (8) Sysadmin page signature simplified to `()` from `({ searchParams })` — dead `PageProps`/`firstParam` removed; damage detail keeps its searchParams for the `confirm_delete_id` feature. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **performance-worker submission form not migrated** — out of scope per brief; uses redirect+searchParam-banner pattern from Brief 6; flag for follow-up if operator hits the same symptom there. (b) **Brief 18 cleanup brief grows to 4 items** — should now also remove `[damage-action]` diagnostic console.log calls. Cleanup recipe: confirm dcRole populates → restore transition filter → remove DcRoleDebugLine → remove logActionEntry/logActionResult. (c) **`router.refresh()` is a soft refresh** — server data re-fetches but client islands keep state across the boundary (PhotoLightbox open state, UserPicker selection survives). Intended behavior; no-op for Brief 19's surfaces but worth knowing for future actions where you want a hard reset. (d) **`data-pending` form attribute is exposed but unused** — future briefs can add `[data-pending="true"]` styling hooks (gray submit button, spinner, etc.); zero cost today. (e) **TS inference on the `ActionResult` union is clean** — `useActionState` resolves the generic params from the action signature, no explicit type annotations needed at call sites. Verified by `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 green. (f) **Login flow's redirect is intentional and unchanged** — that's a navigation by intent (move from /login to /admin/dashboard), not a post-action UX feedback redirect; pattern doesn't apply. **Will the visible-refresh problem actually be fixed?** Yes by data flow: action runs → revalidatePath invalidates Next's route cache → action returns ok result → ActionForm's useEffect fires router.refresh() → Next re-fetches the route's server-component data → server re-renders with the post-mutation state → the new HTML replaces the old in-place. Same end result as pricing admin's client-fetch+setState approach, just routed through Next's standard server-action machinery instead of a worker JSON API. No reliance on redirect-response propagation, which was the OpenNext-on-CF gap. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 4.4s (12 cached, 1 cache miss for `@splash/web` after source changes invalidated turbo cache); `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — Next 15.5.15 compiled in 4.3s, 12/12 static pages generated. **Bundle deltas:** `/admin/damage/[id]` 965 B → 1.33 kB / 106 kB → 106 kB (+365 B route, 0 First Load); `/admin/sysadmin` 1.93 kB → 2.27 kB / 107 kB → 107 kB (+340 B route, 0 First Load). First Load JS unchanged because `useActionState`/`useRouter` were already in the vendor bundle (used by login form + existing client islands). All other route bundle sizes unchanged from Brief 18 snapshot. | Claude Code (Brief 19 session) |

| 2026-05-04 | **Brief 17 completed:** service bindings for apps/web → sibling worker subrequests. **Files modified:** `apps/web/wrangler.toml` (added 5 `[[services]]` blocks above `[assets]` — DASHBOARD_WORKER → splash-dashboard, SIGNUP_WORKER → splash-signup-next, PERFORMANCE_WORKER → splash-performance, SYSADMIN_WORKER → splash-sysadmin, DAMAGE_WORKER → splash-damage); `apps/web/app/_lib/me.ts` (`getMe()` tries `env.DASHBOARD_WORKER.fetch(req)` first, falls back to URL-based fetch on `getCloudflareContext()` throw or undefined binding); `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` (extracted `damageGetResponse` + `damagePost` internal dispatchers — both prefer service binding, fall back to URL fetch; `damageGetJson`/`damageGetJsonOrStatus`/`damagePostForm`/`damagePostMultipart` reshape onto these); `apps/web/app/admin/pricing/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` (`workerGetJson` inline-pattern for SIGNUP_WORKER, fallback in catch and undefined-binding branches); `apps/web/app/admin/performance/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` (`performanceGetJson` + `performancePostJson` for PERFORMANCE_WORKER); `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` (`sysadminGetJson` + `sysadminPostJson` for SYSADMIN_WORKER). **File created:** `apps/web/cloudflare-env.d.ts` — declaration-merges the 5 worker bindings into the global `CloudflareEnv` interface declared by @opennextjs/cloudflare. Uses `/// <reference types="@cloudflare/workers-types" />` to make `Fetcher` resolvable. ASSETS not redeclared (opennext's own .d.ts already declares it). `CLAUDE.md` gained a "Service bindings (Brief 17)" subsection under "Working with apps/web" — documents the binding-with-URL-fallback pattern + lists the 5 bindings + explains *why* (CF same-zone Worker-to-Worker URL fetches loop and 522). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **`https://internal` placeholder host** consistently across all 5 helpers — service bindings ignore the URL host so the literal value doesn't matter, but pinning it prevents debugging confusion if a request leaks. (2) **`damagePhotoUrl` and `damageCheckRequestUrl` stay URL-based** — both return absolute URLs the *browser* fetches (via `<img src>` and `<a href>`), not the apps/web Worker, so the same-zone subrequest concern doesn't apply. Comment block in `damage/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` calls this out so future maintainers don't migrate them by reflex. (3) **Try/catch around `getCloudflareContext({ async: true })` + check for undefined binding** — covers both `next dev` (where `getCloudflareContext` throws because Workers runtime isn't running) and any future runtime where the binding might be unbound. The catch branch + undefined-binding branch both fall through to the same URL-based fetch path so behavior is uniform. (4) **`Origin` header set to `new URL(url).origin`** in POST helpers across both binding and URL paths — under the binding, `url = "https://internal" + path`, so Origin becomes `https://internal`; the worker's `isOriginAllowed` check derives an allow-list from `ALLOWED_ORIGINS` and accepts cross-binding traffic the same way it accepts the URL-based path's Origin (no special-casing needed; the worker's logic is `targetOrigin matches one of allowed_origins`). (5) **Type declaration approach = single `cloudflare-env.d.ts` at apps/web root**, declaration-merging `CloudflareEnv` rather than redeclaring it — opennext's own `cloudflare-context.d.ts` already declares `ASSETS?: Fetcher; IMAGES?: ImagesBinding; ...` in `CloudflareEnv`, so we extend instead of fight upstream. `**/*.ts` in apps/web/tsconfig.json's include picks the new file up automatically. (6) **No changes to `next.config.mjs` rewrites** — they stay as the dev-mode fallback when service bindings aren't available (per brief Out-of-scope). (7) **Service name `splash-signup-next` (not `splash-signup`)** matches `apps/signup-worker/wrangler.toml`'s `name` field. **Forward flag for cutover (item 13):** when signup-worker renames to `splash-signup` at full production cutover, the SIGNUP_WORKER binding's `service = "splash-signup-next"` line must update too — added inline mention in the wrangler.toml comment block. **Latent issues / observations:** (a) `getCloudflareContext({ async: true })` is the right shape for OpenNext 1.19.5 (verified via `apps/web/node_modules/@opennextjs/cloudflare/dist/api/cloudflare-context.d.ts:56-58`); the async overload returns `Promise<CloudflareContext>`, the sync overload throws if not initialized. We use async and rely on the throw → URL-fallback path for dev. (b) **`Fetcher` type resolution.** apps/web's tsconfig (via `@splash/config/tsconfig.next.json`) does NOT include `@cloudflare/workers-types` in `types`, but the helpers don't reference `Fetcher` directly — they just call `.fetch(req)` on `env.DASHBOARD_WORKER` (etc.) which is typed via the merged interface. The merged interface declaration in `cloudflare-env.d.ts` does name `Fetcher`; the `/// <reference types="@cloudflare/workers-types" />` directive at the top pulls workers-types globally for that file (and consequently the project, since it's in the include set). With `skipLibCheck: true`, the heavy workers-types globals don't conflict with DOM lib at lib-check time. Verified clean: `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 green. (c) **POST helpers' Origin under service binding.** I derive Origin from `new URL("https://internal" + path).origin` → `"https://internal"`. The damage-worker / sysadmin-worker / performance-worker `isOriginAllowed` reads `ALLOWED_ORIGINS` from each worker's wrangler.toml (or vars), comparing against the request's Origin/Referer header. None of the worker `ALLOWED_ORIGINS` values include `https://internal`, so under the binding the check would fail. **However:** Cloudflare Workers' service bindings are documented to bypass the edge entirely; `isOriginAllowed`-style checks are a CSRF mitigation against cross-origin browser POSTs and are not technically meaningful for Worker-to-Worker subrequests where there is no malicious script to forge the call. The operator can either (a) leave the helper sending `https://internal` Origin and add it to each worker's allow-list, or (b) drop the Origin header under the binding path and adjust each worker to skip the check when the request comes via service binding (e.g., via a custom header). **Pre-emptive recommendation:** option (a) — explicitly add `https://internal` to each worker's `ALLOWED_ORIGINS` env var at next deploy, since it's small surface and keeps the check on. Flagged for the cutover/staging deploy step; not blocking this brief because the brief explicitly says "Don't deploy from headless mode" and binding-vs-URL behavior is asymmetric enough that the operator should decide. (d) **TS inference with Fetcher type** — clean, no issues. The merged interface declares each binding as `Fetcher` (non-optional); call sites use `if (env.DASHBOARD_WORKER) { ... }` to narrow against undefined-at-runtime (in case the deployment is ever missing the binding), which TS happily allows on a non-optional `Fetcher`. (e) **Dev-mode fallback verification** — the catch branch around `getCloudflareContext` covers `next dev`. Logically clean, but couldn't smoke-test in next dev within this session because the brief is headless and `pnpm dev` would need an interactive terminal. Operator can verify by running `pnpm --filter @splash/web dev` and exercising one of the admin pages — the helper should log nothing unusual and the URL-based fallback should fire (with the existing `NEXT_PUBLIC_*_WORKER_URL` env vars or same-origin proxies, same as before). (f) **No regression on bundle sizes** — every route's First Load JS is identical to the Brief 16 snapshot (e.g., `/admin/damage/[id]` still 965 B / 106 kB). Service-binding code is server-only. (g) **Photo / check-request URLs in damage helper** stay URL-based per brief §scope.4 and explicit comment in code. **Latent issues spotted in the existing helpers (out-of-scope but flagged):** (i) `damageGetResponse` and `damagePost` extracted in this brief duplicate the URL/binding fork; a future cleanup could hoist a generic `workerSubrequest` into `apps/web/app/_lib/worker-subrequest.ts` parameterized on binding name + helper. Five copies of the same try/catch/fall-through pattern is duplication-by-design for now (each helper has its own URL env-var name + Cookie/Origin posture); collapse if the pattern grows further. (ii) When the binding is bound but throws at the worker layer (e.g., 5xx), the helpers will surface it without falling back to URL — correct (we don't want to hide real bugs behind a fallback) but worth knowing for future debugging. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 9.055s (all packages re-ran fresh after TOML/.d.ts changes invalidated the turbo cache); `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — Next 15.5.15 compiled in 4.2s, 12/12 static pages generated, all route bundle sizes unchanged from Brief 16's snapshot (`/admin/damage/[id]` 965 B / 106 kB; `/admin/performance` 1.85 kB / 107 kB; `/admin/sysadmin` 161 B / 105 kB; etc. — service bindings are server-only so zero client JS delta). | Claude Code (Brief 17 session) |

Cache key is just function identity (no args) so the entire request reuses one Session. Verified the cache hits: `/admin/damage/[id]` is the only consumer beyond the layout in this brief, and only one `/api/me` fetch fires per render. (6) **`Origin` header set explicitly** in `getMe()`'s fetch — server-side fetch doesn't auto-populate Origin and the worker's `isOriginAllowed` rejects without it; derived from the target URL so it works in both prod same-origin and dev cross-origin. (7) **Cross-origin URL fork** mirrors `admin/damage/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` — `NEXT_PUBLIC_DASHBOARD_WORKER_URL` short-circuit when set, request-host fallback otherwise. (8) **Header layout** — added a `flex-col items-end leading-tight` group with `text-sm text-white/90` email above a small uppercase tracked sudsy-blue role label, placed in the same nav line as the buttons; outer nav is now `flex-wrap` with `gap-x-4 gap-y-2` so the bar wraps cleanly on narrow viewports rather than overflowing horizontally. (9) **Damage detail null-session branch is defensive** — when `session === null` (in practice unreachable, since `apps/web/middleware.ts` redirects unauthenticated users to `/login` before this page renders), the filter renders zero transitions and the existing "No further transitions available from current status." copy fires. (10) **`session.dcRole === null` ("no damage role assigned") also collapses to zero transitions** — `t.allowedRoles.includes(null)` is always false (DamageRole is a string union with no null), so the same empty-state copy renders, which is correct UX for "you have no damage role." **Latent issues:** (a) **Dev cross-origin cookie limitation** persists per BUILD_STATE.md — when apps/web runs on localhost / its own workers.dev URL and the dashboard-worker is on a different origin, the `sb-access-token` cookie set by the dashboard-worker origin doesn't reach apps/web's origin under SameSite=Lax. `/api/me` reliably returns 401 in that mode and the Header gracefully renders without user info. Not new; called out in the brief's §6 as expected behavior. (b) **`authenticate()` is two round-trips** — `/auth/v1/user` then a Supabase service-role read of `auth_unified`. With React `cache()`, that's one round-trip pair per page render rather than per call site. If the layout becomes a hotspot we could move to JWT-only decode (skip `auth_unified` for the Header's email + role and pull dcRole separately), but for now the read is well within latency budget for SSR. (c) **The TS spec in the brief used `t.roles` and `t.from`** — the actual UITransition shape is `t.allowedRoles` and `t.from` (the latter matches; the former does not). Used `allowedRoles` consistent with the file. **Anything Brief 5d should know:** `getMe()` is now available — 5d's `canMutateDocument` gating (Quote/Receipt edit + delete) can call it directly with no extra fetch cost (cache-shared with the layout's call). `dcRole === null` reliably means "no damage role" and should collapse mutation buttons to zero, mirroring how transitions now behave. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 3.661s (11 cached + dashboard-worker + apps/web ran fresh); `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — Next 15.5.15 compiled in 4.1s, 12/12 static pages generated, `/admin/damage/[id]` route still `ƒ` at 171 B / 105 kB First Load JS (no client JS regression despite the new layout fetch); `wrangler deploy --dry-run` for dashboard-worker succeeded with bundle size 713.29 KiB / gzip 135.20 KiB (well under CF's 3 MiB free / 10 MiB paid limits — no growth concern from the new handler). | Claude Code (Brief 11a session) |
| 2026-05-04 | **Brief 7 completed:** sysadmin UI at `/admin/sysadmin` — five-card super_admin gate consuming all 5 sysadmin-worker mutation endpoints. Replaces the Brief 4 placeholder. **Files created:** `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` (`sysadminGetJson<T>` + `sysadminPostJson<T>(path, body)` — same dev-vs-prod URL fork + Cookie + Origin forwarding as the damage helper, but JSON-bodied since sysadmin-worker reads `request.json()` for all 5 mutations); `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/NoAccessCard.tsx` (server component with `reason="signin" \| "forbidden"` variants — Sign In button + return-path on the former, explanatory text + Back to Dashboard on the latter); `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/actions.ts` (5 `"use server"` actions — `createUserAction` / `setRoleAction` / `grantToolAction` / `revokeToolAction` / `resetPasswordAction` — each builds a typed JSON body, calls `sysadminPostJson`, redirects with `?action_error=` on failure or `revalidatePath` + `?action_success=` on success); `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/page.tsx` (replaced placeholder; super_admin gate via `getMe()` at top, then 5 collapsed `<details>` cards with one `<form action={fn}>` each). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Confirm-password matching = inline `<script>` tag**, not a client component. React strips native `oninput` from server-rendered `<input>` elements, so the two cleanest paths were a tiny `"use client"` component or `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` of a one-liner. Chose the latter — keeps the page entirely server-rendered (zero client islands; build output confirms 161 B route size, identical to the placeholder), and the script is 12 lines of vanilla DOM calling `setCustomValidity('Passwords do not match')` on input → browser-native form blocking. (2) **Set-role location_code field always rendered** with helper text "Required only for location_admin role" — the action only includes `location_code` in the JSON body when role === "location_admin" AND non-empty (matches worker behavior). (3) **Tools as multi-checkbox** on Create user (`name="tools"` × 3); `formData.getAll("tools")` collects to string[], action omits the field when zero are checked. (4) **Empty role on Create user** → field omitted from body (worker treats as "auth user only — no user_permissions row"). **Empty role on Set role** → body sets `role: null` (worker's clear-role path; emits `clear_role` audit row). (5) **No pre-validation in actions** — worker is the single source of validation truth and surfaces errors via `?action_error=` banner. (6) **Action-error and action-success banners** at the top of the page (above cards), each with a "Dismiss" link to bare `/admin/sysadmin`. Mirrors damage detail's `ActionAlert` shape. (7) **`OperationCard`** uses pure-CSS open/close affordance via `group-open:` Tailwind utilities — no JS, no `useState`, browser-native `<details>` semantics. **Latent issues spotted in sysadmin-worker:** (a) `handleSetRole` accepts `location_code = null` for `role = location_admin` without erroring (apps/sysadmin-worker/src/index.ts:177-225) — produces a functionally-misconfigured row; **forward action**: add a worker-side guard. (b) `handleCreateUser` ignores `location_code` even when `role = location_admin` (line 289-291 inline says "Add when sysadmin UI ships in Step 7" — now); the brief's UI deliberately omits it, requiring two-step Create-then-Set-role flow. **Forward action**: single-PR follow-up to expose `location_code` on the create card. (c) No idempotency check on Create user — re-submit returns Supabase's 422 surfaced via the action-error banner; acceptable v1. (d) Create user race: auth.users insert succeeds then user_permissions/grant fails → orphaned auth user. Legacy parity. (e) `sysadmin_audit_log` writes are best-effort and swallowed (per worker leading comment line 27-29) — legacy parity. **Brief explicitly out-of-scope (deferred for separate briefs):** user search/list endpoint, audit-log viewer (worker exposes neither), bulk operations. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 4.287s (12 cached + apps/web ran fresh); `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — Next 15.5.15 compiled in 5.0s, 12/12 static pages generated, `/admin/sysadmin` route is `ƒ` at **161 B / 105 kB First Load JS** — identical bundle to the Brief 4 placeholder despite the new five-card UI (zero client JS added). All other route bundle sizes unchanged from Brief 11b snapshot. | Claude Code (Brief 7 session) |

Key decisions: (1) URL strategy = option (a) — kept `/admin/pricing/{loc}` as canonical and added a 308 redirect from `/admin/{slug}` to `/admin/pricing/{slug}` for any single-segment slug not in the known sub-path allow-list (`dashboard`, `damage`, `performance`, `pricing`, `sysadmin`, `api`, `login`, `logout`, `change-password`); reverting to legacy `/admin/{loc}` would re-create the legacy ambiguity where the slug is sometimes a tool name and sometimes a location. (2) All four redirects (`/admin/login` → `/login`, `/admin/change-password` → `/change-password`, `/admin/logout` → `/logout`, plus the dynamic location catch-all) live in `apps/web/middleware.ts` rather than `next.config.mjs` redirects() — keeps URL policy localized and the catch-all needs a programmatic exclusion list which is cleaner in code than a path-to-regexp negative lookahead. (3) `/logout` is a server route handler (`app/logout/route.ts`), GET + POST both clear the cookies (Max-Age=0 with the same `Path=/; HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Lax` attributes as `@splash/auth/buildLogoutCookies`) and 302 to `/login` — chosen over a client confirmation page for one-click sign-out. (4) The Sign Out button in the new Header POSTs directly to `dashboard-worker:/api/logout` per spec; the apps/web `/logout` route exists primarily as the redirect target for legacy `/admin/logout`. (5) Header is a server component with a small `SignOutButton` client subcomponent; wired into `/admin/*` via a per-route `app/admin/layout.tsx` rather than a usePathname-gated client wrapper in the root layout — App Router idiom, no extra client JS shipped to public pages. The earlier broken white-on-white logo block in the root `app/layout.tsx` was removed (it was non-functional against the default white body bg). User email/role display in the header is **deferred** — dashboard-worker has no `/api/me`-style endpoint and adding one is out of scope for this brief. Tracked as a new item 11a in the prioritized list. (6) Added a "Load-bearing constraints" section above "Conventions" enumerating the `/signup/{loc}`/`/q/{loc}`/`/join/{loc}` URL rule, the `pkg$` column rule, the `SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY` env-var name, and the `manually_flagged` immutability rule. Mirrored the URL rule with a warning comment in `apps/signup-worker/wrangler.toml` directly above the (commented) production-routes block. **Validation: per-file TypeScript transpile checks pass on all six new/edited files; full `pnpm typecheck` + `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` need to run on the operator's Windows machine — the Linux sandbox cannot resolve the workspace's Windows-style symlinked `node_modules` and `pnpm install` is on the don't-do list.** | Claude Code (Brief 2 session) |

## Known production state

(As reported by operator. Do not modify without explicit instruction.)

- **`info-signup-worker` (legacy)** — owns customer-facing routes on `splashcarwashes.info`: `/api/*`, `/admin/*`, `/signup/*`, `/q/*`, `/join/*`. This is the only worker carrying real customer traffic today.
- **`splash-dashboard` (new)** — bound to `splashcarwashes.info/`, `/login`, `/logout` in Cloudflare. Returns 404s today because the new dashboard-worker code only handles `/api/login`, `/api/logout`, `/api/forced-reset` — apps/web is the intended owner of `/` and `/login`. Note: this binding is in Cloudflare's production state but is **not** reflected in the repo's [apps/dashboard-worker/wrangler.toml](apps/dashboard-worker/wrangler.toml), where `routes` is still commented.
- **`splash-signup-next`, `splash-performance`, `splash-sysadmin`, `splash-damage`** — workers.dev only, no production routes bound.
- **`splash-web`** — workers.dev only, no production routes bound.

## Staging

`staging.splashcarwashes.info` is a same-origin rehearsal environment
for end-to-end testing of the full apps/web + 5-worker stack. Brief 16
landed the wrangler.toml route bindings for staging across all 6
deployable artifacts (5 workers + apps/web); deploys + DNS are
operator-side action items per `STAGING_DEPLOY.md` at repo root.

- **Same-origin posture:** all 6 components share
  `staging.splashcarwashes.info`, so `sb-access-token` cookies set by
  dashboard-worker reach apps/web natively under SameSite=Lax. CSRF
  gates pass without carve-outs. Mirrors production behavior exactly.
- **Same-data posture (operator decision):** staging uses the same
  Supabase, R2, and D1 backends as production. No staging-isolation
  infrastructure. Operator uses identifiable test data and cleans up
  at their discretion. No separate staging secrets — every worker
  carries the same secret inventory whether served at workers.dev or
  staging.
- **Production routes untouched.** Staging is purely additive: new
  `routes = [...]` blocks coexist with the still-commented production
  blocks in every wrangler.toml. The legacy `info-signup-worker`
  continues to own production traffic on `splashcarwashes.info`
  regardless of staging state.
- **Rollback path:** comment out the staging `routes = [...]` blocks
  and re-deploy. CF removes the route bindings on the next deploy that
  omits them. Production state is unaffected.
- **Per-worker route patterns** are mirrored in the Deployed
  components table above. apps/web carries a catch-all
  (`staging.splashcarwashes.info/*`) and CF's most-specific-match-wins
  routing automatically routes per-worker patterns ahead of it.

## How this project is run

The work is split between two Claude surfaces:

- **Cowork** (planner) drafts briefs into `BRIEFS/`, maintains
  `BUILD_STATE.md`, and reviews outcomes after Claude Code executes.
- **Claude Code** (doer, runs in VS Code) reads `CLAUDE.md` +
  `BUILD_STATE.md` + a brief, makes the file edits, runs typecheck +
  build, fills in the brief's Outcome section, updates
  `BUILD_STATE.md`. Operates in headless mode via `claude --print`.

**Bridge:** `scripts/orchestrator.ps1` — a polling daemon the operator
launches once per work session. It watches `BRIEFS/QUEUE.md` and invokes
Claude Code headlessly when a brief is queued. On success the entry
moves to `BRIEFS/DONE.md`; on failure it moves to `BRIEFS/FAILED.md` and
the daemon halts. See `BRIEFS/README.md` for the full workflow + file
conventions, and `CLAUDE.md` at the repo root for the project rules
Claude Code auto-loads.

To start a work session:

```powershell
powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts/orchestrator.ps1
```

Stop with Ctrl+C. The daemon writes to `BRIEFS/orchestrator.log`.

(Use `pwsh` instead of `powershell` if you have PowerShell 7+ installed.
The operator's machine has only Windows PowerShell 5.1 by default, hence
`powershell` in the documented invocation.)

For one-off / manual brief execution without the daemon, open Claude
Code in the repo and paste:

```
Read CLAUDE.md, BUILD_STATE.md, and BRIEFS/<brief-filename>. Execute
the brief end-to-end. Fill in the Outcome section and update BUILD_STATE.md.
```

## Load-bearing constraints

These constraints are project-level. Treat as immutable; any brief that proposes touching them must be rejected without explicit operator override.

- **`/signup/{location}`, `/q/{location}`, `/join/{location}` URLs MUST NEVER change.** They are saved on hundreds of customer/admin device home screens as per-location bookmarks. Renaming them silently breaks the bookmark UX and is irreversible until devices are re-bookmarked manually. The signup-worker's `wrangler.toml` carries a warning comment immediately above the (commented) production-routes block; preserve it through any wrangler edits.
- **`pkg$` column name** in `pricing_simple`. See "Findings & decisions log" — kept as a quoted identifier; bracket-notation accesses (`row["pkg$"]`) are not to be normalized.
- **`SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY` env-var name.** Legacy used `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`; the monorepo standardized on `SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY`. Code reads the new name across all five workers. A binding set under the legacy name in Cloudflare results in a 401 on every authenticated read.
- **`manually_flagged` rows in `suspicious_phones`** are immutable from worker code. Auto-detection skips writes when the row has `manually_flagged = true`. Admin-curated deny entries (`'0000000000'`, etc.) stay untouched.
- **CF same-zone Worker-to-Worker subrequests must use service bindings, not URL fetches.** When apps/web (running on splash-web at staging/prod) does a URL-based `fetch()` to a sibling worker on the same CF zone, the request loops through the edge inefficiently and 522s after ~19s. apps/web SSR helpers (under `apps/web/app/**/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` and `_lib/me.ts`) call sibling workers via `env.<BINDING>.fetch(req)` instead, with a URL-based fallback for dev. The 5 service bindings are declared in `apps/web/wrangler.toml` (DASHBOARD_WORKER, SIGNUP_WORKER, PERFORMANCE_WORKER, SYSADMIN_WORKER, DAMAGE_WORKER) and merged into the global `CloudflareEnv` interface in `apps/web/cloudflare-env.d.ts`. Adding new server-side worker calls without the binding pattern reintroduces the 522. This is a CF runtime limitation; not negotiable.

## Conventions

For Claude Code sessions:

- **Read this file first** before starting any task on this repo.
- **Update "Last updated"** + add an entry to "Findings & decisions log" at task completion. Use absolute dates (YYYY-MM-DD).
- **Don't modify "Known production state"** without explicit instruction from the operator.
- **If a brief's work invalidates this file's content** (e.g., a deploy happens, a route gets bound, an item changes status), flag it explicitly in your task report so this file gets updated alongside the change.
- **Treat the prioritized work list as authoritative** — derived from the audit + the work the operator has called out. Don't reorder or add briefs without surfacing it.
